Differential Calculus of Hochschild Pairs for Infinity-Categories This paper is a contribution to the Special Issue on Primitive Forms and Related Topics in honor of Kyoji Saito for his 77th birthday. The full collection is available at https://www.emis.de/journals/SIGMA/Saito.html
Isamu IWANARI

Abstract
In this paper, we provide a conceptual new construction of the algebraic structure on the pair of the Hochschild cohomology spectrum (cochain complex) and Hochschild homology spectrum, which is analogous to the structure of calculus on a manifold. This algebraic structure is encoded by a two-colored operad introduced by Kontsevich and Soibelman. We prove that for a stable idempotent-complete infinity-category, the pair of its Hochschild cohomology and homology spectra naturally admits the structure of algebra over the operad. Moreover, we prove a generalization to the equivariant context.



keywords
Hochschild cohomology; Hochschild homology; operad;
\infty
-category

AMS
16E40; 18N60; 18M60

To Kyoji Saito on his 77th birthday

1
Introduction

Let
M
be a smooth real manifold.
Let
T_{M}^{\bullet}=\bigoplus_{p\geq 0}\wedge^{p}T_{M}

and
\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}=\bigoplus_{q\geq 0}\Omega_{M}^{q}

denote the graded vector space of multivector fields
and differential forms on
M
, respectively.
By convention,
T_{M}^{p}=\wedge^{p}T_{M}
has homological degree
-p

while
\Omega^{q}_{M}
has homological degree
q
:
we adopt the reverse grading.
There are several algebraic structures on the pair
(T_{M}^{\bullet},\Omega_{M}^{\bullet})
.
The graded vector space
T_{M}^{\bullet}
has a graded commutative
(associative) product given by
\wedge
. Further,
the shifted graded vector space
T_{M}^{\bullet+1}
inherits the structure of a graded Lie algebra defined by the
Schouten–Nijenhuis bracket
[-,-]
.
On the other hand,
\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}
has the
de Rham differential
{\rm d}_{\rm DR}

(we do not consider the obvious graded commutative algebra structure on
\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}
because it is irrelevant to the noncommutative context).
Since
\Omega_{M}^{q}
is the dual vector space of
T^{q}_{M}
, the
contraction morphisms
T^{p}_{M}\otimes\Omega_{M}^{q}\to\Omega_{M}^{q-p}

give rise to a
(T_{M}^{\bullet},\wedge)
-module structure on
\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}
:

\displaystyle i\colon\ (T_{M}^{\bullet},\wedge)\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}_{M}\to\Omega_{M}^{\bullet},

where we regard
(T_{M}^{\bullet},\wedge)
as the graded algebra determined by
\wedge
.
The Lie derivative on
M
defines a Lie algebra action of
\big(T_{M}^{\bullet+1},[-,-]\big)

on
\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}
:

\displaystyle l\colon\ \big(T_{M}^{\bullet+1},[-,-]\big)\otimes\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}\to\Omega_{M}^{\bullet}.

The tuple

(\wedge,[-,-],{\rm d}_{\rm DR},i,l)

constitutes fundamental calculus operations on the manifold
M
.
These operations are subject under certain relations such as

{\rm d}_{\rm DR}^{2}=0
, the
Cartan homotopy/magic formula, the compatibility between the Lie algebra
action
l
and the de Rham differential

{\rm d}_{\rm DR}
, and so on. If
X
is a smooth algebraic variety over a field
of characteristic zero,
the pair
(\mathcal{T}_{X}^{\bullet},\Omega_{X}^{\bullet})
of sheaves
of multivector fields (given by the
exterior products of the tangent sheaf) and differential forms admit such an algebraic
structure of calculus.

Let us shift our interest to noncommutative algebraic geometry
in which stable
\infty
-categories, (pretriangulated)
differential graded (dg) categories or the likes play the roles of fundamental geometric objects.
From the Hochschild–Kostant–Rosenberg theorem,
an analogue of
(\mathcal{T}_{X}^{\bullet},\Omega_{X}^{\bullet})

is the Hochschild pair, that is,
the pair of Hochschild cohomology (cochain complex) and Hochschild homology (chain complex).
In
[
23
, Section 11.2]
,
Kontsevich and Soibelman introduced a two-colored topological operad which we shall refer to as the Kontsevich–Soibelman operad and denote here by
\mathbf{KS}
.
It can be used to encode all the structures on
the Hochschild pair, which
are analogous to the structure of calculus.
The operad
\mathbf{KS}
generalizes the little
2
-disks (cubes) operad, i.e., the
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-operad.
It contains two colors,
D
and
C_{M}
, such that
the full suboperad spanned by the color
D
is the
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-operad.
In
[
11
]
, it was shown that a combinatorial dg operad
\mathcal{KS}_{{\rm comb}}
,
which is a certain dg version of
\mathbf{KS}
, acts
on the pair of the Hochschild cochain complex and the Hochschild chain complex
of an associative algebra (
A_{\infty}
-sense),
and in
[
16
]
an algebraic structure on the Hochschild pair over
\mathbf{KS}

was constructed for a ring spectrum by means of the Swiss-cheese operad conjecture
[
34
]
.

In this paper, we provide a conceptual new construction of the structure
of an algebra over
\mathbf{KS}
that yields the following result (see Theorem
7.14
):

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
R
-linear stable
indempotent-complete
\infty
-category.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
be the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module spectrum and

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
the Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum.
Then the pair
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
is promoted to
an algebra over
\mathbf{KS}
, namely, it is
a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra in the
\infty
-category of
R
-module spectra
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.

The structure of the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra on

(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
in Theorem
1.1
induces
the action morphism
u\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
,
which is a counterpart to
i
in the aforementioned tuple.
In the classical differential graded algebraic situation,
we further prove that
u
can be described by means of well-known algebraic constructions.
Thus,
u
can be considered
a noncommutative contraction morphism, see Section
8
, Theorem
8.2
and Proposition
8.8
.
In other words, the underlying morphism given by actions is an expected one.
By considering Cartan’s homotopy formula built in
\mathbf{KS}
,
an analogue
L\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})[1]\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
of the Lie derivative map
l
is also an expected morphism, cf. Remark
8.10
.

In Section
2
,
we briefly describe the idea and approach of our
construction, which is based on a simple observation.
To achieve this,
we prove the following (see Corollary
4.21
for details):

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
such that it admits small colimits and the tensor product functor
\otimes\colon\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}

preserves small colimits separately in each variable.

(
The typical examples of
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
we should keep in mind are the
\infty
-category of spectra, and the
derived
\infty
-category of vector spaces.
)

Let
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1}\!,\mathcal{M}\big)\!
be the functor category from the classifying space

BS^{1}
of the circle
S^{1}
to
\mathcal{M}
, which inherits a pointwise symmetric monoidal structure from the structure on
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
. Namely,
an object of
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
can be viewed as an object
M
of
\mathcal{M}

equipped with an
S^{1}
-action.
Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})
be the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras
in
\mathcal{M}
.
Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})
be the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebras

(
i.e., algebras over the little
2
-disks operad
)
in
\mathcal{M}
.
Similarly,
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

denotes the
\infty
-category of associative algebras
(
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-algebras
)

in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
.
We denote by
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)
the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,M)
such that
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

and
M
is a left
A
-module object in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
.

Then there exists a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}))}\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big).

See Section
4
for the definition of the fiber product on the right-hand side.

This result means that
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})
,
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

and
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)
form building blocks for
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras. This allows us to describe the structure of a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
as a collection of more elementary algebraic data involving associative algebras, left modules, circle actions, and
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebras.
As for
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebras, thanks to Dunn additivity theorem
for
\infty
-operads proved by Lurie
[
25
]
, a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\mathcal{M}))
exists.
While we make use of Theorem
1.2
in the construction process, it would be generally useful in the theory of
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras since
the notion of
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras is complicated as is.
For example, when
\mathcal{M}
is the derived
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}(k)
of vector spaces over a field
k
of characteristic zero,
i.e., in the differential graded context, there is a quite elementary
interpretation.
One may take
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{D}(k)\big)
to be the
\infty
-category obtained from the category of mixed complexes in the sense of Kassel (see, e.g.,
[
24
]
)
by localizing quasi-isomorphisms.
Therefore, an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{D}(k)\big)\big)
may be regarded
as an associative algebra in the monoidal (
\infty
-)category of mixed complexes.
Objects of
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{D}(k)\big)\big)
can be described in a similar way.
Moreover, dg
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-operad is
formal in characteristic zero.

As we will describe in the following section,
our method consists of only natural procedures.
In particular, by contrast with previous work, it does not
involve/use complicated resolutions
of operads or genuine chain complexes.
Thus, we hope that our proposed approach can be applicable
to other settings and generalizations
such as
(\infty,n)
-categories.
Indeed, the method allows us to prove
an equivariant generalization of Theorem
1.1

(see Theorem
9.1
):

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
G
be a group object in the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{S}
of spaces,
that is, a
group-like

\mathbf{E}_{1}
-space
.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
R
-linear stable
idempotent-complete
\infty
-category.
Suppose that
G
acts on
\mathcal{C}

(
namely, it gives a left action
)
.
Then
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
is promoted to
a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
in
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Namely,
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
is a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra in

\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
, which comes equipped with a left action of
G
.

We would like to invite the reader’s attention to the noteworthy features of our method:

•

Our construction of the structure of an algebra
on the Hochschild pair over
\mathbf{KS}

starts with an
R
-linear stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
.
Consequently,
if we have an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
we have a canonical equivalence
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))\simeq(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}))
as algebras over
\mathbf{KS}
(see Remark
9.4
).
Consider the situation that the associative algebra
A
and
A^{\prime}

in the
\infty
-category of
R
-modules have the equivalent module
category
\operatorname{LMod}_{A}
and
\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{\prime}}
, that is,
A
and
A^{\prime}

are (derived) Morita equivalent to one another.
Here
\operatorname{LMod}_{A}
and
\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{\prime}}
denote the
\infty
-categories
of left
A
-module spectra and left
A^{\prime}
-module spectra, respectively
(cf. Section
3
).
Then
\operatorname{LMod}_{A}\simeq\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{\prime}}
induces the canonical equivalence
of Hochschild pairs as algebras over
\mathbf{KS}
.
In other words,
our method provides a natural Morita invariant structure.
This invariance has a fundamental importance in
noncommutative geometry and is reasonable to expect,
whereas the Morita invariant property
of algebra
structures in
[
11
]
and
[
16
]
remains an open problem.
We would like to mention a recent work
[
1
]

in which the authors prove the Morita invariance of the calculus structure at the level of graded vector spaces of homology.

•

The base ring can be any commutative ring spectrum.
Note that it is not possible to use
the dg operad
\mathcal{KS}_{{\rm comb}}
in the generalization
to the “spectral” setting.

•

As stated Theorem
1.3
, our functorial method allows us to generalize
to equivariant situations. The
\infty
-categories with group actions
naturally appear in future applications (see below).

•

As revealed the outline in Section
2
,
our construction works well not with algebras but with
\infty
-categories.
Thus, even when one is ultimately interested in algebras,
it is important to consider the
\infty
-category of their modules.

We would like to view our results from the perspective
of noncommutative algebraic geometry.
As mentioned above, the notion of
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra structures
is a counterpart to the calculus on manifolds.
Thus,
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras are central objects in “noncommutative calculus”.
We refer the reader to
[
12
]
and references therein
for this point of view.

Recall the algebro-geometric interpretations of the Hochschild cohomology

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
and Hochschild homology
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
for stable
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}

or dg categories (somewhat more precisely, we assume that they are “linear” over a field of characteristic zero).
The
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
governs the deformations theory of the stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
in the derived geometric formulation.
The Hochschild homology
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

(more precisely, the Hochschild chain complex)
inherits an
S^{1}
-action that corresponds to the Connes operator.
Then
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
with
S^{1}
-action gives rise to an analogue of the Hodge
filtration: the pair of the negative cyclic homology and the periodic
cyclic homology can be thought of as such a structure.
(These algebraic structures are contained in the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
.)
As revealed in
[
20
]
in the case of associative (dg) algebras
A
,
the action of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
on
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
encoded by the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
structure at the operadic level
is a key algebraic datum that describes
variations of the (analogue of) Hodge filtration along
noncommutative (curved) deformations. Namely,
the period map for noncommutative deformations (of an associative
algebra) is controlled by the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra of the Hochschild cohomology and Hochschild homology.
Therefore, the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
will provide a crucial algebraic input for the theory of period maps for deformations of the
stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
.
The significance of the generalization to the equivariant context
(Theorem
1.3
)
will be seen when one comes to consider fruitful examples.
The motivations
partly come from mirror symmetry.
For example, stable
\infty
-categories endowed with
S^{1}
-actions
or some algebraic actions, that
are interesting from the viewpoint of
S^{1}
-equivariant deformation theory,
naturally appear from Landau–Ginzburg models in the context
of matrix factorizations. Its equivariant deformations together with the
associated Hodge structure should provide
a categorification of the theory of Landau–Ginzburg models.
As a second example, if
X
is a sufficiently nice algebraic stack (more generally, a derived stack),
one can consider the derived free loop space

LX=\operatorname{Map}\big(S^{1},X\big)
of
X
(see, e.g.,
[
5
]
).
The stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Perf}(LX)
of perfect complexes on
LX
comes equipped
with the natural
S^{1}
-action.
Finally, we would also like to mention that main results in this paper form the basis for
our recent work
[
19
]
.

2
Strategy and organization

The purpose of this section is to outline the strategy of a construction of a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra structure on the pair of Hochschild cohomology
and Hochschild homology and to give the brief organization of this paper.
This section is something like the second part of introduction.
We hope that the following outline will be helpful in understanding
the content of the sequel. However, this section is independent with the
rest of this paper so that the reader can skip it.

2.1.
We will give an outline of the construction.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small stable
\infty
-category.
If
\mathcal{C}
is not idempotent-complete,
we replace
\mathcal{C}
by its idempotent-completion: we assume that
\mathcal{C}
is idempotent-complete.
While we work with stable
\infty
-categories over a commutative
ring spectrum
R
in the paper, for simplicity
we here work with plain stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-categories (equivalently, we assume that
R
is the sphere
spectrum). We let
\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
denote the Ind-category
that is a compactly generated stable
\infty
-category.
The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
is also equivalent to
the functor category
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{ex}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}\big)

of exact functors, where
\operatorname{Sp}
is the stable
\infty
-category
of spectra.

Let
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
be the functor category from
\mathcal{D}

to itself that
consists of those functors which preserve small colimits.
This functor category is a compactly generated stable
\infty
-category
and
inherits an associative monoidal structure
given by the composition of functors.
We denote by
\mathcal{E}\textup{nd}(\mathcal{D})^{\otimes}
the
presentable stable
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})

endowed with the (associative) monoidal structure.

Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})
denote the
\infty
-category of associative ring spectra.
Given
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})
, we define
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
to be
the
\infty
-category of right
A
-module spectra.
Let us regard
A
as a right
A
-module in an obvious way.
Then there is an essentially unique colimit-preserving functor
p_{A}\colon\operatorname{Sp}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
which sends the sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}
in
\operatorname{Sp}
to
A
.
Let
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
be the
\infty
-category of presentable stable
\infty
-categories
in which morphisms are colimit-preserving functors.
The assignment
A\mapsto\{p_{A}\colon\operatorname{Sp}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\}

induces
I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})\to\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}
.
The right adjoint
E\colon\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})

of
I
carries
p\colon\operatorname{Sp}\to\mathcal{P}
in
\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}

to the endomorphism (associative) algebra
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{P}}(P)
,
where
P
is the image
p(\mathbb{S})
.
Note that
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})
has a natural symmetric monoidal
structure whose tensor product is induced by the
tensor product
A\otimes B
in
\operatorname{Sp}
.
The
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
also admits an appropriate symmetric monoidal
structure in which
\operatorname{Sp}
is a unit object,
and
I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})\to\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}

can be promoted to a symmetric monoidal functor.
Applying
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(-)
to

I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})\leftrightarrows\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}\,{\colon}\!E
, we obtain

\displaystyle I\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp}))\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)_{\operatorname{Sp}/}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)\ {\colon}E,

where
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})

is the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebras, and

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp}))

and the left equivalence
follows from Dunn additivity theorem. Here we abuse notation by writing
I
and
E
for the induced functors.
Let us regard
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}\big)
as the
\infty
-category
of monoidal presentable stable
\infty
-categories.
The left adjoint sends an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra
A
to
the associative monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}^{\otimes}
.
The right adjoint carries a monoidal presentable stable
\infty
-category

\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
to the endomorphism spectrum
\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathsf{1}_{\mathcal{M}})
of the unit object
\mathsf{1}_{\mathcal{M}}
, endowed with an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra structure.

We define Hochschild cohomology spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\!=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})

as
E\big(\mathcal{E}\textup{nd}(\mathcal{D})^{\otimes}\big)\!\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})
.
The underlying associative algebra

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is the endomorphism algebra of the identity functor

\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
.

Consider the counit map of the adjunction:

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\textup{nd}(\mathcal{D})^{\otimes},

which is a monoidal functor.
Since
\mathcal{E}\textup{nd}(\mathcal{D})^{\otimes}
naturally acts on
\mathcal{D}
,
it gives rise to an action of
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}
on

\mathcal{D}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}\textup{nd}(\mathcal{D})^{\otimes}\curvearrowright\mathcal{D}\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ \operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}\curvearrowright\mathcal{D}.

In other words,
\mathcal{D}
is a left
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}
-module
object in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
.
Let
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}\subset\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}
be the monoidal full subcategory that consists of compact objects.
By the restrictions,
it gives rise to a left
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}
-module object
\mathcal{C}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}\curvearrowright\mathcal{C},

in the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
of small stable idempotent-complete
\infty
-categories in which morphisms are exact functors (
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
also admits
a suitable symmetric monoidal structure).
Informally, we think of it as a categorical associative action of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
on
\mathcal{C}
.
This is induced by the adjunction so that it has an evident universal property.

Construct a functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}\to\operatorname{Sp}
which carries

\mathcal{C}
to the Hochschild homology spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
.
In the classical differential graded context,
Hochschild chain complex comes equipped with the Connes operator.
In our general setting, it is natural to encode such structures
by means of circle actions: Hochschild homology spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

is promoted to a spectrum with an
S^{1}
-action
, that is, an object of
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)
. Thus we configure the assignment

\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
as a symmetric monoidal functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(-)\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}^{\otimes}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)^{\otimes},

where
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)
inherits a pointwise symmetric monoidal
structure from the structure on
\operatorname{Sp}
.

Applying the symmetric monoidal functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(-)
to
the left
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}
-module object
\mathcal{C}
,
we obtain a left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\big(\!\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\!\big)
-module
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\!BS^{1}\!,\operatorname{Sp}\!\big)
. Note that
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))

\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\big(\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\big)

is an associative algebra object in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)
. In other words,
it is an associative ring spectrum equipped with an
S^{1}
-action.

There is a topological operad (
\infty
-operad)
\mathbf{Cyl}
(defined in a geometric way). We have a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)\big)
between
\infty
-categories of algebras,
where
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})
is the
\infty
-category of algebras over
\mathbf{Cyl}
.
There is another two-colored topological operad (
\infty
-operad)

\mathbf{DCyl}
such that operads
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}
and
\mathbf{Cyl}
are contained
in
\mathbf{DCyl}
as full suboperads:
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\subset\mathbf{DCyl}\supset\mathbf{Cyl}
.
Let
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{DCyl}

denote the inclusion, and let
i_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})

be the left adjoint of the forgetful functor
i^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})
.
Consider the sequence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Sp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{!}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{forget}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Sp})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)\big)

and denote by
i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))_{C}
the image of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

under the composite.
We construct a canonical equivalence
i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))_{C}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))

in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)\big)
.

Assembling the constructions, we obtain a triple

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))_{C}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})),

where
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is the
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}
-algebra,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

is the left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
-module (in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}\big)
).
As mentioned in Theorem
1.2
, we prove that the triple exactly amounts to a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra

(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
, that is, the structure of an algebra over
the Kontsevich–Soibelman operad
\mathbf{KS}
on the pair
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
.
This completes the construction.

2.2.
This paper is organized as follows:
Section
3
collects conventions and some of the notation that we will use.
In Section
4
, we discuss algebras over the Kontsevich–Soibelman
operad. The main result of Section
4
is Corollary
4.21

(= Theorem
1.2
).
Along the way,
we introduce several topological colored operads (
\infty
-operads).
In Section
5
, we give a brief review of Hochschild cohomology
spectra that we will use.
In Section
6
, we give a construction of the assignment

\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
which satisfies the requirements for our goal
(partly
because we are not able to find a suitable construction in the literature).
The results of this section will be quite useful for various purposes other than the subject of this
paper.
In Section
7
, we prove Theorem
7.14
(= Theorem
1.1
).
Namely, we construct a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
.
In Section
8
, we study the action morphisms
determined by the structure of the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra on
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
.
In Section
9
, we give a generalization to
an equivariant setting (cf. Theorem
1.3
):
\mathcal{C}
is endowed with the action of a group (a group
object in the
\infty
-category of spaces).

3
Notation and convention

Throughout this paper we use the theory of
quasi-categories
.
We assume that the reader is familiar with this theory
and operads.
A quasi-category is a simplicial set which
satisfies the weak Kan condition of Boardman–Vogt.
The theory of quasi-categories from the viewpoint of models of
(\infty,1)
-categories
were extensively developed by Joyal and Lurie
[
21
,
25
,
27
]
.
Following
[
27
]
, we shall refer to quasi-categories
as
\infty
-categories
.
Our main references are
[
25
]
and
[
27
]
.
Given an ordinary category
\mathcal{C}
, by passing to the nerve
\operatorname{N}(\mathcal{C})
,
we think of
\mathcal{C}
as the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{N}(\mathcal{C})
.
We usually abuse notation by writing
\mathcal{C}
for
\operatorname{N}(\mathcal{C})

even when
\mathcal{C}
should be thought of as a simplicial set
or an
\infty
-category.

We use the theory of
\infty
-operads
which is
thoroughly developed in
[
25
]
.
The notion of
\infty
-operads gives
one of the models of colored operads.
Thanks to Hinich
[
15
]
, there is a comparison between
algebras over differential graded operads
and algebras over
\infty
-operads in values in chain complexes.
In particular, in characteristic zero,
[
15
]
establishes
an equivalence between two notions of algebras, see
loc. cit
.

Here is a list of some of the conventions and notation that we will use:

•

\mathbf{Z}
: the ring of integers,
\mathbf{R}
denotes the set of real numbers which we regard as either a topological space or a ring.

•

\Delta
: the category of linearly ordered non-empty
finite sets (consisting of [0], [1], …,
[n]=\{0,\dots,n\}
, …).

•

\Delta^{n}
: the standard
n
-simplex.

•

N
: the simplicial nerve functor (cf.
[
27
, Section 1.1.5]
).

•

\mathcal{S}
:
\infty
-category of small spaces. We denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}

the
\infty
-category of large spaces (cf.
[
27
, Section 1.2.16]
).

•

\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
: the largest Kan subcomplex of an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
.

•

\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}
: the opposite
\infty
-category of an
\infty
-category. We also use the superscript “op” to indicate the opposite category for ordinary categories and enriched categories.

•

\operatorname{Cat}_{\infty}
: the
\infty
-category of small
\infty
-categories.

•

\operatorname{Sp}
: the stable
\infty
-category of spectra.

•

\operatorname{Fun}(A,B)
: the function complex for simplicial sets
A
and
B
.

•

\operatorname{Fun}_{C}(A,B)
: the simplicial subset of
\operatorname{Fun}(A,B)
classifying
maps which are compatible with
given projections
A\to C
and
B\to C
.

•

\operatorname{Map}(A,B)
: the largest Kan subcomplex of
\operatorname{Fun}(A,B)
when
A
and
B
are
\infty
-categories.

•

\operatorname{Map}_{\mathcal{C}}(C,C^{\prime})
: the mapping space from an object
C\in\mathcal{C}
to
C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is an
\infty
-category.
We usually view it as an object in
\mathcal{S}
(cf.
[
27
, Section 1.2.2]
).

•

\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
: the category of pointed finite
sets
\langle 0\rangle,\langle 1\rangle,\dots\langle n\rangle,\dots
,
where
\langle n,\rangle=\{*,1,\dots,n\}
with the base point
*
.
We write
\Gamma
for
\operatorname{N}(\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast})
.
\langle n\rangle^{\circ}=\langle n\rangle\backslash*
.
Notice that the (nerve of) Segal’s gamma category is the opposite category
of our
\Gamma
.

•

\mathcal{P}^{\textup{act}}
: If
\mathcal{P}
is an
\infty
-operad,
we write
\mathcal{P}^{\textup{act}}
for the subcategory
of
\mathcal{P}
spanned by active morphisms.

•

\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}
: the trivial
\infty
-operad
[
25
, Example 2.1.1.20]
.

•

\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
: the associative operad
[
25
, Section 4.1.1]
, we use
the notation slightly different from
loc. cit.

Informally,
an
\operatorname{As}
-algebra (an algebra over
\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
)
is an unital associative algebra.
For a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
,
we write
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\mathcal{C})
for the
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{As}
-algebra objects.
We refer to an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\mathcal{C})

as an associative algebra object in
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
.
We refer to a monoidal
\infty
-category over
\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
as an associative monoidal
\infty
-category.

•

\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}
: the
\infty
-operad defined in
[
25
, Definition 4.2.1.7]
.
An algebra over
\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}
is a pair
(A,M)

such that an unital associative algebra
A

and a left
A
-module
M
.
For a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
,
we write
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
or
\operatorname{LMod}(\mathcal{C})

for
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
.

•

\mathbf{E}^{\otimes}_{n}
: the
\infty
-operad of little
n
-cubes.
For a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
,
we write
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{n}}(\mathcal{C})
for
the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{E}_{n}
-algebra objects.

4
Operads

4.1.
We will define several simplicial colored operads which are relevant to us.
By a simplicial colored operad, we mean a colored operad in the
symmetric monoidal category of simplicial sets.
A simplicial colored operad is also referred to as
a symmetric multicategory enriched over the
category of simplicial sets.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(0,1)
denote the open interval
\{x\in\mathbf{R}\,|\,0<x<1\}
.
For
n\geq 0
,
let
(0,1)^{n}
be the
n
-fold product, i.e., the
n
-dimensional cube.
An open embedding
f\colon(0,1)^{n}\to(0,1)^{n}
is said to be
rectilinear if it is given by

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=(a_{1}x_{1}+b_{1},\dots,a_{n}x_{n}+b_{n})

for some real constants
0<a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\leq 1,\ 0\leq b_{1},\dots,b_{n}<1
, provided that
the formula defines an embedding.
An embedding
f\colon(0,1)^{n}\to(0,1)^{n}
is said to be
shrinking if it is given by
f(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=(a_{1}x_{1},\dots,a_{n}x_{n})

for some
0<a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\leq 1
.

Let
S^{1}
denote the circle
\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
which we regard as a topological space.
A continuous map
f\colon(0,1)^{n}\times S^{1}\to(0,1)^{n}\times S^{1}

is said to be rectilinear (resp. shrinking) if
f=(\phi,\psi)

such that
\phi\colon(0,1)^{n}\to(0,1)^{n}
is rectilinear (resp. shrinking)
and
\psi\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
is given by a rotation

\displaystyle S^{1}=\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}\ni x\mapsto x+r\in\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}=S^{1}

with
r\in\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
. In particular, when
n=0
,

f\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
is rectilinear if it is given by a rotation.

Let
n\geq 1
. If a continuous map
f\colon(0,1)^{n}\to(0,1)^{n-1}\times S^{1}
is
said to be rectilinear if it factors as
(0,1)^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}(0,1)^{n-1}\times\mathbf{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}(0,1)^{n-1}\times\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
,
where
h
is the projection, and

g
is given by
f(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=(a_{1}x_{1}+b_{1},\dots,a_{n}x_{n}+b_{n})

for some real constants
0<a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\leq 1
,
0\leq b_{1},\dots,b_{n-1}<1
,
b_{n}\in\mathbf{R}
, provided
that
the formula defines an open embedding.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\operatorname{Cyl}
be a simplicial colored operad defined as follows:

(i)

The set of colors of
\operatorname{Cyl}
has a single element,
which we will denote by
C
.

(ii)

Let
I=\langle r\rangle^{\circ}
be a finite set and let

\{C\}_{I}
be a set of colors indexed by
I
.
By abuse of notation, we write
C^{\sqcup r}

for
\{C\}_{I}
, where
r
is the number of elements of
I
.
We remark that
C^{\sqcup r}
does not mean the coproduct.
We define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{Cyl}}(\{C\}_{I},C)=\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{Cyl}}\big(C^{\sqcup r},C\big)
to be the singular simplicial complex of the space

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup r},(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)

of embeddings
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup r}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

such that the restriction to each component

(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
is rectilinear.
Here
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup r}

is the disjoint union of
(0,1)\times S^{1}
, whose set of connected
components is identified with

I
. The space
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup r},(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)
is endowed with the standard topology, that is,
the subspace of the mapping space
with compact-open topology.

(iii)

The composition law in
\operatorname{Cyl}
is given by the composition of rectilinear
embeddings, and a unit map is the identity map.

The color
C
together with
\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{Cyl}}(\{C\}_{I},C)

constitutes a fibrant simplicial colored operad.
By a fibrant simplicial colored operad we mean that
every simplicial set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{Cyl}}(\{C\}_{I},C)
is a Kan complex.
Note that the singular simplicial complex of a topological space is a Kan complex.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}
be a simplicial colored operad defined as follows:

(i)

The set of colors of
\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}
has two elements denoted by
C
and
C_{M}
.

(ii)

Let
I=\langle r\rangle^{\circ}
be a finite set and let

\{C,C_{M}\}_{I}
be a set of colors indexed by
I
,
which we think of as a map
p\colon I\to\{C,C_{M}\}
.
We also write
C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n}
for

\{C,C_{M}\}_{I}
when
p^{-1}(C)
(resp.
p^{-1}(C_{M})
)
has
m
elements
(resp.
n
elements).
Let

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n},(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)

denote the space of embeddings
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

such that the restriction to each component
is rectilinear (the topology is induced by compact-open topology).
We refer to it as the space of rectilinear embeddings.
For
n\geq 1
, let

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}^{t}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C_{M}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}:={\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C)\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C_{M}),(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)}

be its subspace that consists of those rectilinear embeddings
f

such that each restriction to any component in

\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C_{M})
is (not only rectilinear but also)
shrinking.
Here
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C)\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C_{M})
denotes the finite disjoint union
of
(0,1)\times S^{1}
indexed by
p^{-1}(C)\sqcup p^{-1}(C_{M})\simeq I
,
but we distinguish between
components indexed by
p^{-1}(C)
and those indexed in
p^{-1}(C_{M})

since they play different roles. Notice that
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}^{t}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C_{M}\big)

is the empty space for
n\geq 2
.
We define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C_{M}\big)
to be the singular
simplicial complex of
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}^{t}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C_{M}\big)
.
When
n=0
, we define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}},C_{M}\big)
to be the empty simplicial set.

(iii)

We set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}(C^{\sqcup{m}},C)=\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{Cyl}}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}},C\big)
.
If
n\neq 0
,
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C\big)
is the empty set.

(iv)

The composition law is given by the composition of rectilinear embeddings, and a unit map is the identity map.

The colors
C
,
C_{M}
together with simplicial sets of maps constitute a fibrant simplicial colored operad.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\operatorname{DCyl}
be a simplicial colored operad defined as follows:

(i)

The set of colors of
\operatorname{DCyl}
has two elements, which we denoted by
D

and
C
.

(ii)

Let
I=\langle r\rangle^{\circ}
be a finite set and let

\{D,C\}_{I}
be a set of colors indexed by
I
,
that is, a map
p\colon I\to\{D,C\}
.
By abuse of notation we write
D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}
for

\{D,C\}_{I}
when
p^{-1}(D)
(resp.
p^{-1}(C)
)
has
l
elements
(resp.
m
elements).
We define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{DCyl}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}},D\big)
to be the singular simplicial complex of the space

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)^{2}\big)^{\sqcup l},(0,1)^{2}\big)

of embeddings from the disjoint union
(0,1)^{2}\times p^{-1}(D)
to
(0,1)^{2}

such that the restriction to each component
is rectilinear, where the space comes equipped with
the subspace topology of the mapping space
with compact-open topology.

If
m\geq 1
,

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m},D\big)
is the empty
set.

(iii)

We define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{DCyl}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m},C\big)

to be the singular complex of the space of embeddings

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)^{2}\times p^{-1}(D)\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C),(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)

such that the rescriction to a component
(0,1)^{2}
is rectilinear,
and the restriction to a component
(0,1)\times S^{1}
is rectilinear.

(iv)

The composition law and the unit are defined in an obvious way.

The colors
D
,
C
together with simplicial sets of maps
constitute a fibrant simplicial colored operad.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
be a simplicial colored operad defined as follows.

(i)

The set of colors of
\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
has three elements,
which we denote by
D
,
C
, and
C_{M}
.

(ii)

Let
I=\langle r\rangle^{\circ}
be a finite set and let

\{D,C,C_{M}\}_{I}
be a set of colors indexed by
I
,
that is, a map
p\colon I\to\{D,C,C_{M}\}
.
By abuse of notation, we write
D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n}
for

\{D,C,C_{M}\}_{I}
when
p^{-1}(D)
(resp.
p^{-1}(C)
,
p^{-1}(C_{M})
)
has
l
elements
(resp.
m
elements,
n
elements).
We set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}},D\big)=\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{DCyl}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}},D\big)
.
If
m+n\geq 1
,

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},D\big)
is the empty
set.

(iii)

We set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m},C\big)=\operatorname{Mult}_{\operatorname{DCyl}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m},C\big)
. If
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C\big)

is the empty set.

(iv)

For
n\neq 1
,

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup n},C_{M}\big)

is the empty set.
For
n=1
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}\big(D^{\sqcup{l}}\sqcup C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M},C_{M}\big)

is the singular simplicial complex of the space of embeddings

\displaystyle(0,1)^{2}\times p^{-1}(D)\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C)\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C_{M})\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

such that the restriction to a component in
(0,1)^{2}\times p^{-1}(D)
is rectilinear,
the restriction to each component in
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C)

is rectilinear, and the restriction to

\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times p^{-1}(C_{M})\simeq(0,1)\times S^{1}

is shrinking.
By definition, if
l=0
,
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}}(C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M},C_{M})=\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}(C^{\sqcup m}\sqcup C_{M},C_{M})
.

(v)

The composition law and the unit map are defined
in an obvious way.

The colors
D
,
C
,
C_{M}
together with simplicial sets of maps constitute a fibrant simplicial colored operad.

Remark 4.6
.

There is a commutative diagram of inclusions of simplicial colored operads:

\textstyle{\operatorname{Cyl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DCyl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}.}

Each inclusion determines a simplicial colored full suboperad.

We obtain an
\infty
-operad from a fibrant simplicial
colored operad.
We recall the construction from
[
25
, Notation 2.1.1.22]
.

Definition 4.7
.

Let
P
be a simplicial colored operad. Let
P_{\rm col}
be the set of colors
of
P
.
We let
P_{\Delta}
be a simplicial category defined as follows:

(i)

The objects of
P_{\Delta}
are maps

a\colon\langle n\rangle^{\circ}\to P_{\rm col}
, that is,
pairs
(\langle n\rangle,(C_{1},\dots,C_{n}))
,
where
\langle n\rangle\in\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
and

(C_{1},\dots,C_{n})

is a finite sequence
(a(1),\dots,a(n))
of colors.

(ii)

Let
C=(\langle n\rangle,(C_{1},\dots,C_{n}))
and
C^{\prime}=(\langle m\rangle,(C_{1}^{\prime},\dots,C_{m}^{\prime}))
be two objects.
The hom simplicial set
\operatorname{Map}_{P_{\Delta}}(C,C^{\prime})

is given by

\displaystyle\coprod_{\alpha\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle}\prod_{j\in\langle m\rangle^{\circ}}\operatorname{Mult}_{P}\left(\{C_{i}\}_{i\in\alpha^{-1}(j)},C^{\prime}_{j}\right)\!.

(iii)

Composition is determined by the composition laws on
\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}

and on
P
in an obvious way.

There is a canonical simplicial functor
P_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}

which sends
(\langle n\rangle,(C_{1},\dots,C_{n}))

to
\langle n\rangle
.
If
P
is fibrant,
the map of simplicial nerves
\mathbf{P}:=\operatorname{N}(P_{\Delta})\to\operatorname{N}(\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast})=\Gamma

constitutes an
\infty
-operad (cf.
[
25
, Proposition 2.1.1.27]
).
We shall refer to
\operatorname{N}(P_{\Delta})\to\operatorname{N}(\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast})=\Gamma

(or
\operatorname{N}(P_{\Delta})
) as the operadic nerve of
P
.
We shall denote by
\mathsf{P}_{\langle n\rangle}
the fiber

\mathbf{P}\times_{\Gamma}\{\langle n\rangle\}
over
\langle n\rangle
.
We usually identify colors with objects in
\mathbf{P}_{\langle 1\rangle}
.

Definition 4.8
.

We apply the construction in Definiton
4.7

to
\operatorname{Cyl}
,
\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}
,
\operatorname{DCyl}
, and
\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
to obtain
\infty
-operads.

•

Let
\mathbf{Cyl}
be the operadic nerve of
\operatorname{Cyl}
.

•

Let
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
be the operadic nerve of
\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}
.

•

Let
\mathbf{DCyl}
be the operadic nerve of
\operatorname{DCyl}
.

•

Let
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
be the operadic nerve of
\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
.

We now recall Kontsevich–Soibelman operad
[
23
]
.

Definition 4.9
.

Let
KS
be the simplicial colored full suboperad of
\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
which
consists of colors
D
,
C_{M}
.
We refer to
KS
as Kontsevich–Soibelman operad.
Let
\mathbf{KS}
be the operadic nerve of
KS
(the notation is slightly
diffrent
from Introduction).
We abuse terminology by referring to it as Kontsevich–Soibelman operad.
In a nutshell,
\mathbf{KS}\subset\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
is the maximal
simplicial subcomplex
spanned by vertices correponding to those tuples which do not contain the color
C
.
It is not difficult to check that
KS
is equivalent to that of
[
16
]
or
[
23
, Section 11.2]
.
In
[
11
]
, a version of
KS
is called the cylinder operad.

Remark 4.10
.

We note that
\operatorname{DCyl}
has a simplicial full suboperad
\langle D\rangle\subset\operatorname{DCyl}

which consists of the single color
D
.
This operad
\langle D\rangle
is a version of the little
2
-cube operad (e.g.,
[
25
, Definition 5.1.0.1]
).
Let
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}
be the operadic nerve of
\langle D\rangle
,
which we shall refer to as the
\infty
-operad of little
2
-cubes.

Remark 4.11
.

We have the diagram in Remark
4.6

and inlusions
\textup{KS}\subset\overline{\operatorname{DCyl}}
,
\langle D\rangle\subset\operatorname{DCyl}
. These inclusions determine
the following diagram of
\infty
-operads:

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Cyl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{DCyl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{KS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}.}

Let
\textup{E}_{1}
be the simplicial operad of little
1
-cubes.
The definition is similar to the case of little
2
-cubes (see, e.g.,
[
25
, Proposition 5.1.0.3]
).
Namely,
\textup{E}_{1}
has a single color
D_{1}
, and for a finite sequence
(D_{1},\dots,D_{1})
,
the simplicial set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{E}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup n},D_{1}\big)
is defined to be
the singular simplicial complex of the space

\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup n},(0,1)\big)
of rectilinear embeddings.
The composition law and the unit
are defined in the obvious way.
Let
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
denote the
\infty
-operad of little
1
-cubes,
that is, the operadic nerve of
\textup{E}_{1}
.

Definition 4.12
.

Let
\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}
be a simplicial colored
operad defined as follows.

(i)

The set of colors of
\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}
has two elements
which we denote by
D_{1}
and
M
.

(ii)

Let
I=\langle r\rangle^{\circ}
be a finite set and let

\{D_{1},M\}_{I}
be a set of colors indexed by
I
,
which is a map
p\colon I\to\{D_{1},M\}
.
We write
D_{1}^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup M^{\sqcup n}
for

\{D_{1},M\}_{J}
when
p^{-1}(D_{1})
(resp.
p^{-1}(M)
)
has
m
elements
(resp.
n
elements).
Let
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup(0,1),(0,1)\big)

be the topological space of embeddings
(0,1)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup(0,1)\to(0,1)

such that the restriction to each component
is rectilinear.
We define
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup M,M\big)
to be the singular simplicial complex of the subspace

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)\times p^{-1}(D_{1})\sqcup(0,1)\times p^{-1}(M),(0,1)\big)\subset\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup(0,1),(0,1)\big).\!

The subspace consists of those rectilinear embeddings
such that the restriction to
(0,1)\times p^{-1}(M)\simeq(0,1)

is shrinking.
If
n\neq 1
,

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{m}}\sqcup M^{\sqcup n},M\big)
is the empty
set.

(iii)

We set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}},D_{1}\big)=\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{E}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}},D_{1}\big)
.
If
m\neq 0
,

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup M^{\sqcup m},D_{1}\big)
is the empty set.

(iv)

The composition law is given by the composition of embeddings, and a unit map is the identity map.

Let
\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}
be the operadic nerve of
\overline{\textup{E}}_{1}
.

Remark 4.13
.

There is an equivalence from
the
\infty
-operad
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
to the associative

\infty
-operad
\textup{Assoc}^{\otimes}
, see
[
25
, Definition 4.1.1.3]
.
Indeed, if
f\colon(0,1)^{\sqcup n}\to(0,1)
is a rectilinear map,
then it determines a linear ordering on the set of connected component

\pi_{0}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup n}\big)
such that
I_{1}>I_{2}
for two components
I_{1}
and
I_{2}

in
(0,1)^{\sqcup n}
if
a<b
for any
a\in f(I_{1})
and any
b\in f(I_{2})
.
It gives rise to a map from
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup n},(0,1)\big)

to the set of linear ordering on
\pi_{0}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup n}\big)
.
It is a homotopy equivalence so that we have an equivalence

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\textup{Assoc}^{\otimes}

(for details, see
[
25
, Example 5.1.0.7]
).

This equivalence

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{Assoc}^{\otimes}
is extended to an equivalence
\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}
of
\infty
-operads,
where
\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}
is the
\infty
-operad (having two colors) which we use
to describe pairs of associative algebras and left modules
[
25
, Definition 4.2.1.7]
.
Indeed, as above, any map

(0,1)^{\sqcup n}\sqcup(0,1)\to(0,1)

in (ii) Definition
4.12
determines a linear ordering
on
\pi_{0}((0,1)^{\sqcup n}\sqcup(0,1))
such that the black component
is the maximal element. It is easy to see that
this ordering induces an equivalence
\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}

which extends
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\textup{Assoc}^{\otimes}
.

4.2.

Following
[
25
]
,
we recall the notion of algebras over an
\infty
-operad.
Let
\mathcal{O}\to\Gamma
be an
\infty
-operads.
Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
whose underlying
\infty
-category is
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\langle 1\rangle}
.
An
\mathcal{O}
-algebra in
\mathcal{M}

is a map
f\colon{\mathcal{O}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
over
\Gamma

which preserve inert morphisms, that is, a map of
\infty
-operads.
We define

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\subset\operatorname{Fun}_{\Gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
to be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}_{\Gamma}\big(\mathcal{O},\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
spanned by
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebras.
We often write
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{M})

for
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

when the structure on
\mathcal{M}
is clear.
We refer to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

as the
\infty
-category of
{\mathcal{O}}
-algebra objects in
\mathcal{M}
,
cf.
[
25
, Definition 2.1.3.1]
.
When
\mathcal{O}
is
\Gamma
,
we write
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

for
\operatorname{Alg}_{\Gamma}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.

Let
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be the
\infty
-operad of little
1
-cubes
with the natural projection.
Let
\big(BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}
be the simplicial category
having a single object
*
and
Hom simplicial set
\operatorname{Hom}_{(BS^{1})_{\Delta}}(*,*)
.
The simplicial set
\operatorname{Hom}_{(BS^{1})_{\Delta}}(*,*)

is the simplicial complex of
S^{1}=\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
, and
the composition is induced by the ordinary
multiplication
S^{1}\times S^{1}\to S^{1}
.
We denote by
BS^{1}
the simplicial nerve of
\big(BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}
.
It can also be regarded as the classifying space of
S^{1}
in
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
p\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\Gamma

be the composite
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{pr}_{1}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
. We note that
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\Gamma
is not
an
\infty
-operad.
Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
Though the above definition of algebra objects is not applicable to

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\Gamma
,
we define
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
as
follows (cf.
[
25
, Definition 2.3.3.20]
).
Let
\rho_{i}\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
be
the unique inert morphism which sends
i\in\langle n\rangle
to
1\in\langle 1\rangle
.
Then
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}_{\Gamma}\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1},\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

spanned by those
maps
F\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

satisfying the condition:
If
C
is an object of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
lying over
\langle n\rangle
,
and for
1\leq i\leq n

\alpha_{i}\colon C\to C_{i}
is a locally
p
-coCartesian morphism
covering

\rho_{i}\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
,
then
F(\alpha_{i})
is an inert morphism
in
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
.
Let
\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}
be the natural functor which induces

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
.
By the construction of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
,

F
belongs to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

if and only if the composite

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

carries any inert morphism in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
lying over

\langle n\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
to an inert morphism
in
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
.
As observed in
[
25
, Remark 2.1.2.9]
, it is equivalent to the
condition that

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

carries any inert morphism in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
to an inert morphism
in
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
whose underlying category we denote by
\mathcal{M}
.
Let
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
denote the functor category
(
function complex
)

which is endowed with the pointwise symmetric monoidal structure induced
by that of
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
. Namely,
the symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
is given by the projection

\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}:=\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\times_{\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\Gamma)}\Gamma\to\Gamma
.
Then there is a canonical equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big).

Similarly, there is a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)
.

Proof.

We prove
that there is an isomorphism of simplicial sets

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big).

Observe that the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category

\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}
is defined by the following
universal property: for a simplicial set
K
, there is a natural
bijection of

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Set}_{\Delta}}\big(K,\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)

with the set of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
which makes the diagram commute

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BS^{1}\times K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\Gamma.}

The assignment
(\alpha,\beta)\mapsto\alpha
induces

\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
.
Therefore, for a simplicial set
L
, a map
L\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)
amounts to
a map
f\colon BS^{1}\times L\times\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
over
\Gamma

such that for any vertex
(a,l)
in
BS^{1}\times L
and for any inert
morphism
i
in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
, the image
f((a,l,i))
is an inert morphism in

\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
(note also that
by construction
BS^{1}
has a single vertex).
Next, we consider the universal property of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.
By the observation before this lemma, for a simplicial set
L
,
a map
L\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

amounts to a map
g
such that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\times\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma}

commutes and
for any vertex
(l,a)
in
L\times BS^{1}
and for any inert
morphism
i
in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
, the image
g((l,i,a))
is an inert morphism in

\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
. Comparing universal properties of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\!\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1}\!,\mathcal{M}\big)\big)
and
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
,
we have a canonical isomorphism of simplicial sets

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\!\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1}\!,\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.
The final assertion also follows from an argument similar to this proof.
∎

Construction 4.15
.

We will define a functor
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}
over
\Gamma
.
To this end,
we consider the following simplicial categories
\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}

and
\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}
.
Let
\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}

be a simplicial category defined as follows.

•

The objects of
\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}
are
objects
\langle n\rangle
in
\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
(which we regard as formal symbols).

•

For
\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle\in\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}
,
the Hom simplicial set
\operatorname{Hom}_{(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1})_{\Delta}}(\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle)
is the singular complex of the space

\displaystyle\coprod_{\alpha\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle}\bigg(\prod_{1\leq j\leq m}\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)\times\alpha^{-1}(j),(0,1)\big)\bigg)\times S^{1}.

•

The composition is determined by the composition of embeddings
and the multiplication
S^{1}\times S^{1}\to S^{1}
.

There is a canoncial projection
\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
.
The simplicial nerve
\operatorname{N}\big(\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}\big)\to\operatorname{N}(\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast})=\Gamma
is

p\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\Gamma
.

•

The objects of
\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}
are
objects
\langle n\rangle
in
\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
(which we regard as formal symbols).

•

For
\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle\in\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}
,
the Hom simplicial set
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}}(\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle)
is the singular simplicial complex of the space

\displaystyle\coprod_{\alpha\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle}\prod_{1\leq j\leq m}\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\times\alpha^{-1}(j),(0,1)\times S^{1}\big).

The composition is determined by the composition of embeddings.

Passing to simplicial nerves, we get
\operatorname{N}(\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta})\simeq\mathbf{Cyl}\to\Gamma
.
Note that there is a canonical
homeomorphism of spaces

\displaystyle\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\times\alpha^{-1}(j),(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\simeq\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big((0,1)\times\alpha^{-1}(j),(0,1)\big)\times\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times\sharp\alpha^{-1}(j)}

(each factor
a\in(S^{1})^{\times\sharp\alpha^{-1}(j)}

determines the rotation of the restriction
(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
). The diagonal map
S^{1}\to\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times\sharp\alpha^{-1}(j)}

induces a map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1})_{\Delta}}(\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}}(\langle n\rangle,\langle m\rangle)

in the natural way. It gives rise to a functor

Z\colon\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}
of simplicial categories.
Then it induces a map

\displaystyle z\colon\ \mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}

over
\Gamma
.
Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
whose underlying category is
\mathcal{M}
.
The restriction along
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}
induces
a functor
z^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.

Proposition 4.16
.

The functor

\displaystyle z^{*}\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Observe that

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup C_{M},C_{M}\big)
is the singular complex of the space which is homeomorphic to
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{E}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup M,M\big)\times\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times n+1}
.
As in the case of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}
, using
\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{E}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup M,M\big)\times S^{1}\to\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{E}_{1}}\big(D_{1}^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup M,M\big)\times\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times n+1}

induced by the diagonal map
S^{1}\to\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times n+1}

we obtain a morphism
\bar{z}\colon\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
over
\Gamma
. The restriction along
\bar{z}
gives rise to

\bar{z}^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.

Proposition 4.17
.

The functor

\displaystyle\bar{z}^{*}\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Corollary 4.18
.

There are canonical equivalences of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(\mathcal{M}^\otimes\big)}{}\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\textup{Assoc}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{E}}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\lx@text@overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\big(\mathcal{M}^\otimes\big)}{}\simeq\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big).

Here
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)=\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}^{\otimes}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)^{\otimes}\big)
.

Proof.

It follows from Lemma
4.14
, Propositions
4.16
and
4.17
, and Remark
4.13
.
∎

The proof of Proposition
4.17
is similar to
Proposition
4.16
.
We prove Proposition
4.16
.

Proof of Proposition
4.16
.

We use the notion of a weak approximation
in the sense of
[
25
, Definition 2.3.3.6]

(since
z\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}
is not an equivalence of
\infty
-operads, it is necessary to use more delicate notion).
According to
[
25
, Theorem 2.3.3.23]
, if two conditions

(i)

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}
is a weak approximation,

(ii)

z\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}

induces an equivalence between the fiber over
\langle 1\rangle

hold,
then
z^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
is an equivalence.
We first prove (ii).
Let

z_{\langle 1\rangle}\colon\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\langle 1\rangle}\to\mathbf{Cyl}_{\langle 1\rangle}

be the map of fibers over
\langle 1\rangle
.
Both fibers consist of a unique object (here we denote it by
*

whose mapping space
\operatorname{Map}(*,*)
is (homotopy) equivalent to
S^{1}
).
Taking into account our construction of
Z\colon\big(\textup{E}_{1}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Cyl}_{\Delta}
, we see that
z_{\langle 1\rangle}
is a homotopy equivalence
BS^{1}\to BS^{1}
.
This proves (ii).
Next we will prove (i).
Let
p\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\Gamma
be the projection.
Let
\textup{Tup}_{n}
be the subcategory of
\Gamma_{/\langle n\rangle}

whose objects are active morphisms
\langle m\rangle\to\langle n\rangle

and whose morphisms are equivalences.
According to a criterion
[
25
, Proposition 2.3.3.14]
,
to prove (i), it is enough to prove that
for any
X\in\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
with
\langle n\rangle=p(X)
,

z
induces a weak homotopy equivalence

\displaystyle u\colon\ A(X):=\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\big)_{/X}\times_{\Gamma_{/\langle n\rangle}}\textup{Tup}_{n}\to\mathbf{Cyl}_{/z(X)}\times_{\Gamma_{/\langle n\rangle}}\textup{Tup}_{n}=:B(z(X)).

Note that both domain and target are
\infty
-categories.
Consequently, it will suffice to show that

u
is a categorical equivalence.
Clearly,
u
is essentially surjective.
We prove that
u
is fully faithful.
The general case is essentially the same as the case
n=1

except for a more complicated notation, so that
we treat the case of
n=1
.
We think of
D_{1}
as the unique object of
\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\langle 1\rangle}
.
Also, we write
D_{1}^{m}
for the unique object of the fiber
\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\big)_{\langle m\rangle}
over
\langle m\rangle
(namely,
D_{1}=D_{1}^{1}
).
Let
f\colon D_{1}^{m}\to D_{1}
be a map
in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
lying over an active morphism

\alpha\colon\langle m\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
of
\Gamma
.
We regard
f
as the product

g\times h\colon(0,1)^{\sqcup m}\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

of a rectilinear map
\phi\colon(0,1)^{\sqcup m}\to(0,1)

and a rectilinear map
h\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
.
Let
f^{\prime}\colon D_{1}^{m}\to D_{1}
be another map
in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}
lying over
\alpha
.
We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\operatorname{Map}_{A(D_{1})}(f,f^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Map}^{\rm eq}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(D_{1}^{m},D_{1}^{m}\big)\times_{\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}(D_{1}^{m},D_{1})}\{f\}\\
&\hphantom{\operatorname{Map}_{A(D_{1})}(f,f^{\prime})}{}\simeq\big(S^{1}\times\Sigma_{m}\big)\times_{(S^{1}\times\Sigma_{m})}\{f\},\end{split}

where
\operatorname{Map}^{\rm eq}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(D_{1}^{m},D_{1}^{m}\big)

is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(D_{1}^{m},D_{1}^{m}\big)

spanned by equivalences, and

\operatorname{Map}^{\rm eq}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(D_{1}^{m},D_{1}^{m}\big)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}}\big(D_{1}^{m},D_{1}\big)
is induced by the composition with
f^{\prime}
.
Here
\Sigma_{m}
denotes the symmetric group (which comes from permutations
of components).
Thus, the mapping space is contractible because
(S^{1}\times\Sigma_{m})\to\big(S^{1}\times\Sigma_{m}\big)
is an equivalence.
Next, we regard the color
C
in
\operatorname{Cyl}
as an object in
\mathbf{Cyl}
that lies
over
\langle 1\rangle
. We denote by
C^{m}
the unique object of
\mathbf{Cyl}

that lies over
\langle m\rangle
.
Let
z(f),z(f^{\prime})\colon C^{m}\to C
be the images of
f
and
f^{\prime}
,
respectively.
Then we have equivalences in
\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{B(C)}(z(f),z(f^{\prime}))\simeq\operatorname{Map}^{\rm eq}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(C^{m},C^{m}\big)\times_{\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(C^{m},C)}\{z(f)\}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Map}_{B(C)}(z(f),z(f'))}{}\simeq\big(\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times m}\times\Sigma_{m}\big)\times_{((S^{1})^{\times m}\times\Sigma_{m})}\{z(f)\},

where

\operatorname{Map}^{\rm eq}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}\big(C^{m},C^{m}\big)
is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(C^{m},C^{m})
spanned by equivalences.
It follows from the canonical equivalence

\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times m}\times\Sigma_{m}\to\big(S^{1}\big)^{\times m}\times\Sigma_{m}
that
\operatorname{Map}_{B(C)}(z(f),z(f^{\prime}))
is contractible. Thus,

\operatorname{Map}_{A(D_{1})}(f,f^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{B(C)}(z(f),z(f^{\prime}))
is an equivalence. We conclude that
u
is a categorical equivalence.
∎

4.3.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
If
\mathbf{P}^{\otimes}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\otimes}
are small

\infty
-operads
and
c\colon\mathbf{P}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{Q}^{\otimes}
is a morphism
of
\infty
-operads (over
\Gamma
), then we denote
by
c^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{M})

the restriction/forgetful functor
along
c
.
If there exists a left adjoint of
c^{*}
,
we denote it by
c_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Q}}(\mathcal{M})
.
If
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
admits small colimits
and its monoidal multiplication functor

\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
preserves small colimits separately
in each variable,
then there exists a left adjoint
c_{!}
, cf.
[
25
, Corollary 3.1.3.5]
.

The diagram
\mathbf{DCyl}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathbf{Cyl}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b}}{{\to}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}

induces

a^{*}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
and

b^{*}\colon

\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.
It gives rise to the fiber product
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})
.
We also have the inclusions
\mathbf{DCyl}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
.
Then
the restriction/forgetful functors induce

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M}).

Now suppose that

\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
admits small colimits
and the tensor product functor

\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
preserves small colimits separately
in each variable.
Let
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{DCyl}
be the canonical inclusion.
We then have an adjoint pair

\displaystyle i_{!}\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\ \,{\colon}i^{*},

where
i_{!}
is fully faithful (indeed, the left adjoint
is given by the operadic left Kan extension so that
the unit map of the adjunction is the identity).
We write
\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
for the essential image of
i_{!}
.
Note that
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.
We set
