\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M}):=\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.

Proposition 4.19
.

Suppose that
q\colon\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
such that the underlying
\infty
-category
\mathcal{M}
has small colimits and
the tensor product functor
\otimes\colon\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}

preserves small colimits separately in each variable.
Then the functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})

is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.
Moreover, it induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M}).

Proposition 4.20
.

The restriction along the inclusion
\mathbf{KS}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}

induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M}).

Now we consider the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})}

and its restriction to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})
.

Corollary 4.21
.

We have canonical categorical equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M}).

Moreover, by Corollary
4.18
,
the
\infty
-category on the left-hand side
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}))}\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big),

where
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

is the composite

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{!}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Alg}^{\mathbf{D}}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big).

In particular, we have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}))}\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big).

This equivalence commutes with projections to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})

in the natural way.

The proof of Proposition
4.19
requires
Lurie–Barr–Beck theorem
[
25
, Corollary 4.7.3.16]
.
Let us consider the comutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U}
\scriptstyle{G}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}.}

The functor
U
is the functor in the statement in Proposition
4.19
,
and
G
is determined by the forgetful/restriction functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})

and the functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\mathcal{M}

given by the evaluation at
C_{M}
. The functor
G^{\prime}
is determined by the first projection and the functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\mathcal{M}

given by the evaluation at
C_{M}
.
According to Lurie–Barr–Beck theorem
[
25
, Corollary 4.7.3.16]
,

U
is a categorical equivalence if the following
conditions are satisfied:

(i)

The functors
G
and
G^{\prime}
admit left adjoints
F
and
F^{\prime}
, respectively.

(ii)

\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})
admits geometric realizations of simplicial objects, which are preserved by
G
.

(iii)

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})
admits geometric realizations of simplicial objects, which are preserved by
G^{\prime}
.

(iv)

The functors
G
and
G^{\prime}
are conservative.

(v)

For any object
X
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
,
the unit map
X\to GF(X)\simeq G^{\prime}UF(X)
induces an equivalence

G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(X)\to GF(X)
in

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.19
.

We will prove the conditions (i), (ii), (iii), (iv), (v).

A morphism
f
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})
is an equivalence
if and only if the evaluations at
D
,
C
,
C_{M}

are equivalences.
Similarly,
a morphism
f
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
is an equivalence
if and only if the evaluations at objects
D,C

are equivalences.
It follows that

G
is conservative. Similarly, we see that
G^{\prime}
is conservative.
Hence (iv) is proved.
The conditions (ii) and (iii) follow from
the existence and the compatibility of sifted colimits
[
25
, Proposition 3.2.3.1]

and the conservativity in (iv).
(If
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
is a presentably symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category,
the condition (i) follows from adjoint functor theorem
since
G
and
G^{\prime}
preserve small limit and filtered colimit. We prove (i) in full generality below.)

We prove the conditions (v) and (i).
For this purpose, we first consider the left adjoint
F^{\prime}
of
G^{\prime}
.
The values under
F^{\prime}
can be described in terms of operadic colimits
if we assume the existence of a left adjoint
F^{\prime}
.
Let
j\colon\mathbf{Cyl}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
be the canonical inclusion.
Let
j^{\prime}\colon\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
be a morphism from the trivial

\infty
-operad to
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
that is determined by
C_{M}
.
Let
\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}
denote the coproduct of
\mathbf{Cyl}
and
\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}
. Namely, it is a coproduct of
\mathbf{Cyl}
and
\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}

in the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-operads, but
we use its explicit construction in
[
25
, Construction 2.2.3.3]
.
By the universal property, the morphism
j
and
j^{\prime}
induces

k\colon\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
.
By
[
25
, Corollary 3.1.3.5]
,
we have an adjoint pair

\displaystyle k_{!}\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}}(\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ {\colon}\!k^{*}.

Here we use the canonical categorical
equivalence
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{Triv}}(\mathcal{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}
.
There are categorical fibrations
\textup{pr}_{1}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
and
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})

induced by the inclusion
\mathbf{Cyl}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
.
The right adjoint
k^{*}
commutes with these projections to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.
Let
Y\colon\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be a map
of
\infty
-operads, which we regard as an object
Y
of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}}(\mathcal{M})
.
For
A\in\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
,
the evaluation of
k_{!}(Y)
at
A

is an operadic
q
-colimit of the map

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)_{/A}^{\textup{act}}:=\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)\times_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}_{/A}^{\textup{act}}\to\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Y}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes},

lying over
\big((\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes})_{/A}^{\textup{act}}\big)^{\triangleright}\to\Gamma
.
See
[
25
, Sections 3.1.1–3.1.3]
for operadic left Kan extensions and operadic
colimits.
Note that

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\operatorname{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup{n}}\sqcup C_{M}^{\sqcup m},C\big)
is the empty set
for
m\neq 0
.
Hence

\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)\times_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}_{/C}^{\textup{act}}\simeq\mathbf{Cyl}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}_{/C}^{\textup{act}}
so that there is a final object
determined by the identity

C\to C
.
It follows that

\textup{pr}_{1}(Y)\to\textup{pr}_{1}k^{*}k_{!}(Y)
is an equivalence in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
for each
Y\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
.
Thus
(k_{!},k^{*})
is an adjunction relative to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.
See

[
25
, Definition 7.3.2.2]
for the notion of relative adjunctions.
The base change of
(k_{!},k^{*})
along
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})

gives rise to an adjunction

\displaystyle l_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\!\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\!\times\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\!\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})}\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ {\colon}\!l^{*}

relative to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
,
where the right adjoint is
G^{\prime}
.
This shows that
there exists a left adjoint
F^{\prime}
of
G^{\prime}
.
In addition,
F^{\prime}\simeq l_{!}
.
We denote informally by
X=(A,B,M)
an object
of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}}(\mathcal{M})
, where
(A,B)\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
,
M\in\mathcal{M}
,

A
is the restriction to
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\subset\mathbf{DCyl}
,
and
B
is the restriction to
\mathbf{Cyl}\subset\mathbf{DCyl}
.
We compute the image of
(A,B,M)
under the left adjoint
l_{!}
.
The left adjoint
F^{\prime}=l_{!}

is induced by
k_{!}
so that

l_{!}(A,B,M)=((A,B),k_{!}(B,M))
,
where
((A,B),k_{!}(B,M))
indicates the object of the fiber product on the right-hand side.
The pair
(B,M)
is an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
.
Thus we will compute
k_{!}(B,M)

in terms of the operadic left Kan extension;
we describe it as a colimit of a certain diagram.
Let
(B,M)\colon\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be
a morphism of
\infty
-operads which corresponds to
(B,M)\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
. Let
\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
be a morphism
determined by
\mathbf{Cyl}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
and the morphism
\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}

classified by the object
C_{M}
in the fiber
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}_{\langle 1\rangle}

(by using the same symbol
C_{M}

we abuse notation).
Let
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}
be the subcategory spanned by those morphisms
whose images in
\Gamma
are active (cf.
[
25
, Definition 2.1.2.1]
).
Let us consider

\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}=\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)\times_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}
.
We have a morphism

\displaystyle p\colon\ K:=\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}\to\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(B,M)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

that extends
(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes})^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}\to\Gamma
.
According to
[
25
, Proposition 3.1.3.2]
, the operadic left Kan extension
k_{!}(B,M)\colon\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

of
k
carries
C_{M}
to a colimit of
p
, that is, the image of the cone point
under an operadic
q
-colimit diagram
p^{\prime}\colon K^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

that extends
p
.
Let
\star
denote the unique object of
\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}

lying over
\langle 1\rangle
.
Let
Sub
be a category defined as follows
[
25
, Definition 2.2.3.2]
: The objects of
Sub
are triples
(\langle n\rangle,S,T)

such that
\langle n\rangle\in\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
, and
S,T\subset\langle n\rangle

are subsets such that
S\cap T=\ast
and
S\cup T=\langle n\rangle
. A morphism
(\langle n\rangle,S,T)\to(\langle n^{\prime}\rangle,S^{\prime},T^{\prime})
is a morphism
\langle n\rangle\to\langle n\rangle
in
\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
such that
f(S)\subset S^{\prime}
and
f(T)\subset T^{\prime}
.
Let

\displaystyle(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1}=(C,\dots,C),\star)

be an object
of
\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}
lying over
\langle n\rangle

such that
(\langle n\rangle,S,T)
is an object of
Sub
,

C^{n-1}
is the unique
object of
\mathbf{Cyl}_{\langle n-1\rangle}
(lying over
\langle n-1\rangle
),
and
\star\in\operatorname{Triv}=\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}_{\langle 1\rangle}
.
This presentation is based on the explicit construction of the coproducts in
[
25
, Construction 2.2.3.3]
.
For our purpose below,
we may assume that
T\subset\langle n\rangle
is of the form
T=\{*,i\}

so that by default
T
in the above object is
of the form
T=\{*,i\}
.
The mapping space from

\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1},\star\big)
to
\big(\langle m\rangle,S^{\prime},T^{\prime},C^{m-1},\star\big)

is given by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle m\rangle\\
\alpha(S)\subset S^{\prime},\ \alpha(T)\subset T^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in\langle m\rangle\backslash T^{\prime}}\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\times\alpha^{-1}(j),(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\Bigg)\times*,

which we regard as an object in
\mathcal{S}
, and
*
indicates the contractible
space which we regard as the mapping space from
\star
to
\star
.
Using this description we consider mapping spaces in

(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes})^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}
.
We abuse notation by writing

\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1},\star,\big(C^{n-1},C_{M}\big)\to C_{M}\big)

for an object of
K
, where

\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1},\star\big)\in\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}

and
(C,\dots,C,C_{M})=\big(C^{n-1},C_{M}\big)\to C_{M}
is a morphism
in
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}

lying over the active morphism
\langle n\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
,
where
\big(C^{n-1},C_{M}\big)
is a sequence of
n-1

C
’s and a single
C_{M}

which we regard as an object in
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}_{\langle n\rangle}
.
Now it is easy to compute the mapping space
from

H=\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1},\star,\big(C^{n-1},C_{M}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big)

to
H^{\prime}=\big(\langle m\rangle,S^{\prime},T^{\prime},C^{m-1},\star,\big(C^{m-1},C_{M}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big)

in

\big(\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\big)\times_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}
.
We think of
f
as an embedding

\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n-1}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

that belongs to

\operatorname{Mult}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}\big(C^{\sqcup n-1}\sqcup C_{M},C_{M}\big)

(namely,
the restricition to the “right component”
(0,1)\times S^{1}
is shrinking).
Consider the restriction
\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n-1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

and its projection
\overline{f}\colon(0,1)^{\sqcup n-1}\to(0,1)
obtained
by forgetting the
S^{1}
-factor, which is
a rectilinear embedding.
If we denote by
D_{1}^{n}

the unique object in the fiber
\big(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\big)_{\langle n\rangle}

over
\langle n\rangle\in\Gamma
,
we can regard
\overline{f}
as
a map
D_{1}^{n-1}\to D_{1}:=D_{1}^{1}
in
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
.
Let
\operatorname{Map}_{(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes})_{/D_{1}}}(\overline{f},\overline{g})

be the discrete mapping space from
\overline{f}\colon D_{1}^{n-1}\to D_{1}
to
\overline{g}\colon D_{1}^{m-1}\to D_{1}
.
Given a morphism
H\to H^{\prime}
, by applying the same procedure to
the induced morphism
C^{n-1}\to C^{m-1}
,
we obtain a map
\operatorname{Map}_{K}(H,H^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes})_{/D_{1}}}(\overline{f},\overline{g})
.
Note that
\operatorname{Map}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}}(C_{M},C_{M})
is contractible.
Consequently, the restriction to the component
C_{M}
gives rise to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{K}(H,H^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Triv}}(\star,\star)\times_{\operatorname{Map}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(C_{M},C_{M})}\operatorname{Map}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}}(C_{M},C_{M})\simeq*\times_{S^{1}}*=\mathbf{Z}.

Taking account of definitions of
\infty
-operads

\mathbf{Cyl}\boxplus\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}
and
\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
,
we see that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{K}(H,H^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes})_{/D_{1}}}(\overline{f},\overline{g})\times\mathbf{Z}

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
L

be the full subcategory of
K
spanned by the single object

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}=\big(\langle 2\rangle,S\simeq\langle 1\rangle,T\simeq\langle 1\rangle,C,\star,(C,C_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big)

(a morphism
j
is uniquely determined up to homotopy).
We now claim that
L\subset K
is cofinal.
It will suffice to prove that for each
\mathsf{V}\in K
, the
\infty
-category

L\times_{K}K_{\mathsf{V}/}
is weakly contractible, see
[
25
, Definition 4.1.3.1]
.
Let
\mathsf{V}=\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,C^{n-1},\star,\big(C^{n-1},C_{M}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big)
.
By the above discussion about mapping spaces,
a morphism
u\colon\mathsf{V}\to\mathsf{Z}
is
uniquely determined by
a\in\mathbf{Z}

since
\operatorname{Map}_{(\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes})_{/D_{1}}}(\overline{f},\overline{j})

is contractible.
Let
u^{\prime}\colon\mathsf{V}\to\mathsf{Z}

be another object of
L\times_{K}K_{\mathsf{V}/}
that is determined by
a^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Z}
. Note that
\operatorname{Map}_{K}(\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{Z})\simeq\mathbf{Z}

and the composition
\operatorname{Map}_{K}(\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{Z})\times\operatorname{Map}_{K}(\mathsf{V},\mathsf{Z})\simeq\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}\to\operatorname{Map}_{K}(\mathsf{V},\mathsf{Z})\simeq\mathbf{Z}

can be identified with the additive operation
+\colon\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{Z}

(up to automorphisms of
\mathbf{Z}
).
It follows that

\operatorname{Map}_{L\times_{K}K_{\mathsf{V}/}}(u,u^{\prime})
is a contractible space
so that the
\infty
-category
L\times_{K}K_{\mathsf{V}/}
is contractible.
Let
p^{\prime}\colon K^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be the operadic
q
-colimit
diagram. Let
p^{\prime\prime}\colon K^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}
be the diagram
obtained by a
q
-coCartesian natural transformation from
p^{\prime}
.
Since we assume that
\otimes\colon\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}

preserves small colimits separately in each variable, then
by
[
25
, Propositions 3.1.1.15 and 3.1.1.16]
,

p^{\prime\prime}
is a colimit diagram of
p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}\colon

K\to\mathcal{M}
,
and the image of the cone point under
p^{\prime\prime}

is naturally equivalent to the image of the cone point under
p^{\prime}
.
Since
L\subset K
is cofinal, we have a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}\simeq\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{L}
.

Indeed,
\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}
is equivalent to

B\otimes M\otimes S^{1}

(this computation is not necessary to
the proof so that the reader may skip this paragraph, but it may be helpful to get feeling for the operadic left Kan extension
F^{\prime}
).
By construction, the composite

L\hookrightarrow K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

is equivalent to a contant diagram so that
p^{\prime\prime}|_{L}\colon L\to\mathcal{M}
is
a contant diagram taking the value
B\otimes M
.
Note that
\operatorname{Map}_{K}(\mathsf{Z},\mathsf{Z})=\mathbf{Z}

and there is a categorical equivalence
L\simeq B\mathbf{Z}\simeq S^{1}
.
We deduce that
\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{L}\simeq(B\otimes M)\otimes S^{1}
.
Namely, the evaluation
F^{\prime}(A,B,M)(C_{M})
of
F^{\prime}(A,B,M)
at
C_{M}
is

\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}\simeq\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{L}\simeq(B\otimes M)\otimes S^{1}
.
Another way to compute it is as follows.
By Corollary
4.18
, we have
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

and
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)
.
These equivalences commute with forgetful functors arising from
the inclusions
\operatorname{As}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}
and
\mathbf{Cyl}\to\overline{\mathbf{Cyl}}
.
The adjunction
(k_{!},k^{*})
can be identified with the composite of adjunctions

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)\times\mathcal{M}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)\times\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^1,\mathcal{M}\big)\big)\times\mathcal{M}}{}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big),

where the left adjunction is induced by the adjunction
\mathcal{M}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)
which consists of the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\to\mathcal{M}
and the left adjoint free functor which
sends
M
to
S^{1}\otimes M
.
The right adjoint in the right adjunction is given
by the evaluation of the module objects

\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)\!\to\!\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)

and

\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)\!\to\!\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big)

induced by
\operatorname{As}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}
.
The left adjoint functors carry
(B,M)
to

\displaystyle\big(B,B\otimes S^{1}\otimes M\big)\in\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\mathcal{M}\big)\big).

Next we will consider
F(A,B,M)
.
Let
r\colon\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
be a morphism
of
\infty
-operads induced by
\mathbf{DCyl}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}

and
\operatorname{Triv}^{\otimes}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
determined by
C_{M}\in\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}_{\langle 1\rangle}

correpondings to the color
C_{M}
(we slightly abuse notation again).
By
[
25
, Corollary 3.1.3.5]
,
we have an adjunction
r_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M}){\colon}\!r^{*}
. This shows that there exists a left adjoint
F=r_{!}
of
G
.
Consider

\displaystyle e\colon\ P:=\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{pr}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(A,B,M)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M}^{\otimes},

where the right arrow classifies the algebra object
(A,B,M)\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\times\mathcal{M}
.
The evaluation of
r_{!}(A,B,M)
at
C_{M}
is a colimit of
e
, that is, the image of the cone point under an operadic
q
-colimit
diagram
e^{\prime}\colon P^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
that extends
e
.
The computation of the colimit of
e
is similar to that of
p
.
We infomally denote by
(\langle n\rangle,S,T,D^{d},C^{c},\star)

an object of
\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}
,
where
\star\in\operatorname{Triv}
, and
(D^{d},C^{c})

indicates the sequence of colors which consists of
d

D
’s
and
c

C
’s which we regard as
an object in
\mathbf{DCyl}_{\langle n-1\rangle}
(
d+c=n-1
).
By abuse of notation, we write

\displaystyle R=\big(\langle n\rangle,S,T,D^{d},C^{c},\star,(D^{d},C^{c},C_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big)

for an object of
P
, where
f\colon\big(D^{d},C^{c},C_{M}\big)\to C_{M}
is a morphism
in
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}
that lies over the active morphism
\langle n\rangle\to\langle 1\rangle
.
We compute the mapping space from
R
to another object

\displaystyle R^{\prime}=\big(\langle m\rangle,S^{\prime},T^{\prime},D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}},\star,(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}},C_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}C_{M}\big).

Given a morphism
\phi\colon R\to R^{\prime}
we have the induced morphism

\big(D^{d},C^{c},C_{M}\big)\to\big(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}},C_{M}\big)
in
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}
.
Notice that it is given by the union of
\big(D^{d},C^{c}\big)\to\big(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}}\big)

and
C_{M}\to C_{M}
over
C_{M}
. Moreover, we can think of
\big(D^{d},C^{c}\big)\to\big(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}}\big)
as
a rectilinear
embedding
\big((0,1)^{2}\big)^{\sqcup d}\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup c}\to\big((0,1)^{2}\big)^{\sqcup d^{\prime}}\sqcup\big((0,1)\times S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup c^{\prime}}

over
(0,1)\times S^{1}
. In this way,
we obtain the induced morphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{P}(R,R^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{DCyl}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}}\big(\big(D^{d},C^{c}\big),\big(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}}\big)\big).

As in the case of
K
,
the restriction to
C_{M}
gives rise to a morphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{P}(R,R^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Triv}}(\star,\star)\times_{\operatorname{Map}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(C_{M},C_{M})}\operatorname{Map}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C_{M}}}(C_{M},C_{M})\simeq*\times_{S^{1}}*=\mathbf{Z}.

It gives rise to an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{P}(R,R^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\mathbf{DCyl}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}}\big(\big(D^{d},C^{c}\big),\big(D^{d^{\prime}},C^{c^{\prime}}\big)\big)\times\mathbf{Z}.

Let
Q\subset P
be the full subcategory spanned
by
\mathsf{Z}

which we think of as an object of
P

in the obvious way.
As in the case of
K
,
using the above description of
\operatorname{Map}_{P}(R,R^{\prime})
we see that
for any
\mathsf{V}\in P
,
Q\times_{P}P_{\mathsf{V}/}
is weakly contractible
so that

Q\subset P
is
cofinal.
Let
e^{\prime}\colon P^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be an operadic

q
-colimit diagram that extends
e
.
Let
e^{\prime\prime}\colon P^{\triangleright}\to\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\langle 1\rangle}
be the diagram obtained by a
q
-coCartesian natural transformation from
e^{\prime}
.
Then the image of the cone point under
e^{\prime}
is

\operatorname{colim}e^{\prime\prime}|_{P}\simeq\operatorname{colim}e^{\prime\prime}|_{Q}
.
(We can also deduce that
r_{!}(A,B,M)(C_{M})=F(A,B,M)(C_{M})

is
B\otimes M\otimes S^{1}\in\mathcal{M}
in the same way as described above.)

The projection of
G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)\to GF(A,B,M)
to
\mathcal{M}

is the canonical map
\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}\to\operatorname{colim}e^{\prime\prime}|_{P}
.
We note that
the canonical functor
K\to P
induces an equivalence

L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Q
.
Consequently,
\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{K}\to\operatorname{colim}e^{\prime\prime}|_{P}

can be identified with the equivalence

\operatorname{colim}p^{\prime\prime}|_{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{colim}e^{\prime\prime}|_{Q}
.
Next we consider the projection of
G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)\to GF(A,B,M)

to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})
.
Taking into account
the equivalences
\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/D}\simeq\mathbf{DCyl}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/D}

and
\mathbf{DCyl}\boxplus\textup{Triv}^{\otimes}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}\simeq\mathbf{DCyl}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}

and the presentation of
F
in terms of operadic colimits,
we see that
the evaluations of unit maps

A\to GF(A,B,M)(D)
and
B\to GF(A,B,M)(C)
are equivalence
so that
the evaluations
A\simeq G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)(D)\to GF(A,B,M)(D)

and
B\simeq G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)(C)\to GF(A,B,M)(C)
of
G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)\to GF(A,B,M)

are also
equivalences.
Consequently,
G^{\prime}F^{\prime}(A,B,M)\to GF(A,B,M)
is an equivalence since
evaluations at
D
,
C
, and the projection to
\mathcal{M}
are equivalences.
This proves (v). We also have proved the existence of
F
and
F^{\prime}
, that is, (i).
The final assertion is clear.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.20
.

The inclusion
j\colon\mathbf{KS}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
induces an adjunction

\displaystyle j_{!}\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\ {\colon}\!j^{*}.

Since
j_{!}
is fully faithful, it is enough to prove that
the essential image of
j_{!}
is
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})
.
Let
X=(A,B,M)\colon\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})

whose evaluations at
D
,
C
and
C_{M}
are
A
,
B
and
M
, respectively.
Note that the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\mathcal{M})\to\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}

induced by evaluations at
D
,
C
, and
C_{M}

is conservative.
Thus if we write
q\colon\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
for the structure
map,
X
belongs to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})

if and only if

\displaystyle p^{\prime}\colon\ K^{\triangleright}:=\big(\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\times_{\mathbf{DCyl}}\mathbf{DCyl}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}\big)^{\triangleright}\to\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

is an operadic
q
-colimit diagram that extends
p=p^{\prime}|_{K}
, see
[
25
, Definition 3.1.2.1]
.
Let
Y\colon\mathbf{KS}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
be an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})
.
Let us consider the image of
C
under

j_{!}(Y)\colon\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
.
It is an operadic
q
-colimit of

\displaystyle e\colon\ L:=\mathbf{KS}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}\to\mathbf{KS}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}.

In view of
[
25
, Propositions 3.1.1.15 and 3.1.1.16]
,
to prove the image of
j_{!}
is contained in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})
,
it is enough to observe that the natural functor

\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\times_{\mathbf{DCyl}}\mathbf{DCyl}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}\to\mathbf{KS}\times_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}_{/C}
is a categorical equivalence.
It follows from the fact that
if
a sequence
E=(D,\dots,D
,
C,\dots,C,C_{M},\dots C_{M})
(regarded as an object
of
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
) contains
C_{M}
, then
there is no morphism from
E
to
C
in
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\textup{act}}
.
Consequently, we have a new adjunction

j_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})\,{\colon}\!j^{*}
.
By the comparison of operadic
q
-colimit diagrams
from
K^{\triangleright}
and
L^{\triangleright}
, the counit map of this adjunction is an equivalence.
Therefore, we obtain
a categorical equivalence
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\mathcal{M})
induced by
j_{!}
(or
j^{*}
).
∎

5
Hochschild cohomology

In this section, we recall Hochschild cohomology spectra of stable
\infty
-categories
\mathcal{C}
.
The definition is based on the principle that,
under a suitable condition on
\mathcal{C}
,
Hochschild cohomology of
\mathcal{C}
is the endomorphism
algebra of the identity functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}
.
Moreover, since
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C})
has the monoidal structure
given by the composition,
Hochschild cohomology is the endomorphism algebra
of the unit object of
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C})

so that it comes equipped with the structure
of an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra, cf.
[
4
,
22
]

(see also references cited in
loc. cit.
for Deligne conjecture concerning Hochschild cochains).
We establish some notation.
Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum.
Let
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
be the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
of
R
-module spectra whose underlying category we denote by
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
be the
\infty
-category
of the associative algebra objects
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Let
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
be the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories
whose morphisms are those functors that preserve small colimits.
This category
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
admits a symmetric monoidal structure,
see
[
25
, Notation 4.8.1.7 and Proposition 4.8.1.15]
.
The
\infty
-category of small spaces
\mathcal{S}
is a unit object in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
.
For
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
,
the tensor product
\mathcal{D}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

comes equipped with a functor
\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{D}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

which preserves small colimits separately in each variable and satisfies
the following universal property: for any
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
, the composition induces a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D}\otimes\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})\to\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{F})

whose essential image is spanned by those functors
\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}

which preserves small colimits separately in each variable,
where
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(-,-)
indicates the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}(-,-)

spanned by those functors which preserves small colimits.
The underlying associative monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

can be regarded as an associative algebra object in

\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
since
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
is presentable and the
tensor product functor
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\times\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

preserves small colimits separately in each variable.
We denote by
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
the
\infty
-category
of left
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
-module
objects
in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
.
Given
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
, we write
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}:=\operatorname{RMod}_{A}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

for the
\infty
-category
of right module objects of
A
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
We remark that the forgetful lax symmetric monoidal functor

\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{Sp}
induces
\operatorname{Mod}_{A}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Mod}_{A}(\operatorname{Sp})
,
where we use the same notation
A
to indicate the image in

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Sp})
.
The functor
\operatorname{Mod}_{A}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Mod}_{A}(\operatorname{Sp})
is an equivalence
of
\infty
-categories (for example, apply Lurie–Barr–Beck theorem
to this functor endowed with projections to
\operatorname{Sp}
) so
that the notation
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
is consistent with that of
[
25
]
.
The category
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
has a natural left module structure

\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\times\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
informally given by
(M,N)\mapsto M\otimes_{R}N
. In what follows, when we treat the tensor product of objects in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

(over
R
), we write
\otimes
for
\otimes_{R}
.
The assignment
A\mapsto\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
gives rise to a functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)

that sends
A
to
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
and carries a morphism
f\colon A\to B
to
the base change functor
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{B};\ N\mapsto N\otimes_{A}B
, that is,
a left adjoint of the forgetful functor
\operatorname{RMod}_{B}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
,
see
[
25
, Section 4.8.3, Notation 4.8.5.10 and Theorem 4.8.5.11]
.
We have the induced functor

\displaystyle I\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})_{R/}\to\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/},

which sends
A
to the base change functor

\operatorname{Mod}_{R}=\operatorname{RMod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
.
The functor
I
is fully faithful and admits
a right adjoint
E
.
A morphism
f\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)

is determined by the image
f(R)
of
R\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

in an essentially unique way (up to a contractible space of choices).
Therefore, an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}

is regarded as a pair
(\mathcal{D},D)

such that
\mathcal{D}
belongs to
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)

and
D
is an object of
\mathcal{D}
.
The essential image of
I
can naturally be identified with

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
. Namely, it
consists of pairs of the form
(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},A)
:

I
carries
A
to
(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},A)
.
Put another way, the essential image is spanned by pairs
(\mathcal{D},D)
such that
\mathcal{D}
is a compactly generated stable

\infty
-category equipped with a single compact generator
D
.
The right adjoint
E
sends
(\mathcal{D},D)

to an endormorphism algebra object
\textup{End}(D)\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

[
25
, Theorem 4.8.5.11]
.
Since the left adjoint
I
is fully faithful,
the unit map
\textup{id}\to E\circ I

is a natural equivalence. Namely, the adjunction
(I,E)

is a colocalization.
If we denote

\mathcal{A}\subset\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}

by the essential image of
I
, then

E
induces a categorical equivalence

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.

The functor
I
is extended to a symmetric monoidal functor.
To explain this, note that
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
comes equipped with a symmetric monoidal structure induced by that of
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
,
see
[
25
, Section 3.2.4]
or Construction
7.9
.
Since
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
such that
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
has small colimits and the tensor product functor

\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\times\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
preserves small colimits separately in each
variable,
we define
\operatorname{Mod}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)

to be the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
-module objects in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
, cf.
[
25
, Section 3.3.3]
.
If we denote the underlying
\infty
-category
by
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
, then

\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)\simeq\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
.
Hence
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{0}^{\otimes}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right))
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure.
In summary, we have the adjunction

\displaystyle I\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\ {\colon}\!E

whose left adjoint is symmetric monoidal and fully faithful, and whose right adjoint is
lax symmetric monoidal.
It gives rise to an adjunction

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}I\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))&\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\big)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\big)\\
&\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\big)\big)\ {\colon}\!E,\end{array}

where we abuse notation by writing
(I,E)
for the
induced adjunction.
By virtue of the canonical equivalence
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}

and the
\infty
-operad version of Dunn additivity theorem
[
25
, Theorem 5.1.2.2]
,
we have a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))

(we can also use additivity theorem
to the equivalence on the right-hand side).

We refer to an object of
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}:=\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
as
an
R
-linear presentable
\infty
-category.
Note that the underlying

\infty
-category of an
R
-linear presentable
\infty
-cartegory
is stable.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
R
-linear presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
\otimes_{R}\colon\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\times\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\to\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
be the tensor product functor.
There exists a morphism object from
\mathcal{D}
to itself
(
i.e., an internal hom object
)

\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})\in\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}

equipped with
e\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}

for
\mathcal{D}
.
Moreover,
(\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}),e)
is
promoted to an object of
\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)

together with a left module action
\mathcal{E}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

According to
[
25
, Corollaries 4.7.1.40 and 4.7.1.41]
,
the second assertion follows from the first assertion.
We will show the existence of a morphism object
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
.
Recall that a morphism object for
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

is an
R
-linear presentable

\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
together with
a morphism
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
such that the composition
induces
an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{C})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

for each
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
,
which informally carries
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{F}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}
.
We first consider the case where
R
is the sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}
.
Let
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

that consists of colimit-preserving functors.
Then
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
together with the
evaluation functor

\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\times\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
exhibits

\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
as an internal hom object.
Thus we have a morphism object
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.
Next, we consider the general case.
Let
F\colon\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)\,{\colon}\!U
be
an adjunction which consists of the forgetful functor
U

and the free functor
F
given informally by
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Here
\otimes
indicates the tensor product in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}
.
If we suppose that
\mathcal{D}
is a free object, i.e.
\mathcal{D}=F(\mathcal{C})=\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
, then there is a morphism object for
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
Indeed, we observe that

\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})=\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
together with

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\otimes_{R}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}^{\prime}

constitutes a morphism object for
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
.
To prove that

\displaystyle\theta\colon\ \operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}\big(\mathcal{P},\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}\big(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\big)\big)\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_{R}(\mathcal{C}\otimes\operatorname{Mod}_{R}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

is an equivalence,
we may and will assume that
\mathcal{P}
is a free object
since the
tensor operation functor
\otimes_{R}
preserves small colimits separately in
each variable (see the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 5.1.2.9]
), and

\mathcal{P}
is a (small) colimit of the diagram of free objects:
for example, using the adjunction
(F,U)
we have a simplicial diagram
of free objects whose colimit is
\mathcal{P}
.
When
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\otimes\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
,
by the adjunction we see that
\theta
is an equivalence.
We put
\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}\mathcal{D}_{i}
, where each
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is a free object.
Then for any
\mathcal{P}\in\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
there exist natural equivalences

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_{R}(\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}\mathcal{D}_{i}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}_{i}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{{L}}_R}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_R(\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I} \mathcal{D}_i),\mathcal{D}')}{}\simeq\lim_{i\in I}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{D}_{i},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{{L}}_R}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_R(\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I} \mathcal{D}_i),\mathcal{D}')}{}\simeq\lim_{i\in I}\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{P},\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D}_{i},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{{L}}_R}(\mathcal{P}\otimes_R(\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I} \mathcal{D}_i),\mathcal{D}')}{}\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{P},\lim_{i\in I}\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D}_{i},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})).

Hence there exists a morphism object
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, that is,
\lim_{i\in I}\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D}_{i},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{\simeq}

be the largest Kan subcomplex
of the underlying
\infty
-category of
\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
, which we regarded as an object in
\mathcal{S}
. By the above proof,

\operatorname{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})^{\simeq}
is equivalent to the mapping space
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
.

We shall write
\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}(\mathcal{D})
for
\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)
.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be an
R
-linear presentable
\infty
-category.
Applying
E\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
,
we define the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module spectrum of
\mathcal{D}

to be

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}_{R}(\mathcal{D}):=E(\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}(\mathcal{D}))

in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
We often abuse notation by identifying
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}_{R}(\mathcal{D})

with its image in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
If no confusion can arise, we write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}_{R}(\mathcal{D})
.

Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
be the
\infty
-category of small
stable idempotent-complete
\infty
-categories
whose morphisms are exact functors.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
stable idempotent-complete
\infty
-category
and let

\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
denote the
\infty
-category of Ind-objects.
Then
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
is a compactly generated stable
\infty
-category.
The inclusion
\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
identifies
the essential image with the full subcategory
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})^{\omega}
spanned by compact obejcts in
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
.
Given
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
, if
we write
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{ex}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
for the full subcategory
spanned by exact functors,
the left Kan extension
[
27
, Proposition 5.3.5.10]
gives rise to
a fully faithful functor
\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{ex}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Fun}^{\textup{L}}(\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}))
whose essential image consists of those functors that carry
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
We set
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}=\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Sp}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
,
which can be regarded as the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}

that consists of stable presentable
\infty
-categories.
The assignment
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})

identifies
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
with the subcategory
of
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
whose objects are
compactly generated stable
\infty
-categories,
and whose morphisms are those functors that preserve compact objects.
The
\infty
-category
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure
from the structure on
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
.
The stable
\infty
-category of compact spectra is a unit object in
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
.
Given two objects
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
of
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
, the tensor product

\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is naturally equivalent to
the full subcategory

(\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime}))^{\omega}\subset\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
spanned by compact objects.
If we let
\textup{Cgt}_{\textup{St}}^{\textup{L}}
denote the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
spanned by compactly generated stable

\infty
-categories, then we have a sequence

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}\to\textup{Cgt}_{\textup{St}}^{\textup{L}}\subset\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}},

where
\textup{Cgt}_{\textup{St}}^{\textup{L}}\subset\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
is closed under
the tensor product so that

\textup{Cgt}_{\textup{St}}^{\textup{L}}
inherits a symmetric monoidal structure from the
structure on
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{\textup{St}}
,
and the left arrow is a symmetric monoidal faithful functor given by
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
.
In
\textup{Cgt}_{\textup{St}}^{\textup{L}}
, every object is dualizable.
For more details, we refer the readers to
[
6
, Section 3]
,
[
25
, Section 4.8]
.

Consider
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
for
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
We let
\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
be the full subcategory
of
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
spanned by compact objects.
This subcategory is the smallest stable subcategory which
contains
A
(regarded as a right module)
and is closed under retracts.
When
A
belongs to
\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
,
we write
\operatorname{Perf}_{A}
for
\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
.
In this case,
\operatorname{Perf}_{A}
is closed under taking tensor product
so that it inherits a symmetric monoidal structure from
that of
\operatorname{Mod}_{A}^{\otimes}
.
We usually regard the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category

\operatorname{Perf}^{\otimes}_{R}
as an object of
\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
, and
we write
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
for
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}^{\otimes}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
.
We refer to an object of
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}

as a small
R
-linear stable
\infty
-category.
Since
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Perf}_{R})^{\otimes}
,
there is a natural symmetric monoidal functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}\to\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}=\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}}\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}\right)
which carries
\mathcal{C}
to
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
.

Definition 5.4
.

Given
\mathcal{C}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
,
we define the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}_{R}(\mathcal{C})

to be

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{R}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}))
.
If no confusion can arise, we write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}_{R}(\mathcal{C})
.

6
Hochschild homology

Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum.
Suppose that we are given a small
R
-linear stable
\infty
-category

\mathcal{C}
.
In this section, we assign to
\mathcal{C}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
the Hochschild homology
R
-module
spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
For the main purpose of this paper,
we require the following additional structures:

•

the
R
-module spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
has
an action of the circle
S^{1}
.
Namely,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is promoted to an object
of
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
, and the assignment
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

gives rise to a functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
,

•

if
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
is equipped with
a pointwise symmetric monoidal strcuture induced by that of
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
,
then the above functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)

is promoted to
a symmetric monoidal functor from
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
to
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
.

To this end,
we will use enriched models of stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-categories, i.e., spectral categories.

Symmetric spectra.

We give a minimal review of the theory of symmetric spectra,
introduced and developed
in
[
17
]
.
This theory provides a nice foundation of the homotopy theory of highly structured ring spectra as well as a theoretical basis
for spectral categories.
We let
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
be the closed symmetric monoidal category of symmetric spectra.
We write
\mathbb{S}
for the unit object which we call the sphere spectrum.
We use the notation slightly different from
[
17
,
32
]
:

\mathbb{S}
is
S
in
[
17
]
.
We use a symmetric monoidal proper combinatorial model category structure on

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
satisfying the monoid axiom in the sense of
[
30
, Definition 3.3]
,
in which a weak equivalence is a stable equivalence.
There are several versions of such model structures.
We here focus on two model structures.
One is described in
[
17
, Theorems 3.4.4 and 5.4.2, Corollaries 5.3.8 and 5.5.2]

which is called the stable model structure.
In
[
32
]
, it is proved that there is
another model structure called the stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure.
The difference (relevant to us)
between stable model structure and stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure
is that
cofibrations in the stable model structure
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.4]

are contained in the class of cofibrations in
the stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure while
both have the same class of weak equivalences.
Let
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
denote the category of commutative algebra objects
in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
. We refer to an object of
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)

as a commutative symmetric ring spectrum.
The category
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
admits a model category structure:
we use the model structure on
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
, defined in
[
32
, Theorem 3.2]
in which a morphism is a weak equivalence if the underlying morphism
in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
is a stable
equivalence.
The stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure on
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
has
the following pleasant property:
if
\mathbb{R}
is a cofibrant object in
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
, then
the underlying object
\mathbb{R}
in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
is cofibrant
with respect to the stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure,
see
[
32
, Section 4]
.

Let
\mathbb{R}
be a commutative symmetric ring spectrum,
which we think of as a model of
R\in\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{Sp})
.
Unless otherwise stated, we assume that

\mathbb{R}
is cofibrant in
\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
.
We let
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
denote the category of
\mathbb{R}
-module objects
in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
, which is endowed with the natural symmetric monoidal
structure induced by the structure on
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
.
In virtue of
[
32
, Theorem 2.6]
(or
[
30
, Theorem 4.1]
),
there is a combinatorial symmetric monoidal
projective model structure on
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
satisfying the monoid axiom,
in which a morphism is a weak equivalence (resp. a fibration)
if the underlying morphism in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
is a stable equivalence
(resp. a fibration with respect to stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure).
We refer to this model structure as the stable
\mathbb{R}
-model structure.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathbb{R}
be a commutative symmetric ring spectrum.
An
\mathbb{R}
-spectrum category is a category
enriched over
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
.
More explicitly,
a (small)
\mathbb{R}
-spectrum category
\mathcal{A}
consists of the data:

•

A (small) set of objects,

•

An
\mathbb{R}
-module symmetric spectrum
\mathcal{A}(X,Y)\in\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
for each ordered pair of objetcs
(X,Y)
,

•

The composition law
\mathcal{A}(Y,Z)\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{A}(X,Y)\to\mathcal{A}(X,Z)

satisfying the standard associativity axiom,

•

\mathbb{S}\to\mathcal{A}(X,X)
for each object
X
that
satisfies the standard unit axiom.

Here
\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}
denotes the wedge product
over
\mathbb{R}
, which defines the tensor product in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
.
A functor of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories is an enriched functor,
that is, a functor as enriched categories.
We refer to them as
\mathbb{R}
-spectral functors.
We write
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
for
the category of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories whose morphisms
are
\mathbb{R}
-spectral functors.
We refer to an
\mathbb{S}
-spectral category
(resp. an
\mathbb{S}
-spectral functor)
as a spectral category (resp. a spectral functor). We write
\wedge
for
\wedge_{\mathbb{S}}
.

Thanks to works
[
6
, Corollary 2.4]
,
[
28
, Theorem 1.1]
,
[
33
, Theorem 7.25]
,

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
admits a combinatorial model structure
whose weak equivalences are Dwyer–Kan equivalences (DK-equivalences for short).
See, e.g.,
[
6
, Definition 2.1]
for DK-equivalences.

Let us recall the notion of Morita equivalences in the context
of spectral categories, see

[
6
, Sections 2 and 4]
for an excellent account.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be a small spectral category and
let
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)
be the spectral
category of spectral functors.
There is a combinatorial spectral model structure
where the class of weak equivalences (resp. fibrations)
are objectwise stable equivalences
(resp. objectwise fibrations with respect to the stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure)
[
29
, Appendix]
.
The enriched Yoneda embedding

\mathcal{A}\to\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)

is contained in the full subcategory of cofibrant objects.
If we replace
\mathcal{A}
by a fibrant object in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}
,
the embedding lands in
the full subcategory
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)^{\rm cf}

that consists of cofibrant and fibrant objects.
Let
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be a spectral functor of spectral categories.
Then we have a Quillen adjunction

\displaystyle F_{!}\colon\ \operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{B}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)\ {\colon}\!F^{*},

where
F^{*}
is determined by the composition with

\mathcal{A}^{\rm op}\to\mathcal{B}^{\rm op}
, see
[
29
, Appendix]
.
Let
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
be the homotopy category of
\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A}):=\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)^{\rm cf}
. It constitutes a triangulated category.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})
be the smallest thick subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})

that contains the image of
\mathcal{A}
under the Yoneda embedding.
The subscript “pe” stands for “perfect”.
We write
\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})
for the full subcategory
of
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\right)^{\rm cf}
spanned by those
objects that belong to
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})
.
If
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a spectral functor,
we have
the induced (left-derived) functor
\mathbb{L}F_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
.
Since

\mathbb{L}F_{!}
is an exact functor of triangulated categories,
it follows that the restrcition of
\mathbb{L}F_{!}
induces

\mathbb{L}F_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})
.

Definition 6.2
.

We say that a spectral functor
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a Morita equivalence
if the induced functor
\mathbb{L}F_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})

is an equivalence of categories.
When
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-spectral functor,

F
is said to be a Morita equivalence
if
F
is a Morita equivalence as a spectral functor.

Let
\mathbb{R}
be a commutative symmetric ring spectrum.
Let us recall the tensor product of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories.
Suppose that we are given
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\in\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The tensor product
\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}
is defined by the following data:

•

The set of objects of
\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}
is the set of pairs

(A,B)
, where
A
is an object of
\mathcal{A}
, and
B
is an object
of
\mathcal{B}
,

•

\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}((a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))=\mathcal{A}(a,a^{\prime})\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}(b,b^{\prime})

for
(a,b)
,
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}
.

This tensor product determines a symmetric monoidal structure
on
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
A unit object is defined as follows:
Let
B\mathbb{R}
be the spectral category which has a single object
\ast

together with the morphism ring spectrum
B\mathbb{R}(\ast,\ast)=\mathbb{R}
.
The composition
\mathbb{R}\wedge\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and the unit

\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{R}
are determined by the algebra structure
on
\mathbb{R}
in an obvious way. Clearly,
B\mathbb{R}

is a unit object in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Since
\mathbb{R}
is commutative, we can also think of
B\mathbb{R}
as a symmetric monoidal
spectral category. Namely, it is a commutative algebra object
in the symmetric monoidal category
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}
.
Note that an
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category
\mathcal{A}
is
regarded as a
B\mathbb{R}
-module in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
.
Namely, there is a canonical equivalence of categories

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}})
,
where the target is the category of
B\mathbb{R}
-module objects
in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}
.

For technical reasons, we use the notion of pointwise-cofibrant spectral categories, cf.
[
7
, Section 4]
.
We say that an
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category
\mathcal{A}
is pointwise-cofibrant
if each morphism spectrum
\mathcal{A}(X,Y)
is cofibrant in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})

with respect to the stable
\mathbb{R}
-model structure.
Using the same argument as that in the proof in
[
7
, Proposition 4.1]
,
we have:

Proposition 6.3

(
[
7
]
)
.

(i)

Every
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category is functorially Morita equivalent to
a pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category with the same objects.

(ii)

The subcategory of pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category is closed under the tensor product.

(iii)

If
\mathcal{A}
is a pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category, the tensor operation
\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}(-)
preserves Morita equivalences and colimits.

(iv)

If
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are both pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories, then the
\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B}
computes the derived tensor product.

We denote by
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
the category of small pointwise-cofibrant
spectral categories.
By Proposition
6.3
,

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
admits a symmetric monoidal structure
given by tensor products, and the tensor products
preserves Morita equivalences in each variable.
Similarly, we denote by
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}
the category of small
pointwise-cofibrant

\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories.

Inverting morphisms.

We recall the notion of
\infty
-categories obtained from an
\infty
-category
endowed with a set of morphisms.
We refer the readers to
[
25
, Sections 1.3.4 and 4.1.3]
for more details.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
\infty
-category.
Suppose that we are given a set
S
of edges (morphisms)
(we assume all equivalences are contained in
S
).
Then there exists
an
\infty
-category

\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]
together with
\xi\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]

such that for any
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
the composition
induces a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}\big(\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big],\mathcal{D}\big)\to\operatorname{Map}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})

whose essential image consists of those functors
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
which carry edges in
S

to equivalences in
\mathcal{D}
.
We shall refer to
\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]

as the
\infty
-category obtained from
\mathcal{C}

by inverting
S
. We note that

\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]
is generally not locally small even when
\mathcal{C}
is so.
When
\mathcal{C}
is an ordinary category,
an explicit construction of
\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]

is given by the hammock localization
[
13
]
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
Let
S
be a set of edges in
\mathcal{C}

such that all equivalences are contained in
S
.
Assume that
for any object
C\in\mathcal{C}
and any morphism
C_{1}\to C_{2}
in
S
,
the induced morphisms
C\otimes C_{1}\to C\otimes C_{2}

and
C_{1}\otimes C\to C_{2}\otimes C

belong to
S
.
Then there exists a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}

together with a symmetric monoidal functor

\tilde{\xi}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}
whose underlying functor is equivalent to
\xi
.
There is a universal property: for any symmetric
monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}

the composition induces a fully faithful functor

\operatorname{Map}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{C}\big[S^{-1}\big]^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Map}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\big)

whose essential image consists of those
functors
F\colon\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
which carry morphisms in
S

to equivalences in
\mathcal{D}
.
Here
\operatorname{Map}^{\otimes}(-,-)
indicates the space of symmetric monoidal functors.

Example 6.4
.

Let
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
be the full subcategory that consists of cofibrant objects. The tensor product
\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}
given by the wedge product over
\mathbb{R}
preserves cofibrant objects.
In addition, if
C\in\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
and
f\colon C_{1}\to C_{2}

is a weak equivalence (i.e., stable equivalence), then

C\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}f
is a weak equivalence.
Consequently, we have the symmetric monoidal

\infty
-category
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}

obtained by inverting weak equivalence.
In the case of
\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{S}
,
by the characterization of
\operatorname{Sp}^{\otimes}

[
25
, Corollary 4.8.2.19]
,
there is a canonical (unique) symmetric monoidal equivalence

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{S})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}^{\otimes}
.
In addition, if we denote
R
by the image of
\mathbb{R}

in
\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{Sp})
, then by
[
25
, Theorem 4.3.3.17]
there is a canonical symmetric monoidal
equivalence

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{S})^{c}[W^{-1}]^{\otimes})\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Sp}^{\otimes})=\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
.
Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
be the category of associative
algebra objects in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
, which is endowed with the symmetric
monoidal structure induced by that of
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
.
Then if
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}

denotes the associated symmetric monoidal

\infty
-category
obtained by inverting weak equivalences,
then
we have equivalences of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\big(\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}),

where the left equivalence follows from
the rectification result
[
25
, Theorem 4.1.8.4]
.
In particular, given an associative algebra
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
,
there is an associative algebra
\mathbb{A}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)

together with an equivalence
\sigma\colon\mathbb{A}\simeq A
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
. In this case, we say that
\mathbb{A}
(together with
\sigma
) represents
A
.

Example 6.5
.

We use the symbol
M
to indicate the class of Morita equivalences in

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
or
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}
.
By Proposition
6.3
, we can invert
the class of Morita equivalences
M
to obtain
symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}
and

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}
.
Thanks to multiplicative Morita theory
[
7
, Theorem 4.6]
,
there is a canonical equivalence of symmetric monoidal

\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}.

Construction 6.6
.

There is a sequence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]

such that
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}
and
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
are ordinary symmetric
monoidal categories, the left arrow is the forgetful
lax symmetric monoidal
functor,
and the right arrow is the canonical symmetric monoidal functor.
Consequently, if we write
R

for the image of
\mathbb{R}
in
\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{Sp})
,
it gives rise to a lax symmetric monoidal functor

\displaystyle\pi\colon\ \operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}).

To obtain
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
, we need to check that
the essential image of
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}

is contained in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}
.
It is enough to show that the forgetful functor
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{S})=\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
carries cofibrant objects
to cofibrant objects in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
.
For this purpose, we recall that cofibrations in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}

with respect to the stable
\mathbb{S}
-model structure
is the smallest weakly saturated class
[
27
, Definition A.1.2.2]
of morphisms
that contains
\{\mathbb{S}\otimes i\}_{i\in\operatorname{Mon}}
, where
\operatorname{Mon}
is
the class of monomorphisms of symmetric sequences, and

\mathbb{S}\otimes i
denotes the morphism of symmetric spectrum
induced by
i
, namely,
\mathbb{S}\otimes(-)
is the left adjoint of the forgetful functor from
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
to the category of symmetric sequences, see
[
32
]
.
The class of cofibrations in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})

with respect to the stable
\mathbb{\mathbb{R}}
-model structure
is the smallest weakly saturated class of morphisms
containing
\{\mathbb{R}\otimes i=\mathbb{R}\wedge(\mathbb{S}\otimes i)\}_{i\in\operatorname{Mon}}
.
Note that we assume that
\mathbb{R}
is a cofibrant object in

\operatorname{CAlg}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big)
so that the underlying object
\mathbb{R}
is cofibrant in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
.
It follows that the underlying morphisms

\mathbb{R}\otimes i
in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
are cofibrations.
Since
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
preserves colimits,

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}
preserves cofibrations.

The following is a rectification result for
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}^{\otimes}_{R}
.

Proposition 6.7
.

\pi\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
in Construction
6.6
induces
a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}^{\otimes}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big).

In particular, we have
a canonical symmetric monoidal equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})=\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}^{\otimes}.

Proof.

The underlying functor is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.
In fact, there are sequence of categorical equivalences
