\displaystyle\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc})\big[M^{-1}\big]\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big),

where the middle
\infty
-category is obtained from
the ordinary category
\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big)

by inverting Morita equivalences (we do not claim that
the middle
\infty
-category
admits a monoidal structure).
The right arrow is induced by
\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big)\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
.
The left arrow is induced by the inclusion

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big)\subset\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}
. The functorial cofibrant resolution

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}
induces an inverse equivalence of
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\allowbreak\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big)\big[M^{-1}\big]

(a cofibrant one is pointwise-cofibrant).
It is proved in
[
9
, Theorem 5.1]

that the right arrow is a categorical equivalence.
Next, we prove that
\pi\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)

is symmetric monoidal.
Namely, we show that the natural morphisms

\operatorname{Perf}_{R}\to\pi(B\mathbb{R})
and

\pi(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{R}\pi(\mathcal{B})\to\pi(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})

are equivalences in
\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
, where
\pi(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{R}\pi(\mathcal{B})
indicates the tensor product
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
.
By construction,
the morphism
\operatorname{Perf}_{R}\to\pi(B\mathbb{R})
is an equivalence.
We prove that
\pi(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{R}\pi(\mathcal{B})\to\pi(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})

is an equivalence.
We here write
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm pe}

for the
\mathbb{R}
-spectral full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)^{\rm cf}
that consists of cofibrant-fibrant objects lying over
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}

(
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\rm pe}
is DK-equivalent to
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})

as spectral categories,
see the proof of Lemma
6.11
and Claim
6.12

for the notation).
For any other
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category
\mathcal{P}
,
we define
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}_{\rm pe}
in the same way.
By Claim
6.12
below, the image of
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
is equivalent to
\pi(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})
in the natural way. Let
\{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
be
the filtered family (poset) of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral full subcategories
of
\mathcal{A}
such that for any
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
,

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda})

(or
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)^{\rm cf}
) admits a single compact generator (so that

(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\lambda})_{\rm pe}
is Morita equivalent to
B\mathbb{A}

for some
\mathbb{A}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
, where
B\mathbb{A}

has one object
\ast
with the morphism ring spectrum
B\mathbb{A}(*,*)=\mathbb{A}
).
Then we have
the filtered family (poset) of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral full subcategories

\big\{\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}\big\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

of
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
. The filtered colimit
of this family in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]

is
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
.
Indeed, from the categorical
equivalence
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})

and the conservative colimit-preserving functor
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
, if we transfer
\big\{\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}\big\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}
into the filtered family of stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-categories, we are reduced to proving that the colimit of the resulting diagram in
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
is a stable
\infty
-category
obtained from the spectral category
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
.
We think of
\big\{\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}\big\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

as a filtered diagram (poset) of full subcategories of
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
. Since
\cup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}=\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
, a colimit of this diagram in
\textup{Cat}_{\infty}
is naturally equivalent to

\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
.
Note that a filtered colimit of stable
\infty
-categories in
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
naturally
coincides with a colimit which is taken in
\textup{Cat}_{\infty}

[
25
, Proposition 1.1.4.6]
.
It follows that
\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}
is a colimit of
\big\{\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}\big\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}

in
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}
.
We then deduce that
\operatorname{colim}_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\widehat{(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}\to\widehat{(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})}_{\rm pe}

is an equivalence in
\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
.
In addition, we note that the tensor product in

\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
preserves small colimits
in each variable since
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}^{\otimes}
is a symmetric
monoidal compactly generated
\infty
-category whose tensor product preserves
small colimits in each variable (see
[
6
, Corollary 4.25]
).
Therefore, taking into account Proposition
6.3
(3),
we may and will suppose that
\mathcal{A}=B\mathbb{A}
for some
\mathbb{A}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
.
Taking the same procedure to
\mathcal{B}
,
we may and will suppose that
\mathcal{B}=B\mathbb{B}

for some
\mathbb{B}\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
.
We write
A
and
B
for the images of
\mathbb{A}
and
\mathbb{B}
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
, respectively.
In this situation, we have
a canonical equivalence

\pi(\mathcal{A})\otimes_{R}\pi(\mathcal{B})\simeq(\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{B})^{\omega}\simeq(\operatorname{RMod}_{A\otimes_{R}B})^{\omega}\simeq\widehat{(B\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{B})}_{\rm pe}\simeq\pi(\mathcal{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{B})

(cf.
[
5
, Proposition 4.1(2)]
,
[
25
, Theorem 4.8.5.16]
),
where
(-)^{\omega}
indicates the full subcategory of compact objects. Hence
\pi\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
is symmetric monoidal, which induces
a symmetric monoidal functor

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}^{\otimes}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
whose underlying functor
is a categorical equivalence. Thus we have the desired symmetric monoidal equivalence

\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mod}_{B\mathbb{R}}^{\otimes}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)
.
∎

Using the equivalence in Proposition
6.7
,
we obtain equivalences of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

\displaystyle\theta\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}^{\otimes}=\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}^{\otimes}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)\simeq\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}.

Next, to an
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category we assign
Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum endowed with circle action.
The construction is based on the Hochschild–Mitchell cyclic nerves

(cf.
[
7
,
8
]
)
.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a pointwise-cofibrant small
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category.
Let
p\geq 0
be a nonnegative integer.
Let

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}:=\bigvee_{(X_{0},\dots,X_{p})}\mathcal{A}(X_{p-1},X_{p})\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\dots\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{A}(X_{0},X_{1})\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{A}(X_{p},X_{0}).

The coproduct is taken over the set of sequences

(X_{0},\dots,X_{p})
of objects of
\mathcal{A}
.
The composition in
\mathcal{A}
determines degeneracy maps

d_{0},\dots,d_{p}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p-1}
,
and the unit map
\mathbb{S}\to\mathcal{A}(X_{i},X_{i})
determines
face maps
s_{0},\dots,s_{p}\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p+1}
.
The cyclic permutation

(X_{0},\dots,X_{p})\mapsto(X_{p},X_{0},\dots,X_{p-1})

gives rise to the action of the cyclic group
\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
on
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}
.
The family
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{\bullet}:=\{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}\}_{p\geq 0}
equipped with
degeneracy maps and face maps
form a simplicial object in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
.
If we take into account the action of
\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
on
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}
,
then
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{\bullet}=\{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}\}_{p\geq 0}
is promoted to
a cyclic object,
that is, a functor
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})

from (the opposite category of) the cyclic category of Connes
such that the composite
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})

is the simplicial object.
Here
\Lambda
denotes the Connes’ cyclic category,
see
[
10
, Section 2]
,
[
24
]

for the definition of the cyclic category.

We let
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)
denote the ordinary
functor category from
\Lambda^{\rm op}
to
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
.
The category
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)
inherits
a symmetric monoidal structure given by the pointwise tensor product

F\otimes G([n])=F([n])\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}G([n])
.

From the definition of the tensor product of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories
and the construction of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{p}
, it is straightforward
to check that
the assignment
\mathcal{A}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{\bullet}
determines
a symmetric monoidal functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big).

The image of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}
is contained in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)

since the stable
\mathbb{R}
-model structure satisfies
the axiom of symmetric monoidal model categories.
Let
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
be the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
obtained from

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}

by inverting stable equivalences.
The underlying
\infty
-category is presentable since

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
is a combinatorial model category.
There is a canonical symmetric monoidal functor

\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)

induced by the symmetric monoidal
functor
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
.

We recall the following results from
[
10
,
18
,
24
]
:

Lemma 6.9
.

(i)

Let
\Lambda\to\overline{\Lambda}
be the groupoid completion.
Namely, it is induced by a unit map of the adjunction
\textup{Cat}_{\infty}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{S}\,{\colon}\!\iota
, where
\iota
is the fully faithful inclusion.
Then
\overline{\Lambda}
is equivalent to
BS^{1}
in
\mathcal{S}
.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
F\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\!\to\mathcal{C}
be a cyclic object
in
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
F^{\prime}\colon BS^{1}\!\to\mathcal{C}
be a functor.
Let
\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}
be the map determined by the unique object
of
BS^{1}
.
Consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Delta^{\rm op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{\rm op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{BS^{1},}

where we regard
\Delta^{0}
as the groupoid completion of
\Delta^{\rm op}

(\Delta^{\rm op}
is sifted so that the groupoid completion is given
by the contractible space
\Delta^{0}
,
cf.
[
27
, Section 5.5.8]
)
.
Then
F^{\prime}
is a left Kan extension of
F
along

\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}
if and only if
the composite
\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}\to\mathcal{C}
is a colimit
of the composite
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

The first assertion is a result of Connes, see
[
10
, Théorème 10]
,

[
18
,
24
]
.
The second assertion is proved in
[
18
, Proposition 1.1]
.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
R\colon\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)
be the functor induced by composition with

\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\overline{\Lambda}^{\rm op}\simeq BS^{1}
.
Let

\displaystyle L\colon\ \operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)

be a left adjoint

(
the existence of a left adjoint follows from the adjoint functor theorem
[
27
]

and the fact that both
\infty
-categories are presentable
)
. Then this left adjoint is symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

Since the right adjoint is a symmetric monoidal functor,
the left adjoint is an oplax symmetric monoidal functor.
Thus it is enough to show that

L(F\otimes G)\to L(F)\otimes L(G)
is an equivalence
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)
for
F
,
G\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
.
To this end, note first that

L(F),L(G)\colon BS^{1}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]

are given by left Kan extensions of
F
and
G
, respectively,
along
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}
.
By Lemma
6.9
(ii),

F^{\prime}\colon BS^{1}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
is a left Kan extension
of
F\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
if and only if
the image of the unique object
\ast
of
BS^{1}
under
F^{\prime}
is a colimit of the composite
F_{\Delta}\colon\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
.
Let
G_{\Delta}
be the restriciton of
G
to
\Delta^{\rm op}
.
Then
L(F\otimes G)(\ast)
is a colimit of the composite
\Delta^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{diag}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Delta^{\rm op}\times\Delta^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F_{\Delta}\times G_{\Delta}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}[W^{-1}]\times\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\otimes}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
.
On the other hand,
L(F)\otimes L(G)(\ast)

is a colimit of
\Delta^{\rm op}\times\Delta^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F_{\Delta}\times G_{\Delta}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\times\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\otimes}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
since the tensor product
of
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]
preserves small colimits separately in each variable.
Note that the diagonal functor
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Delta^{\rm op}\times\Delta^{\rm op}

is cofinal.
Thus the canonical morphism
L(F\otimes G)(\ast)\to L(F)\otimes L(G)(\ast)
is an equivalence.
This means that
L
is symmetric monoidal.
∎

Assembling these lemmata and constructions, we obtain
a sequence of symmetric monoidal functors

\displaystyle H\colon\ \operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{H\colon\ \operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\ \ }{}\simeq\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big).

More explicitly, the composition of
H
and the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]

sends a pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category

\mathcal{A}
to the homotopy colimit of the simplicial object

\Delta^{\rm op}\hookrightarrow\Lambda^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(\mathcal{A})_{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
.

Next, we observe the invariance under Morita equivalences.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories.
Let
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-spectral
functor
which is a Morita equivalence.
Then
H\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)

carries
F
to an equivalence in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
.

Proof.

It will suffice to prove that

H(F)
is an equivalence in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
By
[
8
, Theorems 5.9 and 5.11]
,
the image of
H(F)
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\simeq\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big[W^{-1}\big]
is an equivalence
if
F\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is a Morita equivalence
over

\mathbb{R}
.
We explain the notion of a Morita equivalence
over

\mathbb{R}
,
which we distinguish from the notion of Morita equivalences for the moment.
Let
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)
be the
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category
of
\mathbb{R}
-spectral functors. As in the case of
\mathbb{R}=\mathbb{S}
,
it admits a combinatorial
\mathbb{R}
-spectral model structure
whose weak equivalences (resp. fibrations) are objectwise
stable equivalences (resp. fibrations).
Let
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
denote the homotopy (triangulated) category of the full subcategory
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)^{\rm cf}
spanned by cofibrant and fibrant objects.
Let
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
be the smallest thick subcategory
that contains the image of the Yoneda embedding
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We define
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})_{\mathbb{R}}
in a similar way.
The functor
F
induces
(\mathbb{L}F_{!})_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})_{\mathbb{R}}

as
F
induces
\mathbb{L}F_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})
.
We say that
F
is a Morita equivalence over
\mathbb{R}
if

(\mathbb{L}F_{!})_{\mathbb{R}}
is an equivalence.
Thus to prove our assertion, it is enough to show the following claim:

Claim 6.12
.

There exist equivalences
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})

and
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})

which identifies
(\mathbb{L}F_{!})_{\mathbb{R}}
with

\mathbb{L}F_{!}
up to natural equivalence.
In particular,
F
is a Morita equivalence over
\mathbb{R}
if and only if

F
is a Morita equivalence.

Proof.
We let
\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}

be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}_{\mathbb{R}}\big(\mathcal{A}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})\big)^{\rm cf}

spanned by those objects that belongs to
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
The Yoneda embedding

I\colon\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
(after replacing
\mathcal{A}
by a fibrant one)
induces
I_{!}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}\big(\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}\big)
.
By
[
6
, Proposition 4.11]
,
we deduce that the canonical functor

\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}\big(\mathcal{D}^{\Sigma}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}\big)

is a DK-equivalence. Thus we have

\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}

(in the homotopy category of
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}
).
By the correspondence between
stable idempotent-complete
\infty
-categories and
spectral categories up to Morita equivalences
(
[
6
, Theorems 4.22 and 4.23]
),
passing to stable (idempotent-complete)
\infty
-categories, we may replace

\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}^{\Sigma}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}

by the induced exact functor of stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-categories which we denote by
f\colon\operatorname{Perf}(\mathcal{A})\to\operatorname{Perf}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Namely, it is enough to show that
f
is an equivalence.
Taking account of the Yoneda embedding, we see that

f
is fully faithful on the full subcategory spanned by the image of
\mathcal{A}
.
It follows that
f
is fully faithful on
the smallest stable subcategory of
\operatorname{Perf}(\mathcal{A})
, containing

\mathcal{A}
. Note that
\operatorname{Perf}(\mathcal{A})_{\mathbb{R}}

is the idempotent completion of the smallest stable subcategory
that contains the image of
\mathcal{A}
under the Yoneda embedding.
Then we conclude that
f
is an equivalence.
Similarly,
\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\rm pe}(\mathcal{B})
.
Finally, using the construction of these equivalences
and the functoriality of left adjoints
(-)_{!}
,
we identify
(\mathbb{L}F_{!})_{\mathbb{R}}
with

\mathbb{L}F_{!}
.
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

The symmetric monoidal functor
H\colon\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)

factors as

\textstyle{\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\overline{H}}

Proof.

It follows from the universal property of
\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]

and Lemma
6.11
.
∎

Composing with
\theta
, we obtain a sequence of symmetric monoidal functors

\textstyle{\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{H}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big).}

Definition 6.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
R
-linear stable
\infty
-category.
We denote by
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
the image of
\mathcal{C}
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)

under the above composite
\overline{H}\circ\theta
.
We often abuse notation by writing
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
for its image
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
. We refer to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

as Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum of
\mathcal{C}
.
If
\mathcal{A}
is a pointwise-cofibrant
\mathbb{R}
-spectral category,
we refer to the image
\overline{H}(\mathcal{A})
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
or
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

as Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum of
\mathcal{A}
.

We record our construction as a proposition:

Proposition 6.15
.

There is a sequence of symmetric monoidal functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{H}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{forget}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mod}_{R},

which to
R
-linear stable
\infty
-categories or pointwise-cofibrant

\mathbb{R}
-spectral categories assigns Hochschild homology
R
-module spectra.
In particular, for any
\infty
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}
it gives rise to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))\to\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\big(\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_R}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))}{}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{O}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}).

7
Construction

In this section, we prove Theorem
7.14
. Namely, we construct the structure of a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra on the pair of Hochschild cohomology spectrum and Hochschild homology spectrum. We maintain the notation of Section
6
.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be (possibly empty) finite disjoint unions of open interval

(0,1)
and the circle
\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}=S^{1}
.
That is,
M=(0,1)^{\sqcup m}\sqcup\big(S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n}
and
M^{\prime}=(0,1)^{\sqcup m^{\prime}}\sqcup\big(S^{1}\big)^{\sqcup n^{\prime}}
.
Let
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}(M,M^{\prime})
be
the space of rectilinear embeddings.
A rectilinear embedding
M\to M^{\prime}
is an open embedding
such that any restriction
(0,1)\to(0,1)
,

(0,1)\to S^{1}
and
S^{1}\to S^{1}
is rectilinear, see Definition
4.1
.
The topology is induced from
the compact-open topology (or parameters of rectilinear maps).

Let
\textup{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}
be the
the fibrant simplicial colored operad
whose set
\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}\big)_{\rm col}
of colors consists of (possibly empty)
finite disjoint unions of
(0,1)
and
S^{1}
.
For a finite family
\{M_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of colors
and
N\in\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}\big)_{\rm col}
,
the simplicial hom set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}}(\{M_{i}\}_{i\in I},N)
is
defined to be the singular complex
of the space
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}(\sqcup_{i\in I}M_{i},N)
of rectilinear embeddings. The composition is defined in the obvious way.
Then from Definition
4.7
,
we obtain the associated
\infty
-operad

(\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1})^{\otimes}

\to\Gamma
, which is a symmetric monoidal

\infty
-category by construction.
Informally, objects of this symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
are finite disjoint unions of
(0,1)
and
S^{1}
, and the
symmetric monoidal structure is given by disjoint union.
The empty space is a unit. The mapping spaces are spaces of rectilinear
embeddings.
Let
\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}
denote the underlying
\infty
-category.
Let
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\rm rec}\subset\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm rec}
be the full subcategory spanned by finite disjoint
unions of
(0,1)
. It is closed under taking tensor products
so that
\mathsf{Disk}^{\rm rec}_{1}
is promoted to a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category

(\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{red})^{\otimes}
(it is equivalent to an ordinary symmetric monoidal 1-category).

Remark 7.2
.

There are several variants which are equivalent to
\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}
.
Let
\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm fr}_{1}
be the
\infty
-category of framed (or oriented)
1
-manifolds without boundaries whose mapping spaces
are spaces of embeddings of framed manifolds (see, e.g.,
[
2
, Section 2]
).
The symmetric monoidal structure is given by disjoint union.
It is easy to see that
there is an equivalence
\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm fr}_{1}

as symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.
If we write
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\rm fr}

for the full subcategory of
\mathsf{Mfld}^{\rm fr}_{1}

spanned by framed
1
-disks,
it also induces an equivalence
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\rm rec}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\rm fr}

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories
.

From now on, for ease of notation, we write
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}
,
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}
,
and
\mathsf{Disk}^{\otimes}_{1}
for
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}^{\rm rec}
,
\mathsf{Disk}^{\rm rec}_{1}
,
and
\big(\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\rm rec}\big)^{\otimes}
, respectively.

We set
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}:=\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}(\mathsf{Mfld}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
.
Let
\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
be the full subcategory of
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}
that consists of
S^{1}
.
By the equivalence
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(S^{1},S^{1}\big)\simeq S^{1}
, it follows that

\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
is equivalent to
BS^{1}
, that is, the
\infty
-category
which has one object
\ast

together with the mapping space
\operatorname{Map}_{BS^{1}}(\ast,\ast)=S^{1}

endowed with the composition law induced by the multiplication of
S^{1}
.
Let
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)

which consists of those functors
h\colon\Delta^{1}\to\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}

such that
h(0)\in\mathsf{Disk}_{1}
and
h(1)\in\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
.
In other words,
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle=\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
, where the functor from
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)
to the left
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}

(resp. the right
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}
) is induced by the restriction to the source
(resp. the target).
The projection

\Delta^{0}=\{S^{1}\}\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle=BS^{1}

induces

\displaystyle(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\big\{S^{1}\big\}

\displaystyle\hphantom{(\mathsf{Disk}_1)_{/S^1}}{}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)\times_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle=\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle,

where the left categorical equivalence follows from
[
27
, Proposition 4.2.1.5]
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}
be the full subcategory of
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}
spanned by nonempty spaces (namely, the empty space is omitted from
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}
).
We set
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle=\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\times_{\mathsf{Disk}_{1}}\big(\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\big)
.
Let
\Lambda
be the cyclic category of Connes
[
10
, Section 2]
.
There is an equivalence of categories
\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
.

Proof.

This is a comparison between definitions which look different.
We first recall that objects of
\Lambda

are
(p)
for
p\geq 0
, which is denoted by
\Lambda_{p}
in
[
10
]
.
Let
\big(S^{1},p\big)
be the circle
S^{1}=\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
equipped with
the set of torsion points
\frac{1}{p+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}
.
The hom set
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda}((p),(q))
is defined to be the set of homotopy classes of monotone degree one maps
\phi\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}

such that
\phi\big(\frac{1}{p+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}\big)\subset\frac{1}{q+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}
.
We denote points
\frac{0}{p+1},\dots,\frac{p}{p+1}\in\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}

by
x^{0}_{p},\dots,x^{p}_{p}
, respectively.
Let
I_{p}^{i}=\big\{x_{p}^{i}<x<x_{p}^{i+1}|x\in[0,1)\big\}
be the open set in
S^{1}
,
where we use the obvious bijection of sets
[0,1)\simeq\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
.
In what follows, we regard the superscripts in
x_{p}^{i}
and
I_{p}^{i}

as elements of
\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
.
For
u,v\in[0,1)
,
\{u<x<v\}
means
\{x\in[0,1)\,|\,u<x<v\}

if
u<v
,
\{x\in[0,1)\,|\,u<x<1
,
0\leq x<v\}

if
u>v>0
, and
\{x\in[0,1)\,|\,u<x<1\}
if
u>v=0
.
Given
(p)\in\Lambda
, we think of
j_{p}\colon(\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z})\backslash\big(\frac{1}{p+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}\big)=\big(I_{p}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{p}^{p}\big)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}=S^{1}
as an object of
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
.
We fix
I^{i}_{p}\simeq(0,1)
such that
I_{p}^{i}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}

is equivalent to
(0,1)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{R}\to\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}
.
We write
J(p)
for it. We note that every object of
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle

is equivalent to
J(p)
for some
p\geq 0
.
Since each component of
\operatorname{Map}_{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}}\big((0,1)^{\sqcup p+1},S^{1}\big)
is naturally
equivalent to
S^{1}
,
the computation of mapping spaces shows that
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
is
equivalent to the nerve of a
1
-category.
We may and will abuse notation by identifying
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle

with its homotopy category.
Suppose that we are given a monotone degree one map
\phi\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}

such that
\phi\big(\frac{1}{p+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}\big)\subset\frac{1}{q+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}
.
Assume that
p\geq 1
and
\phi\big(j_{p}\big(I_{p}^{i}\big)\big)
is not a one-point space.
Let
\sigma(i,\phi)
be
an element of
\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
such that

x_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)}=\phi\big(x_{p}^{i}\big)
.
Consider a rectilinear embedding

\displaystyle\iota_{i,\phi}\colon\ I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)}\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)+1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i+1,\phi)-1}\simeq(0,1)\sqcup\dots\sqcup(0,1)\hookrightarrow(0,1)\simeq I^{i}_{p}

such that
\iota_{i,\phi}\big(I^{\sigma(i,\phi)}_{q}\big)<\dots<\iota_{i,\phi}\big(I_{q}^{\sigma(i+1,\phi)-1}\big)
in
(0,1)
(we here abuse notation: for two subsets
S
,
T\subset(0,1)
,
S<T
if
s<t
for any pair
(s,t)\in S\times T
).
When
p=0
, we define
\iota_{i,\phi}

by replacing
I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)}\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)+1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i+1,\phi)-1}
by
I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)}\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)+1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{q}^{\sigma(i,\phi)-1}
.
Such a rectilinear embedding is unique up to equivalences.
Given
\phi\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda}((p),(q))
,
we define the class of a map
\phi^{*}\colon J(q)\to J(p)
in
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle

such that the fiber of the induced morphism

I_{q}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{q}^{q}\to I_{p}^{0}\sqcup\dots\sqcup I_{p}^{p}

over the connected component
I_{p}^{i}

is (equivalent to)
\iota_{i,\phi}
if
\phi\big(I_{p}^{i}\big)
is not a one-point space,
and if otherwise there is no component which maps to
I^{i}_{p}
.
Notice that such a class is unique.
It is routine
to check that the assignments
(p)\mapsto J(p)
and
\phi\mapsto\phi^{*}

determine a categorical equivalence
\Lambda^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
,
where the target is identified with the homotopy category.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\pi\colon\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
be the projection.
It is a groupoid completion of
\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
6.9
(i) and
7.3
,
there is
a groupoid completion
c\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle

\to BS^{1}
.
Thus, by the universal property, there is a canonical morphism
from
c\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}

to
\pi\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle

in
(\operatorname{Cat}_{\infty})_{\Lambda^{\rm op}/}
.
It will suffice to show that the induced morphism

g\colon BS^{1}\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\simeq BS^{1}
is an equivalence, equivalently, it is induced by an equivalence
S^{1}\to S^{1}
as
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-monoid spaces.
To this end, assume that

g\colon BS^{1}\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\simeq BS^{1}
is
induced by a map
S^{1}\to S^{1}
of degree
n
,
where
|n|=p+1
,
p>0
. We will show that this gives rise to
a contradiction.
The automorphism group of
(p)

in
\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
is

\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
so that there is the functor

h\colon B\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}\to BS^{1}
induced by
\pi
.
By the factorization
\Lambda^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c}}{{\to}}BS^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\simeq BS^{1}

and our assumption,
h\colon B\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}\to BS^{1}

factors through the canonical morphism
\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}
.
Thus, the fiber product of
B\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h}}{{\to}}BS^{1}\leftarrow\Delta^{0}=*

is
B(\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z})
.
On the other hand, the space/
\infty
-groupoid
in

(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\times_{\langle S^{1}\rangle}\Delta^{0}

spanned by
J(p)\colon(0,1)^{\sqcup p+1}

\to S^{1}
(obtained by discarding non-invertible morphisms) is equivalent to
B\mathbf{Z}
.
It gives rise to a contradiction
B(\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z})\simeq B\mathbf{Z}
.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

There is another category relevant to the cyclic category:
the paracyclic category

\Lambda_{\infty}
. Let us recall the definition of the paracyclic category.
We follow
[
14
]
. The set of objects of
\Lambda_{\infty}

is
\{(0)_{\infty},(1)_{\infty},\dots,(p)_{\infty},\dots\}_{p\geq 0}
.
The hom set
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda_{\infty}}((p)_{\infty},(q)_{\infty})

is defined to be the set of monotonically increasing maps
f\colon\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{Z}
such that

f(i+k(p+1))=f(i)+k(q+1)
for any
k\in\mathbf{Z}
.
We define a functor
\Lambda_{\infty}\to\Lambda

which carries
(p)_{\infty}
to
(p)
.
The map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda_{\infty}}((p)_{\infty},(q)_{\infty})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda}((p),(q))

carries
f
to
\phi_{f}\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
,
where
\phi_{f}
is the class of a map such that

\phi_{f}(x_{p}^{i})=x_{q}^{f(i)}\in\frac{1}{q+1}\mathbf{Z}/\mathbf{Z}

for
i\in\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
.
Here, we regard
f(i)
as belonging to
\mathbf{Z}/(q+1)\mathbf{Z}
.
This determines a functor
\Lambda_{\infty}\to\Lambda
.
Unwinding the definition of
\Lambda_{\infty}\to\Lambda
,
we see that it is a (homotopy)
quotient morphism
\Lambda_{\infty}\to\Lambda_{\infty}/B\mathbf{Z}\simeq\Lambda

that comes from
a free action of
B\mathbf{Z}
on
\Lambda_{\infty}
.
This free action of
B\mathbf{Z}
is determined by the natural equivalence from the identity functor
\textup{id}_{\Lambda_{\infty}}
to itself such that for any
p\geq 0
,
the induced map

(p)_{\infty}\to(p)_{\infty}
is the map
i\mapsto i+p+1

(see
[
14
]
for details).
The paracyclic category also has a geometric description.
From the proof of Lemma
7.6
below,

\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\infty}
is (left) cofinal
so that it induces an equivalence between their groupoid completions.
Since the groupoid completion of
\Delta^{\rm op}
is contractible (note
that it’s
sifted),
the groupoid completion
\overline{\Lambda}_{\infty}
of
\Lambda_{\infty}

is a contractible space.
It follows that the geometric realization of
\Lambda
is equivalent to
BB\mathbf{Z}=BS^{1}

(see also Lemma
6.9
(i)).
The composition with the opposite functor
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle=BS^{1}
factors through the groupoid completion
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\overline{\Lambda}_{\infty}^{\rm op}\simeq\Delta^{0}
.
Consequently, we have the induced functor

\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\times_{\langle S^{1}\rangle}\Delta^{0}
. This is an equivalence. Clearly, it is essentially surjective.
The map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}}((q)_{\infty},(p)_{\infty})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda^{\rm op}}((q),(p))

is a homotopy quotient map that comes from a free action of
\mathbf{Z}
.
We see that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}}((q)_{\infty},(p)_{\infty})

is a (homotopy) fiber product
\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}}((q)_{\infty},(p)_{\infty})/\mathbf{Z}\times_{B\mathbf{Z}}\Delta^{0}\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\Lambda^{\rm op}}((q),(p))\times_{B\mathbf{Z}}\Delta^{0}
.
It follows that
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}
is fully faithful.
Hence
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category.
Let
\Lambda_{\infty}
be the paracyclic category, see
[
14
]
or Remark
7.5
.
Let
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\simeq\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\simeq\Lambda^{\rm op}

be the natural functor.
Let
f\colon\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\mathcal{C}
and
g\colon\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\mathcal{C}
be
functors and let
f\to\pi\circ g
be a natural transformation.
Then
g
is a left Kan extension of
f
along
\pi\colon\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
if and only if

\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}\simeq\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}\mathcal{C}
determines
a colimit of the composite
\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}\mathcal{C}
.

Moreover, the inclusion
\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\to(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}

is cofinal. Therefore, if we suppose that

f\colon\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\mathcal{C}
is the restriction of a functor
\tilde{f}\colon\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\mathcal{C}
,
the above condition
that
g
is a left Kan extension of
f

is also equivalent to the condition that

\Delta^{0}\to BS^{1}\to\mathcal{C}
determines
a colimit of the composite
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{f}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{C}
,
where the first arrow
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
is the natural functor.

Proof.

There is a faithful functor
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}_{\infty}
that is (left) cofinal
[
26
, Proposition 4.2.8]
: it is the same as the functor
m
in Remark
7.7
.
Thus, for any paracyclic object
F\colon\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\mathcal{C}
,
the canonical morphism
\operatorname{colim}_{[p]\in\Delta^{\rm op}}F([p])\to\operatorname{colim}_{(p)_{\infty}\in\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}}F((p)_{\infty})
is an equivalence.
Our first assertion now follows from this fact and Lemma
6.9
(ii).

To prove the second assertion, it will suffice to prove
that
\big(\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\big)_{e/}=\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}

\times_{(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}((\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}})_{e/}

is weakly contractible, where
e
is the map
e\colon\phi\to S^{1}

from the empty space to
S^{1}
.
Since
e
is an initial object in
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
, we are reduced to
proving
that
\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}

\simeq\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}
is weakly contractible.
By Quillen’s theorem A,
it is clear because

\Delta^{\rm op}
is weakly contractible and
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}
is (left) cofinal (it follows also from the fact that
\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}
is sifted).
∎

Remark 7.7
.

We have the following commutative diagram of categories:

\textstyle{\big\{(0,1)\to S^{1}\big\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\big(\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\big)_{(0,1)\to S^{1}/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
forget
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\{[0]\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{\rm op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Lambda^{\rm op}.}

It is straightforward to observe that the composite

\big(\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\big)_{(0,1)\to S^{1}/}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}
is
a faithful and essentially surjective functor
whose image is
\Delta^{\rm op}
contained in
\Lambda^{\rm op}
.

Let
\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}
be the
colored simplicial full suboperad of
\textup{Mfld}^{\rm rec}_{1}
(Definition
7.1
)
whose set
\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\rm col}
of colors is

\big\{(0,1),S^{1}\big\}
.
Namely, for
M_{i},N\in\{(0,1),S^{1}\}
,
the simplicial Hom set
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}}(\{M_{i}\}_{i\in I},N)

is the singular complex
of the space
\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}(\sqcup_{i\in I}M_{i},N)
of rectilinear embeddings. The superscript “
ic
” stands for the “interval” and the “circle”.
Let
\textup{Disk}_{1}
be the full suboperad
whose set of colors is
\{(0,1)\}
.
Notice that
\textup{Disk}_{1}
is identical with
\textup{E}_{1}
in Section
4
.
From Definition
4.7
,
we obtain the associated
\infty
-operad

\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
of simplicial categories
constructed from
\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}
.
Also, to
\textup{E}_{1}=\textup{Disk}_{1}\subset\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}

we associate
(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}=(\textup{Disk}_{1})_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
.

Construction 7.8
.

We define

\rho\colon(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}\times\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{DCyl}_{\Delta}

which makes the following diagram commute

\textstyle{(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}\times\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\operatorname{DCyl}_{\Delta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\times\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wedge}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}.}

Here
\operatorname{DCyl}_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
is the map of simplicial categories
associated to
\operatorname{DCyl}
.
The lower horizontal arrow
\wedge\colon\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\times\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
that sends
a pair
(\langle m\rangle,\langle n\rangle)
to
\langle mn\rangle

is defined in
[
25
, Notation 2.2.5.1]
.
Let
X=(\langle m\rangle,(L_{1},\dots,L_{m}))
be an object
of
(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}
, where
L_{s}=(0,1)
for each
1\leq s\leq m
.
Let
Y=(\langle n\rangle,(M_{1},\dots,M_{n}))
be an object of
\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\Delta}

where
M_{t}
is either
(0,1)
or
S^{1}
for each
1\leq t\leq n
.
We define
\rho((X,Y))
to be

(\langle mn\rangle,(L_{s}\times M_{t}))_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq s\leq m\\
1\leq t\leq n\end{subarray}}
,
where we abuse notation
by the identifications
(0,1)^{2}=D
and
(0,1)\times S^{1}=C
, see Definition
4.4
.

X^{\prime}=(\langle m^{\prime}\rangle,(L^{\prime}_{1},\dots,L^{\prime}_{m}))
be another object
of
(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}
.
Let
Y^{\prime}=(\langle n^{\prime}\rangle,(M^{\prime}_{1},\dots,M^{\prime}_{n}))

be another object of
(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1})_{\Delta}
.
Given a pair of morphisms
f\colon\langle m\rangle\to\langle m^{\prime}\rangle

and
g\colon\langle n\rangle\to\langle n^{\prime}\rangle
,
we define a map

\textstyle{\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\sqcup_{s\in f^{-1}(s^{\prime})}L_{s},L_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}\big)\times\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\sqcup_{t\in g^{-1}(t^{\prime})}M_{t},M_{t^{\prime}}^{\prime}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\textup{Emb}^{\rm rec}\big(\sqcup_{(s,t)\in f^{-1}(s^{\prime})\times g^{-1}(t^{\prime})}L_{s}\times M_{t},L_{s^{\prime}}^{\prime}\times M_{t^{\prime}}^{\prime}\big)}

that sends
(\phi,\psi)
to
\phi\times\psi
.
Taking the product parameterized by
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime})
with
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq m^{\prime}
,
1\leq t^{\prime}\leq n^{\prime}
and taking simplicial
nerves, we obtain morphisms of hom simplicial sets.
It gives rise to a functor
\rho\colon(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}\times\big(\textup{Mfld}^{\rm ic}_{1}\big)_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{DCyl}_{\Delta}
which makes the diagram commute.
This construction is a natural extension of that in
[
25
, Construction 5.1.2.1]
:
Let
\langle D\rangle\subset\operatorname{DCyl}
be the full suboperad
whose set of colors is
\{D\}
.
Let
\langle D\rangle_{\Delta}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
be the correpsonding
simplicial full subcategory of
\operatorname{DCyl}_{\Delta}
.
Then the restriction of
\rho
induces

(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}\times(\textup{E}_{1})_{\Delta}\to\langle D\rangle_{\Delta}

lying over
\wedge\colon\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\times\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}\to\operatorname{Fin}_{\ast}
,
which is defined in
loc. cit
.

Let
\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}
be the simplicial nerve of
\big(\textup{Mfld}_{1}^{\rm ic}\big)_{\Delta}
.
The simplicial nerves of the above diagrams
give rise to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathbf{DCyl},}

which lies over
\wedge\colon\Gamma\times\Gamma\to\Gamma
.
We abuse notation by writing
\rho
for the associated map.

Given an
\infty
-operad
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
,
there exist a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Env}({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes})\to\Gamma
and a map
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Env}\big({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\big)

of
\infty
-operads such that
for any symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
,
the composition induces
a categorical equivalence
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\operatorname{Env}\big({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\big),\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\big)
, see
[
25
, Section 2.2.4]
.
Here
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\operatorname{Env}\big({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\big),\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}\big)
denotes
the
\infty
-category of symmetric monoidal functors.
We shall refer to
\operatorname{Env}\big({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\big)

as the symmetric monoidal envelope of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}

(in
loc. cit.
, it is referred to as the
\Gamma
-monoidal envelope).
Through the categorical equivalence, for a map of
\infty
-operads

f\colon{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
, there exists a symmetric monoidal functor

\tilde{f}\colon\operatorname{Env}\big({\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\big)\to\mathcal{D}^{\otimes}
which is unique up
to a contractible space of choices. We refer to
\tilde{f}

as a symmetric monoidal functor that corresponds to
f
.
Let
\operatorname{Oper}_{\infty}
be the
\infty
-category of (small)
\infty
-operads
[
25
, Section 2.1.4]

and let
\operatorname{Cat}_{\infty}^{\otimes}
be the
\infty
-category of (small)
symmetric monoidal

\infty
-categories whose morphisms are symmetric monoidal functors.
Then the construction of symmetric monoidal envelopes gives
a left adjoint
\operatorname{Oper}_{\infty}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\infty}^{\otimes}
of the canonical functor
\operatorname{Cat}_{\infty}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Oper}_{\infty}
.
Here are some examples. The symmetric monoidal envelope
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{1}^{\otimes}

of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}
as symmetric monoidal

\infty
-categories
.
Similarly, a symmetric monoidal envelope
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}

of
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}

is equivalent to the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathsf{Disk}_{2}^{\otimes}

of (possibly empty) finite disjoint unions of
(0,1)^{2}
defined
as in the case of
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}
: mapping spaces
are spaces of rectilinear embeddings, and the tensor product is
again given by disjoint union.
Another quick example of symmetric monoidal envelopes
is
\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}^{\otimes}
.

Construction 7.9
.

Let
q\colon\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.
Let
p\colon\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be a symmetric monoidal

\infty
-category (resp. an
\infty
-operad).
We construct a symmetric monoidal structure on the
\infty
-category

\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
of symmetric monoidal functors
(resp. the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)

of algebra objects),
see
[
25
, Section 3.2.4]
for more details of the case of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
.
We define a map
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}\to\Gamma

(resp.
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)\to\Gamma
)
by the universal property that for any
\alpha\colon K\to\Gamma
,
the set of morphisms
K\to\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}

over
\Gamma
(resp.
K\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)

over
\Gamma
)
is defined to be the set of morphisms

f\colon K\times\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
such that

(i)

the diagram

\textstyle{K\times\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha,\textup{id})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\Gamma\times\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma}

commutes; here the lower horizontal arrow is induced by
\wedge\colon\Gamma\times\Gamma\to\Gamma
,

(ii)

for any vertex
k
of
K
and any
p
-coCartesian edge
\phi

in
\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}
,
f(k,\phi)
is a
q
-coCartesian edge
(resp. for any vertex
k
of
K
and any inert morphism
\phi

in
\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}
,
f(k,\phi)
is an inert morphism in
\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
).

The morphism
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)\to\Gamma

is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category whose underlying
\infty
-category
is
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}}(\mathcal{C})
, cf.
[
25
, Proposition 3.2.4.3]
.
Similarly,
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}\to\Gamma

is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category whose underlying
\infty
-category
is
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
: the proof of
[
25
, Proposition 3.2.4.3]

based on the theory of categorical patterns
can also be applied to
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}
.
An edge
\Delta^{1}\to\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{P}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}

is a coCartesian edge if and only if for any
X\in\mathcal{P}
,
the
composite
\Delta^{1}\times\{X\}\subset\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
determines a
q
-coCartesian edge (this means that the tensor product
F\otimes G

of two symmetric monoidal functors

F\colon\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}
and
G\colon\mathcal{P}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}

is informally given by objectwise tensor products

(F\otimes G)(X)=F(X)\otimes G(X)
).

Let
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma
be an
\infty
-operad and let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma

be the symmetric monoidal envelope. The composition with the inclusion
{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes}
induces a map over
\Gamma

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)

that preserves coCartesian edges, namely, it is a symmetric monoidal functor.
Since the underlying functor is an equivalence
[
25
, Proposition 2.2.4.9]
,
it gives rise to a symmetric monoidal equivalence.
That is, the categorical equivalence
\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}^{\otimes},\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{{\mathcal{O}}^{\otimes}}\big(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes}\big)
is promoted to a symmetric monoidal equivalence in the natural way.

Let
A
be an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
By definition,
it is a map of
\infty
-operads
A\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

over
\Gamma
. We denote by

\displaystyle i_{!}(A)\colon\ \mathbf{DCyl}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

the operadic left Kan extension of
A
along the inclusion
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{DCyl}
. If we think of the color
S^{1}
as an object in the fiber
(\mathbf{Mfld}_{1})_{\langle 1\rangle}
of
\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\Gamma
over
\langle 1\rangle
,
the full subcategory
\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
spanned by
S^{1}

determines the inclusion
\iota\colon BS^{1}\simeq\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{Mfld}_{1})_{\langle 1\rangle}\subset\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}
.
Then we have the following diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textup{id}\times\iota}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathbf{DCyl}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{!}(A)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\times\{\langle 1\rangle\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\times\Gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wedge}
\textstyle{\Gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
id
\textstyle{\Gamma.}

See Construction
7.8
for
\rho
.
The composite
\Gamma\simeq\Gamma\times\{\langle 1\rangle\}\to\Gamma
of lower horizontal arrows is the identity map.
Note that the composite
\rho\circ(\textup{id}\times\iota)\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{DCyl}
is the map

z\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times BS^{1}\to\mathbf{Cyl}\subset\mathbf{DCyl}

which was defined in the discussion before Proposition
4.16
.
Taking into account the above diagram, Proposition
4.16
and Lemma
4.14
,
we have the induced functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big),

and we write
i_{!}(A)_{C}
for the image of
i_{!}(A)
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.

Remark 7.10
.

The image of
C=(0,1)\times S^{1}
under
i_{!}(A)\colon\mathbf{DCyl}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

can be viewed as the factorization homology
\int_{C}A
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
in this context, cf.
[
2
,
25
]
.

We continue to suppose that
A
is an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Let us consider the Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum of
A

defined as follows.
Let
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
be the category of associative algebra objects of
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
,
where
\mathbb{R}
be a (cofibrant) commutative symmetric ring spectrum that
represents
R
, and
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})
spanned by cofibrant objects (cf. Section
6
).
The ordinary category
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
admits
a symmetric monoidal structure given by
\mathbb{A}\otimes\mathbb{B}=\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{B}
.
Define a symmetric monoidal functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}
which carries
\mathbb{A}
to
B\mathbb{A}
, where
B\mathbb{A}
is the

\mathbb{R}
-spectral category having one object
\ast
with
the morphism spectrum
\mathbb{A}=B\mathbb{A}(\ast,\ast)
.
We define
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A})
to be the Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum of
B\mathbb{A}
.
Namely, we use canonical symmetric monoidal functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{H}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big),

see Corollary
6.13
.
By inverting weak equivalences we obtain symmetric monoidal functors

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]\to\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\big[M^{-1}\big]\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big),

see Example
6.4
for the first symmetric equivalence.
This functor sends
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
.
Note that there is
a canonical categorical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

that follows from the trivial fibration
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
and the equivalence
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\otimes\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}
(Dunn additivity theorem).
Thus, we have the induced functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big).

Given
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
,
we define
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
to be the image of
A
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.

Proposition 7.11
.

There is a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}i_{!}(A)_{C}

in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.

Let us consider
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\left(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B(-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)
, see Definition
6.8
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}
.
We write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Lambda}(-)
for the composite.
Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
be the symmetric monoidal envelope
of
\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
.
The equivalence
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\operatorname{As}
induces

\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
.
There is a canonical symmetric monoidal equivalence

\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)^{\otimes}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
, see Construction
7.9
.
We write
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}
for the inclusion of the fiber of the coCartesin fibration
\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\Gamma

over
\langle 1\rangle
.
Using Lemma
7.3
, we have

\displaystyle\xi\colon\ \Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\langle S^{1}\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{forget}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}.

The composition with
\xi

induces

\displaystyle g\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big),

which is a symmetric monoidal functor.

Lemma 7.12
.

The functor
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Lambda}(-)\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
can be identified with

\displaystyle g\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)

in the natural way.
In particular,

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)
induced by
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}
can be identified with

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)\big[W^{-1}\big]\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)

induced by the composition with
\xi
.

Proof.

We use the notation in Lemma
7.3
. Let
\phi_{p-1,i}\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
be a monotone degree one map which we think of as a morphism
(p-1)\to(p)
in

\Lambda

such that

\phi_{p-1,i}\big(x^{k}_{p-1}\big)=x_{p}^{k}
for
0\leq k\leq i-1
,
and
\phi_{p-1,i}\big(x^{k}_{p-1}\big)=x_{p}^{k+1}
for
i\leq k\leq p-1

(in particular,
x^{i}_{p}
does not lie in the image of
\big\{x_{p}^{k}\big\}_{0\leq k\leq p-1}
).
Let
\mathbb{A}
be an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big)
.
Consider the composition
\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
, where the final map is a symmetric monoidal
functor corresponding to
\mathbb{A}
.
By inspection, if
\{\phi_{p-1,i}\}_{0\leq i\leq p}

are regarded as morphisms
(p)\to(p-1)
in
\Lambda^{\rm op}
,
their images in
\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}
define
(p+1)
degeneracy maps
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+1}\to\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}

given by the multiplication
\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}
.
Let
\psi_{p,i}\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}
be a monotone degree one map which we think of as a morphism

(p)\to(p-1)

such that
\psi_{p,i}\big(x^{k}_{p}\big)=x_{p-1}^{k}
for
k<i+1
,
and
\psi_{p,i}\big(x^{k}_{p}\big)=x^{k-1}_{p-1}
for
k\geq i+1
.
As in the case of
\{\phi_{p-1,i}\}_{0\leq i\leq p}
,
these maps give rise to
p
face maps
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\to\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+1}
given by the unit
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{A}
.
Consider the rotation
r_{p}\colon S^{1}\to S^{1}

which sends
x^{k}_{p}
to
x^{k+1}_{p}
for
k\in\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
.
We regard
r_{p}
as an isomorphism
(p)\to(p)
.
It yields the action of
\mathbf{Z}/(p+1)\mathbf{Z}
on
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+1}
given by the cyclic permutation of factors.
It is straightforward to check that these maps constitute a cyclic object
that coincides with the cyclic object obtained from
B\mathbb{A}
in
Definition
6.8
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.11
.

Taking into account Lemma
7.12
and

\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
,
for an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra
A
, we consider
the image of
A
under

\displaystyle h\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}\big)\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big),

where the right functor is induced by the composition with
\xi\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
.
We abuse notation by writing
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\Lambda}_{\bullet}(A)
for the image of
A

under
h
.
In the following discussion, we will use the canonical identification

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}(-)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}(-)
which comes from the canonical equivalence
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}
of
\infty
-operads.
Let us consider

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_2}(\operatorname{Mod}_R)}{}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big).

The second equivalence follows from Construction
7.9
,
and the third functor is induced by
\xi\colon

\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
.
The composition is identified with
h
via the equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\simeq\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big).

Let
A_{\flat}\colon\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

be a map of
\infty
-operads that corresponds to
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}\big)\big)
.
We let
\widetilde{A}_{\flat}\colon\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
be a symmetric monoidal functor from the symmetric monoidal envelope
\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
that corresponds to
A_{\flat}
(namely, the composite
\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
is equivalent to
A_{\flat}
).
Observe that the composite
\Lambda^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{A}_{\flat}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

gives rise to a functor
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

which is equivalent to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\Lambda}_{\bullet}(A)
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
Note that
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
is defined to be the image of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\Lambda}_{\bullet}(A)
under the functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
induced by the symmetric monoidal functor

L\colon\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
in Lemma
6.10
.
Here
L
is a left adjoint of the symmetric monoidal
functor
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
induced
by the composition with
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}
.
Thus,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
can also be regarded as the image under the (left adjoint) functor
\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\allowbreak\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)
given by left Kan extensions along
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}
.
Consequently,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\colon BS^{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
is
a left Kan extension of
\Lambda^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\xi}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{A}_{\flat}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

along
\Lambda^{\rm op}\to BS^{1}
.
Next, we
let
\widehat{A}_{\flat}\colon\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
be an operadic left Kan extension of
A_{\flat}\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Let
A^{\prime}_{\flat}\colon\mathsf{Mfld}^{\otimes}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

be a symmetric monoidal functor which corresponds to
\widehat{A}_{\flat}
.
The composite

\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathsf{Mfld}^{\otimes}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

is equivalent to
\widetilde{A}_{\flat}\colon\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Consider the diagram of
\infty
-categories:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Disk}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})}
\textstyle{\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The upper left horizontal arrow is induced by the restriction to the source.
The left vertical arrow is induced by the restriction to the target.
The upper right arrow is the underlying functor of
\widetilde{A}_{\flat}
.
The arrow
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
is the underlying functor
of
A^{\prime}_{\flat}
. The right triangle commutes whereas the left square does not
commute (but it admits a canonical natural transformation
induced by the evaluation map
\Delta^{1}\times\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{1},\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\big)\to\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}
).
The functor
\mathsf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

carries
S^{1}
to
\operatorname{colim}_{U\to S^{1}\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)

which means a colimit of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times_{\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}}\big(\mathbf{Mfld}^{\textup{act}}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\simeq(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{A}_{\flat}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
By Lemma
7.6
, the composite

\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

is a left Kan extension of
\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Since
