\Lambda^{\rm op}\simeq\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}/\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\simeq BS^{1}
is a groupoid
completion by Lemma
7.4
,
it follows that the composite

BS^{1}\simeq\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

is equivalent to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
.
In other words,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
is equivalent to
BS^{1}\simeq\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{A}_{\flat}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.

Next, we relate
i_{!}(A)_{C}
with

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\colon BS^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widehat{A}_{\flat}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
For this purpose, we consider the following setting.
Let
\mathbf{DCyl}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}}
be a symmetric monoidal
envelope of
\mathbf{DCyl}
.
Composing with maps into symmetric monoidal envelopes,
we have the left diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}},}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}}\big)}

lying over
\wedge\colon\Gamma\times\Gamma\to\Gamma
.
Then by the universal property of the tensor product
of
\infty
-operads, it induces the right commutative diagram consisting of maps of
\infty
-operads
over
\Gamma
, where
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}\big)

and
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}}\big)
are symmetric monoidal

\infty
-categories (defined over
\Gamma
), and the right vertical arrow
is a symmetric monoidal (fully faithful) functor.
In the following discussion, we replace
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

by an arbitrary symmetric monoidal presentable
\infty
-category

\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

whose tensor product
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}

preserves small colimits separately in each variable.
The example of
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
we keep in mind is
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
.
Let
A
be an
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra object in
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
, that is,
a map
A\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
of
\infty
-operads over
\Gamma
.
The inclusion
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{DCyl}

gives rise to the adjoint pair
i_{!}\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\mathcal{M})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathcal{M})\,{\colon}\!i^{*}
.
Let
\widetilde{A}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

and
\widetilde{i_{!}(A)}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}

be symmetric monoidal functors that correspond to
A
and
i_{!}(A)
, respectively.
We have the diagram of
\infty
-operads

\textstyle{\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{2}^{\otimes}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\widetilde{A})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{DCyl}}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\widetilde{i_{!}(A)})}

As before,
we let
A_{\flat}\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
be the composite
of top horizontal arrows,
which amounts to
A\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
since
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\otimes\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\simeq\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}
.
Let
\hat{A}_{\flat}\colon\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)

be the operadic left Kan extension of
A_{\flat}
along
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}
.
Let
\hat{A}_{\sharp}\colon\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
be
the composite.
We note that
i_{!}(A)_{C}
is equivalent to
BS^{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
determined by the composite
BS^{1}\simeq\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\hookrightarrow}}\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{A}_{\sharp}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}^{\otimes}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.
The universal property
[
25
, Proposition 3.1.3.2]
induces
a canonical morphism
\hat{A_{\flat}}\to\hat{A}_{\sharp}
.
It suffices to
prove that the restriction
\hat{A_{\flat}}|_{\langle S^{1}\rangle}\to\hat{A}_{\sharp}|_{\langle S^{1}\rangle}
to
\big\langle S^{1}\big\rangle
is an equivalence. (It gives rise to an equivalence

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq i_{!}(A)_{C}

in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)
.)
To this end, it is enough to show the following lemma, which completes
the proof of Proposition
7.11
.
∎

Lemma 7.13
.

The induced morphism
\hat{A}_{\flat}\big(S^{1}\big)\to\hat{A}_{\sharp}\big(S^{1}\big)
is an equivalence
in the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.

Proof.

We first consider
\hat{A}_{\flat}\colon\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Alg}^{\otimes}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.
The operadic left Kan extension gives

\hat{A}_{\flat}\big(S^{1}\big)=\operatorname{colim}_{U\to S^{1}\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)
.
Here,
\operatorname{colim}_{U\to S^{1}\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)

means a colimit of
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\times

{}_{\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}}(\mathbf{Mfld}_{1})^{\textup{act}}_{/S^{1}}\simeq(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{A}_{\flat}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)
.
By
[
2
, Corollary 3.22]
or
[
25
, Proposition 5.5.2.15]
,
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
is sifted
(strictly speaking, in the statement in
[
25
, Proposition 5.5.2.15]
,
mapping spaces between disks are spaces of (not necessarily rectilinear)
open embeddings, but the overcategory
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
is equivalent to
a nonrectilinear version in
loc. cit.
).
The forgetful functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\mathcal{M}

preserves sifted colimits.
Consequently, the image of
\hat{A}_{\flat}\big(S^{1}\big)
in
\mathcal{M}

is a colimit of
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\big)\to\mathcal{M}
.
Given a topological
r
-manifold
T
,
we let
\textup{Disj}(T)
be the poset that consists of open sets
U\subset T

such that
U
is homeomorphic to a finite disjoint union of
(0,1)^{r}
.
We think of
\textup{Disj}(T)
as a category.
Then
according to
[
25
, Proposition 5.5.2.13]
,
the natural functor
\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\to(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
is left cofinal.
Thus,
\operatorname{colim}_{U\in\textup{Disj}(S^{1})}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{U\to S^{1}\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)
in
\mathcal{M}
, where

\operatorname{colim}_{U\in\textup{Disj}(S^{1})}A_{\flat}(U)
is a colimit of
\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\to(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}\to\mathcal{M}

(this equivalence also follows from the fact that

(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
is obtained from
\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)
by localizing
with respect to those inclusions
U\subset U^{\prime}
that are isotopic to
an isomorphism
[
2
, Proposition 2.19]
).

Next, we consider the image of
\hat{A}_{\sharp}\big(S^{1}\big)
in
\mathcal{M}
.
If
\mathsf{Disk}_{2}
denotes the underlying
\infty
-category of
\mathsf{Disk}_{2}^{\otimes}

and we set
C=(0,1)\times S^{1}
, then
the image of
C
in
\mathcal{M}
under the operadic left Kan extension

i_{!}(A)
is
\operatorname{colim}_{V\to C\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{2})_{/C}}A(V)
, that is,
a colimit of
\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\times_{\mathbf{DCyl}}(\mathbf{DCyl})^{\textup{act}}_{/C}\simeq(\mathsf{Disk}_{2})_{/C}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{A}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}
, where the latter functor
is the underlying functor of the induced symmetric monoidal functor

\widetilde{A}\colon\mathsf{Disk}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}
(we abuse notation).

Let
\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)
be
the full subcategory (poset) of
\textup{Disj}(C)
spanned by those open sets
V\subset C

such that
V
is the image of a rectilinear embedding,
and the composite
V\hookrightarrow C=(0,1)\times S^{1}

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{pr}}}{{\to}}S^{1}

is not surjective.
By applying the argument of
[
25
, Proposition 5.5.2.13]

to
\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)\to(\mathsf{Disk}_{2})_{/C}
,
we see that
\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)\to(\mathsf{Disk}_{2})_{/C}
is left cofinal.
It follows that there
is a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{colim}_{V\in\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\widetilde{A}(V)\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{V\to C\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{2})_{/C}}\widetilde{A}(V)
.

To prove that
\hat{A}_{\flat}\big(S^{1}\big)\to\hat{A}_{\sharp}\big(S^{1}\big)
is an equivalence,
it is enough to show that

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{U\in\textup{Disj}(S^{1})}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)\to\operatorname{colim}_{V\in\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\widetilde{A}(V)

is an equivalence in
\mathcal{M}
.
Unwinding the definition,
this morphism is the composite of

\displaystyle\operatorname{colim}_{U\in\textup{Disj}(S^{1})}\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)\to\operatorname{colim}_{(0,1)\times U\in\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\widetilde{A}((0,1)\times U)\to\operatorname{colim}_{V\in\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\widetilde{A}(V),

where the right arrow
is induced by the universal property of the colimit,
and the left arrow is an equivalence because
\widetilde{A}_{\flat}(U)\simeq\widetilde{A}((0,1)\times U)
.
To see that the right arrow is an equivalence,
it will suffice to prove that
\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\to\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)

that sends
U
to
(0,1)\times U
is left cofinal:
for any
V\in\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)
, the category

\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\times_{\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)_{V/}

is weakly contractible.
Consider the image
W
of
V
under the projection

(0,1)\times S^{1}\to S^{1}
. Then
W
belongs to

\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)
since

V\hookrightarrow C\to S^{1}
is not surjective. It follows that
\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\times_{\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)_{V/}
has an initial object
so that the opposite category is filtered. Thus, by
[
27
, Proposition 5.5.8.7]
,

\textup{Disj}\big(S^{1}\big)\times_{\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)}\textup{Disj}^{\rm rec}(C)_{V/}

is weakly contractible as desired.
∎

Theorem 7.14
.

Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a small
R
-linear stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-category, that is, an object of
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}=\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t})
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
be the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module spectrum
which belongs to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}\!(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
, see Definitions
5.3
and
5.4
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
be the Hochschild homology
R
-module
spectrum which lies in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
, see Definition
6.14
.
Then
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
is promoted to
an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
in a natural way.

Remark 7.15
.

By Corollary
4.21
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))}\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_R)}{}\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\times\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big).

Theorem
7.14
means that we can obtain
an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
which “lies over”
the pair
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\times\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
.

The proof proceeds in Construction
7.16
, Proposition
7.17
and Construction
7.18
.

Construction 7.16
.

We write
\mathcal{D}
for the
\operatorname{Ind}
-category

\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
, which is an
R
-linear compactly generated
stable
\infty
-category.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})
be the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module
spectrum.
Recall that
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})=E(\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}(\mathcal{D}))
.
The counit map of the adjunction

I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big){\colon}\!E

induces to an associative monoidal functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}=\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{D}),

that is, a morphism in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
,
where
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}:=\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
is the associative monoidal
\infty
-category of right modules of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
.
According to
[
25
, Corollary 4.7.1.40]
,
the associative monoidal
\infty
-category

\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{D})

naturally acts on
\mathcal{D}
. More precisely,

\mathcal{D}
is a left module of
\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{D})
,
that is, an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{D})}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
(this action is universal in an appropriate sense, cf.
[
25
, Section 4.7.1]
).
Then the associative monoidal functor
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}}_{R}^{\otimes}(\mathcal{D})

induces a left
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}
-module
structure on
\mathcal{D}
. That is,
\mathcal{D}
is promoted to
an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
.

Let
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
be the full subcategory
of
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
spanned by compact objects.
This subcategory is the smallest stable subcategory which
contains
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
(regarded as a right module)
and is closed under retracts.
Hence
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
inherits an associative monoidal structure
from the structure on
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
. We denote by
\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
the resulting associative
monoidal small
R
-linear stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-category which we regard as an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})
.

Proposition 7.17
.

We continue to assume
that
\mathcal{C}
is a small
R
-linear stable idempotent-complete

\infty
-category.
If we think of
\mathcal{D}=\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})

as the left
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}^{\otimes}
-module

(
as above
)
,
the restriction exhibits

\mathcal{C}
as a left
\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
-module,
that is, an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})
.
In particular,
\mathcal{C}
is promoted to
an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})
.

Proof.

We may and will suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is the full subcategory of compact objects
in
\mathcal{D}
.
The tensor product functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\times\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}

sends

\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\times\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
to
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\subset\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
.
It will suffice to prove that
the action functor

\displaystyle m\colon\ \operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\times\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}

sends
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\times\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be the full subcategory of
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}

spanned by those objects
P
such that the essential
image of
\{P\}\times\mathcal{C}

is contained in
\mathcal{C}
.
Note that
m
preserves the shift functors (
\Sigma
or
\Omega
)
and small colimits separately in each variable.
Moreover, the stable subcategory
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathcal{D}
is closed
under retracts.
Thus, we see that
\mathcal{P}
is a stable subcategory which is closed under
retracts.
Since
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is a unit object,

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
lies in
\mathcal{P}
.
Keep in mind that
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}
is
the smallest stable subcategory which
contains
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

and is closed under retracts.
It follows that
\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}\subset\mathcal{P}
.
∎

Construction 7.18
.

Take
{\mathcal{O}}
to be
\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}
in Proposition
6.15
.
By definition,
\operatorname{LMod}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))

is
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))
, and
\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)=\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
We then have

\textstyle{\operatorname{LMod}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big),}

where the vertical arrows are given by the restriction along

\operatorname{As}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{LM}}
.
By Proposition
7.17
,
we think of
\mathcal{C}
as an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{\operatorname{Perf}_{R}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}))
.
Applying the above functor to
\mathcal{C}
, we obtain

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
which belongs to
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})})}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
The lower horizontal arrow carries the associative monoidal
\infty
-category

\operatorname{RPerf}^{\otimes}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})}

to an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
That is,

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})})
is
an associative algebra object
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
.
Consequently, the Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is a left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})})
-module object in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)
.
Next, we set
A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
By the invariance of Hochschild homology under Morita equivalences
(cf. Lemma
6.11
),

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RPerf}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})})
in

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
Let

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{!}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{DCyl}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)

be a sequence of functors such that the first one is induced by left Kan extensions along
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{DCyl}

(cf. the discussion before Proposition
4.19
),
the second one is the restriction along
\mathbf{Cyl}\to\mathbf{DCyl}
,
and the third functor (equivalence) comes from Corollary
4.18
.
By definition, the image of
A
in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)

is
i_{!}(A)_{C}
defined in the discussion before Proposition
7.11
. According to Proposition
7.11
,
we have the canonical equivalence
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq i_{!}(A)_{C}

in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)
.
Therefore,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})=A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

and the left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
-module
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

together with
i_{!}(A)_{C}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
determines
an object of

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))}\operatorname{LMod}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}),

where the equivalence comes from
the canonical equivalences in Corollary
4.21
.
In other words,
it defines an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}^{\mathbf{D}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\subset\operatorname{Alg}_{\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
which induces a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra via the restriction.
Thus, we obtain the desired object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.

8
The action

In this section, we study the maps induced by the action of Hochschild
cohomology spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
on
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
, constructed in Theorem
7.14
.

8.1.
Let
R
be a commutative ring spectrum.
We let
\mathcal{C}
be a small stable
R
-linear
\infty
-category.
We let
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
and
suppose that
\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
.
In other words, we assume that
\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
admits a single compact
generator.
In this setting, we can describe morphisms induced by module actions
by means of concrete algebraic constructions.
For ease of notation, we write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)

for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{RPerf}_{A})=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A})
.
We can safely confuse
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A})

with the Hochschild homology
R
-module spectrum
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
of
A

because of the invariance under Morita equivalences.
We do not distinguish between the notation
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A})
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
: we write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A})
as well.
Write
A^{e}
for
A^{\rm op}\otimes_{R}A
.
As before, by
\otimes
we mean the tensor product over
R

when we treat the tensor products of objects in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
or
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.

We define a morphism
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)

which we refer to as the contraction morphism:

Definition 8.1
.

Consider the functor

\displaystyle(-)\otimes_{A^{e}}(-)\colon\ \operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}\times\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{e}}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

which is informally given by the two-sided
bar construction
(P,Q)\mapsto P\otimes_{A^{e}}Q
.
Note that
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
is left-tensored over
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
.
If we regard
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
as an object of
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
,
there is a morphism
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A
in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
,
which exhibits
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
as a morphism object from
A
to itself
in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}

(i.e., hom
R
-module),
see Corollary
8.6
.
Let
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A)\otimes_{A^{e}}A\to A\otimes_{A^{e}}A

be the morphism induced by the morphism
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A
in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
.
We identify
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
with
A\otimes_{A^{e}}A
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
,
see Lemma
8.7
. It gives rise to

\displaystyle\sigma\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes(A\otimes_{A^{e}}A)\to A\otimes_{A^{e}}A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A).

We shall refer to it as the contraction morphism.

We denote by
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))

the pair endowed with the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra structure
constructed in Theorem
7.14
: we will think
that the pair is promoted to an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Let
D
and
C_{M}
be colors in the colored operad
KS
.
There is a class of an active morphism

f_{j}\colon(\langle 2\rangle,D,C_{M})\!\to\!(\langle 1\rangle,C_{M})
in
\mathbf{KS}

lying over the active morphism

\rho\colon\langle 2\rangle\!\to\!\langle 1\rangle
(with
\rho^{-1}(\ast)\!=\!\ast
).
Such a morphism
f_{j}
is unique up to equivalences.
This is induced by an open embedding

j\colon(0,1)^{2}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
such that

j_{1}\colon(0,1)^{2}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
is rectilinear
and
j_{2}\colon(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
is a shrinking embedding,
cf. Definition
4.1
.
If
h\colon\mathbf{KS}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
denotes a map of
\infty
-operads that encodes

(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
,
passing to
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
via a coCartesian natural
transformation, the image of
f_{j}
induces a morphism in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
:

\displaystyle u\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\simeq h(D)\otimes h(C_{M})\to h(C_{M})\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}).

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
A
be an associative algebra in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
and let
\mathcal{C}
be
\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
.
Then
u\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})

is equivalent to the contraction morphism
\sigma\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
as a morphism in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.

Example 8.3
.

If
\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
has a Calabi–Yau structure of dimension
d
,
then there is a morphism
w\colon\Sigma^{d}R\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

(which we can think of as an analogue of a global section of a volume form)
such that the composite
\Sigma^{d}\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\simeq\Sigma^{d}R\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle w\otimes\textup{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
is an equivalence. Here
\Sigma
indicates the suspension.
It follows from Theorem
8.2

that
\Sigma^{d}\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
induced by
w

and
u
instead of
\sigma
also gives an equivalence.

Construction 8.4
.

We set
\mathcal{C}=\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}
.
Consider a morphism

\displaystyle e\colon\ \operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})

in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}

which carries
R
to the identity functor, cf. Lemma
5.1
.
Applying the adjunction

\displaystyle I\colon\ \operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\rightleftarrows\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\ {\colon}\!E

(see Section
5
) to the morphism
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})
,
we have the morphisms in
\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A}\simeq\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{A}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^\bullet(A)\otimes A}}{}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A},

where the right arrow is the canonical morphism,
and the middle arrow is induced by the counit map
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A})}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})
of the adjunction.
Here,
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}
is endowed with
p_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}
which carries
R
to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
.
The morphisms from
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
are omitted from the notation.
Recall that
I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\rightarrow\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}
that
sends
A
to
p_{A}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
with
p_{A}(R)=A
is fully faithful
so that the full subcategory of
\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}

spanned by objects of the form
p_{A}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}
is equivalent to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
Thus, the composite
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}

in
\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}
gives rise to a morphism of associative algebras

\displaystyle\alpha\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A,

that is, a morphism in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
).
Since

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e\otimes\textup{id}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{A}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}

is naturally
equivalent to
the identity functor, we have
a homotopy from the composite
A\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}A
to the identity morphism of
A
, where
A\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A

is induced by the morphism from
the unit algebra
R\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
.

We can make the following observation:

Lemma 8.5
.

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
is
a center of
A
. See
[
25
, Section 5.3.1]
or the proof below for centers and centralizers.

Proof.

The statement of this lemma is slightly imprecise.
Given
B\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
,
we define
c(B,A)
to be the fiber product

\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A)\times_{\operatorname{Map}(A,A)}\{\textup{id}_{A}\}
, where
\operatorname{Map}(-,-)
means the mapping space in

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
, and
\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A)\to\operatorname{Map}(A,A)

is induced by the composition with
R\otimes_{R}A\to B\otimes_{R}A
.
The assignment
B\mapsto c(B,A)
induces a functor

c(-,A)\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\mathcal{S}
.
A center of
A
is
Z\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
that
represents
c(-,A)
, that is, a centralizer of the identity morphism

A\to A
(cf.
[
25
]
).
Through the equivalence
\operatorname{Map}(Z,Z)\simeq c(Z,A)
,
the identity of
Z
determines

Z\otimes A\to A
and a homotopy/equivalence from the composite

A\to Z\otimes A\to A
to
\textup{id}_{A}
.
Our lemma claims that
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
together with
\alpha\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A
and the homotopy induces an equivalence in
\mathcal{S}
:

\displaystyle\theta_{B}\colon\ \operatorname{Map}(B,\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))\to\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A)\times_{\operatorname{Map}(A,A)}\{\textup{id}_{A}\}

for any
B\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
. Here,

\operatorname{Map}(B,\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))\to\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A)
is the functor
which sends

f\colon B\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
to the composite
B\otimes A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f\otimes\textup{id}_{A}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}A
.

We will prove our claim. Namely, we show that
\theta_{B}
is an equivalence.
For ease of notation, we set
p_{B}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{B}=\mathcal{M}_{B}
and
p_{A}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A}=\mathcal{M}_{A}
.
Let
X=\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{A}^{\simeq}}\{A\}
, where
A
is the right
A
-module determined by the multiplication of
A
, and

\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R},\mathcal{M}_{A})\simeq\mathcal{M}_{A}^{\simeq}
is induced by the composition
with
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}=\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{B}\otimes p_{A}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{M}_{B}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M}_{A}
.
Similarly, we define
Y=\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{A}^{\simeq}}\{A\}
.
Note that

\displaystyle X\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}}(\operatorname{RMod}_{B}\otimes_{R}\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A).

Similarly,
Y\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})\simeq\operatorname{Map}(A,A)
.
The morphism
\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})

given by the composition with
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\mathcal{M}_{B}
determines
X\to Y
.
Let
\Delta^{0}\to Y
be the map determined by the identity functor
\mathcal{M}_{A}

and the identity morphism of
A
.
We have a canonical equivalence

\displaystyle X\times_{Y}\Delta^{0}\simeq\operatorname{Map}(B\otimes A,A)\times_{\operatorname{Map}(A,A)}\{\textup{id}_{A}\}=c(B,A).

By the universal property of
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B},\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A}))\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A}).

Using this equivalence, we deduce that

\displaystyle X\times_{Y}\Delta^{0}\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B},\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A}))\times_{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})^{\simeq}}\{\textup{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{A}}\}.

Moreover, taking into account the universal property of
\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})
in
\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}

(we omit
p_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}

and
e\colon\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A})
from the notation),
we see that
the composition gives rise to an equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B},\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)})\times_{\operatorname{RMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)}^{\simeq}}\{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\}

\displaystyle\qquad{}\simeq\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}}(\mathcal{M}_{B},\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A}))\times_{\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{M}_{A},\mathcal{M}_{A}))^{\simeq}}\{\textup{id}_{\mathcal{M}_{A}}\},

where the left hand side is naturally equivalent to
\operatorname{Map}(B,\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))
.
Unwinding the construction, we have the desired equivalence
\theta_{B}\colon\operatorname{Map}(B,\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))\simeq c(B,A)
.
∎

Corollary 8.6
.

Let us regard
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A
as a right
A^{e}
-module induced by that of
A

(
that is,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes(-)
means the tensor product with
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
and regard

\alpha\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A
as a morphism of right

A^{e}
-moudles in the natural way.
Then the morphism
\alpha\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A

exhibits
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
as a morphism object from
A
to itself in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.5
,

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
(endowed with
\alpha
and an identification
\sigma

between

A\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)

\otimes A\to A
and the identity morphism) is a center
of
A
.
According to
[
25
, Theorem 5.3.1.30]
, the morphism

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes A\to A
of the right
A^{e}
modules, that is obtained from
the center, exhibits
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
as a morphism object from
A
to
A
.
Thus, our claim follows.
∎

We describe the bar construction
P\otimes_{A^{e}}A

by means of symmetric spectra.
Let
\mathbb{R}
be a cofibrant commutative symmetric ring spectrum.
Let
\mathbb{A}
be a cofibrant associative symmetric ring
\mathbb{R}
-module
spectrum
which represents
A
, cf. Example
6.4
.
We write
\wedge
for the wedge/tensor product
\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}
over
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
be a simplicial diagram

\Delta^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}

of symmetric spectra (called the bar construction),
which is given by
[p]\mapsto\mathbb{A}\wedge\dots\wedge\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\wedge\mathbb{A}
.
We refer to
[
31
, Definition 4.1.8]
for the explicit formula of
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
.
The degeneracy maps
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+2}\to\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+1}

is induced by the multiplication of
\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}
,
and face maps
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+2}\to\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p+3}

is induced by the unit map
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{A}
.
Each term
\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\wedge\mathbb{A}
is a free left
\mathbb{A}^{e}:=\mathbb{A}^{\rm op}\wedge\mathbb{A}
-module generated by
\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}=\mathbb{R}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\wedge\mathbb{R}
.
In addition,
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
can be thought of as a simplicial
diagram of left
A^{e}
-modules.
The homotopy colimit of
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
is naturally equivalent
to
\mathbb{A}
with respect to stable equivalences
[
31
, Lemma 4.1.9]

so that the colimit of the induced diagram in
\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{e}}
is
A
.
Let
\mathbb{P}
be a right
\mathbb{A}^{e}
-module which is cofibrant as an
\mathbb{R}
-module.
Let
\mathbb{P}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})

be a simplicial diagram induced by
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})

which carries
[p]

to
\mathbb{P}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}(\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\wedge\mathbb{A})\simeq\mathbb{P}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}
.
Consider the composition

\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\left(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle B(-)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Cat}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\rm pc}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}(-)_{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)\to\operatorname{Fun}\left(\Delta^{\rm op},\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)
, where the final morphism is determined by the restriction
\Delta^{\rm op}\subset\Lambda^{\rm op}
.
We write
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(-)
for the composite.
Note that
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})
gives rise to a simplicial
diagram in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
whose colimit is
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
.
The standard computation shows that

\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
can be identified with
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})
.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
be a right
\mathbb{A}^{e}
-module symmetric spectrum which is cofibrant as an
\mathbb{R}
-module.
We write
P
for the image of
\mathbb{P}
in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
.
Then
P\otimes_{A^{e}}A
can be identified with
a colimit of the simplicial
diagram induced by
\mathbb{P}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
.
In particular,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
can be identified with
A\otimes_{A^{e}}A
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.

Proof.

Note that the two-sided bar construction preserves colimits in each variable.
Moreover, the colimit of
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})

is
A
after passing to
\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{e}}
,
and each
\mathbb{P}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}\big(\mathbb{A}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}\wedge\mathbb{A}\big)\simeq\mathbb{P}\wedge\mathbb{A}^{\wedge p}
computes

P\otimes A^{\otimes p}\simeq P\otimes_{A^{e}}\big(A\otimes A^{\otimes p}\otimes A\big)
.
Therefore, lemma follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.2
.

We use the notation in the discussion about the setting of Theorem
8.2
.
We first consider an operadic left Kan extension

h^{\prime}\colon\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
of
h\colon\mathbf{KS}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
over
\Gamma

along
\mathbf{KS}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
, cf. Proposition
4.20
.
The open embedding
j\colon(0,1)^{2}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{1}\sqcup j_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(0,1)\times S^{1}

factors as the composition of two open embeddings

(0,1)^{2}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d\sqcup\textup{id}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(0,1)\times S^{1}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r\sqcup j_{2}}}{{\to}}(0,1)\times S^{1}
.
See Definition
4.3
for the notation and convention.
The embedding
d\colon(0,1)^{2}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

and
r\colon(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

are recti-linear embeddings such that the composite
r\circ d
is
j_{1}

(of course, the image of
r
does not intersect with that of the shrinking embedding
j_{2}
). Thus,
f_{j}
factors as
(\langle 2\rangle,D,C_{M})\to(\langle 2\rangle,C,C_{M})\to(\langle 1\rangle,C_{M})
in
\overline{\mathbf{DCyl}}
, that lie over
\langle 2\rangle=\langle 2\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\to}}\langle 1\rangle
.
It follows that
h(D)\otimes h(C_{M})\to h(C_{M})
induced by
f_{j}

factors as

\displaystyle h(D)\otimes h(C_{M})\simeq h^{\prime}(D)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C_{M})\simeq h(C_{M}),

where
h^{\prime}(D)\to h^{\prime}(C)
and
h^{\prime}(C)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C_{M})

are induced by
d
and
r\sqcup j_{2}
, respectively.

Next, we describe
h^{\prime}(D)\to h^{\prime}(C)
in an explicit way.
If
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
is encoded by the map of an
\infty
-operads

\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
,
we let

i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))\colon\mathbf{DCyl}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
be its operadic left Kan extension
along
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{DCyl}
.
Let
i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))((\langle 1\rangle,D))\to i_{!}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))((\langle 1\rangle,C))

be a morphism in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
, that is
determined by
d
. For simplicity,
we set
Z:=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)
and we write
i_{!}(Z)(D)\to i_{!}(Z)(C)
for
this morphism.
If we think of
Z
as the underlying
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-algebra
given by the composite
\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
,
we denote by
l_{!}(Z)\colon\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
its operadic left Kan extension along

l\colon\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Mfld}_{1}
.
A rectilinear embedding
(0,1)\to S^{1}
gives rise to

l_{!}(Z)((0,1))\to l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)
in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
(we abuse notation as above).
More explicitly,
l_{!}(Z)((0,1))\to l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)

is
Z=\tilde{Z}((0,1))\to\operatorname{colim}_{U\to S^{1}\in(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\tilde{Z}(U)
,
where
Z
means the underlying
R
-module,
and
\tilde{Z}\colon\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
is the underlying functor of the
symmetric monoidal functor
\tilde{Z}\colon\mathsf{Disk}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}

corresponding to the composite

\mathbf{E}_{1}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
.
Since
the forgetful functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

preserves colimits over the sifted category
(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}
,
it follows from Lemma
7.13

that

i_{!}(Z)(D)\to i_{!}(Z)(C)
is naturally equivalent to

Z=l_{!}(Z)((0,1))\to l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)=\operatorname{colim}_{(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\tilde{Z}(U)

as a morphism in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Therefore, we may identify
h^{\prime}(D)\to h^{\prime}(C)
with
Z=l_{!}(Z)((0,1))\to l_{!}(Z)(S^{1})=\operatorname{colim}_{(\mathsf{Disk}_{1})_{/S^{1}}}\tilde{Z}(U)
.
Henceforth,
we regard
Z
as an associative algebra in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
,
which is given by a symmetric monoidal functor
\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Mod}^{\otimes}_{R}
.
The map
\xi\colon\Lambda^{\rm op}\to\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes}
(see the discussion
proceeding to Lemma
7.12
)
and
\Delta^{\rm op}\hookrightarrow\Lambda^{\rm op}_{\infty}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}

induces

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Fun}^{\otimes}\big(\widetilde{\operatorname{As}}^{\otimes},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_R)}{}\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\to\operatorname{Fun}\big(\Delta^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big).

It follows from Lemma
7.6

that
the image of
Z
in
\operatorname{Fun}(\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op},\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

is the composite
\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\simeq\big(\mathsf{Disk}^{\dagger}_{1}\big)_{/S^{1}}\to\mathsf{Disk}_{1}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

whose colimit is naturally equivalent to
l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)
.
Moreover, from the cofinality of
\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda^{\rm op}_{\infty}
,

l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)

is naturally equivalent to the
colimit of
c\colon\Delta^{\rm op}\to\Lambda_{\infty}^{\rm op}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
.
Taking into account Remark
7.7
,
Z\to l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)
can be identified with

Z=c([0])\to\operatorname{colim}_{[p]\in\Delta^{\rm op}}c([p])
.

Next let us consider
h^{\prime}(C)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C_{M})
.
According to Construction
7.18
,
its underlying morphism in
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}

can naturally be
identified with

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z\otimes A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)

induced by
\alpha\colon Z\otimes A\to A
.
Let
\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{A}
be cofibrant and fibrant associative ring symmetric
\mathbb{R}
-module
spectra that represent
Z
and
A
, respectively
(namely, they are objects in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\!\big(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}\big(\mathbb{R}\big)\big)

which are both cofibrant and fibrant with respect to
the projective model structure).
Let
\bar{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}
be a morphism in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\!\left(\operatorname{Sp}^{\Sigma}(\mathbb{R})^{c}\right)

which represents
\alpha
.
The composite
\mathbb{A}\simeq\mathbb{R}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}

induced by
\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{Z}
is equivalent to the identity morphism of
\mathbb{A}
.
Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z})\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})
denote
the bisimplicial diagram induced by the wedge product
and
\big(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z})\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})\big)^{\rm diag}

the associated diagonal simplicial diagram.
The morphism
\bar{\alpha}
induces the following morphism of simplicial diagrams

\displaystyle\big(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z})\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})\big)^{\rm diag}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})

whose colimit is equivalent to

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z\otimes A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)

(notice that
\Delta^{\rm op}
is sifted).

Note that

l_{!}(Z)\big(S^{1}\big)=\operatorname{colim}_{[p]\in\Delta^{\rm op}}c([p])\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z)
.
If
\mathbb{Z}_{cst}
denotes the constant simplicial diagram
taking the value
\mathbb{Z}
,

\{[0]\}\to\Delta^{\rm op}
induces
\mathbb{Z}_{cst}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z})

whose colimit is
Z=c([0])\to\operatorname{colim}_{[p]\in\Delta^{\rm op}}c([p])\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z)
.
Consider
\mathbb{Z}_{cst}\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})\to\big(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{Z})\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})\big)^{\rm diag}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})
.
Their colimits give
Z\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(Z)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
,
which is equivalent to
h^{\prime}(D)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C)\otimes h^{\prime}(C_{M})\to h^{\prime}(C_{M})
.
Observe that the composition
\mathbb{Z}_{cst}\wedge\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})=(\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A})\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})\to\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{A})

is induced by
\bar{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A}\to\mathbb{A}
.
The contraction morphism

(Z\otimes A)\otimes_{A^{e}}A\to A\otimes_{A^{e}}A

is obtained from

\mathbb{Z}\wedge(\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A}))=(\mathbb{Z}\wedge\mathbb{A})\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})\to\mathbb{A}\wedge_{\mathbb{A}^{e}}B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
by taking colimits. This completes the proof.
∎

8.2.
Let
k
be a field.
We suppose that
R
is the Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum of
k
.
We write
k
for
R
.
In this context, we will give a concrete model of the contraction morphism

\displaystyle\sigma\colon\ \operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)

as a morphism of chain complexes of
k
-vector spaces.
Let
\operatorname{Comp}^{\otimes}(k)
be the symmetric monoidal
category of chain complexes of
k
-vector
spaces, whose tensor product is given by the standard tensor
product of chain complexes.
There is a symmetric monoidal (projective) model structure
on
\operatorname{Comp}(k)

such that a morphism is a weak equivalence (resp. a fibration)
if it is a quasi-isomorphism (resp. a termwise surjective),
see, e.g.,
[
25
, Proposition 7.1.2.11]
.
Since
k
is a field, every object is
both cofibrant and fibrant.
Let
\operatorname{Comp}(k)\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}
be the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
obtained by inverting quasi-isomorphisms. We fix a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\operatorname{Mod}_{k}^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Comp}(k)\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}
,
see
[
25
, Theorem 7.1.2.13]
.
Thus it gives rise to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Comp}(k)\big[W^{-1}\big]\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})
.
We here regard
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))
as (the nerve of) the category of differential graded
k
-algebras in an obvious way.
Let
\mathsf{A}
be an associative (cofibrant) differential graded

k
-algebra, i.e., an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))

that represents
A\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})
.
Let
\mathsf{A}^{e}:=\mathsf{A}^{\rm op}\otimes\mathsf{A}
.
The natural functor
\operatorname{Comp}(k)^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Comp}(k)\big[W^{-1}\big]^{\otimes}\simeq\operatorname{Mod}_{k}^{\otimes}
induces

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))\times\operatorname{LMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))\to\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})\times\operatorname{LMod}_{A^{e}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})\to\operatorname{Mod}_{k},

where the right functor is informally given by the bar construction
(i.e., the relative tensor product)
(-)\otimes_{A^{e}}(-)
.
We describe the contraction morphism by using explicit resolutions.
Let
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}):=B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{A})

be the right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-module associated to
the total complex of the simplicial diagram of
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-modules
[p]\mapsto\mathsf{A}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{\otimes p}\otimes\mathsf{A}
(defined as in
B_{\bullet}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})
).
The associated total complex
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
computes a homotopy
colimit of the simplicial
diagram, and there is a canonical morphism of right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-modules

B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\to\mathsf{A}
which is a quasi-isomorphism.
Let
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)

be the hom chain complex of right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-modules.
By Lemma
8.9
below,

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)
represents/computes the (derived) hom complex from

\mathsf{A}
to
\mathsf{A}
in
\operatorname{RMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))
.
Let us consider an evaluation morphism of right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-modules

\displaystyle\textup{Ev}\colon\ \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}))\otimes B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\to B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})

defined in the obvious way,
where
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\otimes B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
comes equipped with the right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-module structure
induced by
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
.
Let
\operatorname{RMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))\big[W^{-1}\big]
denote the
\infty
-category

obtained by inverting quasi-isomorphisms (after restricting to
cofibrant objects).
By the universal property of the morphism
\big(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big),\textup{Ev}\big)

and the equivalence

\displaystyle\operatorname{RMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))\big[W^{-1}\big]\simeq\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})

[
25
, Theorem 4.3.3.17]
together with Lemma
8.9
,
it represents a morphism object from
A
to
A

in
\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
. Note that

B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}
is the chain complex associated to the simplicial chain complex given by
[p]\mapsto\big(\mathsf{A}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{\otimes p}\otimes\mathsf{A}\big)\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}\simeq\mathsf{A}^{\otimes p}\otimes\mathsf{A}
so that the associated complex
is a model of
A\otimes_{A^{e}}A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
. That is,
its image in
\operatorname{Mod}_{k}
is naturally equivalent
to
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathsf{A}\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
.
By the Morita theory (cf., e.g.,
[
5
, Section 4]
),

\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{k}(\operatorname{RMod}_{A},\operatorname{RMod}_{A})\simeq\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
,
where the identity functor amounts to
A\in\operatorname{RMod}_{A^{e}}
having the diagonal
module structure. Consequently,

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\in\operatorname{Comp}(k)

is a model of
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\in\operatorname{Mod}_{k}
.
Moreover,
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\to\mathsf{A}
induces
a quasi-isomorphism

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),\mathsf{A}\big)

(perhaps, the latter is more common Hochschild cochain complex).
We conclude:

Proposition 8.8
.

The above evaluation morphism induces

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}),B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\otimes B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}\to B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A},

which is equivalent to the contraction morphism

\sigma\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
.
In particular, it is one of (explicit) models of
u

(
cf. Theorem
8.2
)
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Let us consider
\operatorname{RMod}_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k))

to be a category endowed with
\operatorname{Comp}(k)
-enriched projective
model structure, where a morphism is a weak equivalence

(
resp. a fibration
)
if it is a quasi-isomorphism
(
resp. a termwise surjective
)
, see, e.g.,
[
3
, Theorem 3.3]
.
Then
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
is cofibrant
with respect to this model structure.

Proof.

Since
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
is obtained from the simplicial
chain complex
[p]\mapsto\mathsf{A}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{\otimes p}\otimes\mathsf{A}
,
there is an increasing filtration of
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-submodules

0=F_{-1}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\hookrightarrow F_{0}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\hookrightarrow F_{1}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)\hookrightarrow\cdots

such that
\cup_{p\geq 0}F_{p}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)=B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})
.
The quotient
F_{p+1}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)/F_{p}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)

is isomorphic to
\mathsf{A}\otimes\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\otimes p+1}\otimes\mathsf{A}

as a right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-module, where
\overline{\mathsf{A}}
is the cokernel of a unit morphism
k\to\mathsf{A}
. That is,

F_{p+1}(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A}))/F_{p}\big(B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})\big)

is a free right
\mathsf{A}^{e}
-module generated by
\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\otimes p+1}\in\operatorname{Comp}(k)
.
By
[
3
, Definition 9.17, Theorem 9.20]
, the existence of this
filtration implies that
B^{\rm dg}_{\bullet}(\mathsf{A})

is cofibrant with respect to the
r
-model structure in
[
3
, Section 4]
.
We deduce from the assumption that
k
is a field
that the projective model structure coincides with this
r
-model
structure (the projective model structure is the same as the
q
-model
structure in
[
3
, Section 3]
).
This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.10
.

There are other operations between

\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
,
which is induced by the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra structure
on
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A))
.
We continue to work with the coefficient field
R=k
.
We further assume that
k
is of characteristic zero.
Observe first that the Kan complex

\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})

is equivalent to the product of the circles
S^{1}\times S^{1}

in
\mathcal{S}
, where
S^{1}\times S^{1}
is regarded as an object of
\mathcal{S}
.
The left factor
S^{1}
is homotopy equivalent to
the space of configuration of one point on
(0,1)\times S^{1}
,
which we regard as the space of rectilinear embeddings
from
(0,1)^{2}
into
(0,1)\times S^{1}
.
The right factor
S^{1}
can be identified with
the mapping space from
C_{M}
to itself, that is,
the space of shrinking embeddings
(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}
.
The map
of
\infty
-operads
h\colon\mathbf{KS}\to\operatorname{Mod}_{k}^{\otimes}
encoding
the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A))

in Theorem
7.14

induces morphisms
h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}\colon\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{k}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A))

and
h_{(\{C_{M}\},C_{M})}\colon\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{C_{M}\},C_{M})\to\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{Mod}_{k}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A))
in
\mathcal{S}
.
To discuss operations induced by these morphisms,
we adopt the differential graded setting (we simplify the problem).
By taking the singular chain complex of the simplicial sets

\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(-,-)
with coefficients in
k
,
we obtain the differential graded (dg)
operad
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
from
KS
,
i.e., an operad in the symmetric monoidal category
\operatorname{Comp}(k)
.
We refer to
[
15
]
for the relation between
dg operads and
\infty
-operads.
By a rectification result of Hinich
[
15
, Theorem 4.1.1]
,
there is a canonical equivalence between
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k})

and the
\infty
-category
of
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
-algebras in
\operatorname{Comp}(k)
in the “conventional” sense
so that the

\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A))
gives rise to
a (an essentially unique)
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
-algebra
in
\operatorname{Comp}(k)

in the “conventional” sense: see
[
15
, Section 2.2.4]
(we here use
the assumption of characteristic zero).
We denote this
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
-algebra by
(HH^{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A))
.
Then
the
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
-algebra
(HH^{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A))

induces morphisms into hom chain complexes:

\displaystyle h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}\colon\ C_{\bullet}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH^{\bullet}(A)\otimes HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A))

and

\displaystyle h_{(\{C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}\colon\ C_{\bullet}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{C_{M}\},C_{M}))\to\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A))

in
\operatorname{Comp}(k)\big[W^{-1}\big]
.
Given a simplicial set
S
,
we write
H_{*}(S)
for the homology
H_{*}(C_{\bullet}(S))
of the singular chain complex
C_{\bullet}(S)
of
S

with coefficients in
k
.
Passing to homology, we obtain a morphism of graded
k
-vector spaces

\displaystyle H_{*}\big(h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}\big)\colon\ H_{*}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))

\displaystyle\hphantom{H_*\big(h_{(\lx@text@lbrace D,C_M\lx@text@rbrace,C_M)}^{\rm dg}\big)\colon}{}\ \to H_{*}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH^{\bullet}(A)\otimes HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A)))

from
h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}
.
Note that by definition the left-hand side is connective.
This map can also be obtained
by taking homology of
C_{\bullet}(h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})})
up to isomorphisms.
Since the mapping space
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})
is homotopy equivalent to
S^{1}\times S^{1}
,
it follows that

\displaystyle H_{0}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))\simeq k,

\displaystyle H_{1}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))\simeq k\oplus k,

\displaystyle H_{2}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))\simeq k,

and the other parts are zero.

Let
[0,1]\subset\mathbf{R}
be the closed interval and let

\phi\colon[0,1]\times S^{1}=[0,1]\times\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}\to\mathbf{R}/\mathbf{Z}=S^{1}

be the continuous map given by
(t,x\ \textup{mod}\ \mathbf{Z})\mapsto x+t\ \textup{mod}\ \mathbf{Z}
. We think of
\phi
as a homotopy from the identity map
S^{1}\to S^{1}

to itself. The product
\operatorname{id}_{(0,1)}\times\phi

determines a homotopy
\overline{\phi}\colon[0,1]\times(0,1)\times S^{1}\to(0,1)\times S^{1}

from the identity map of
(0,1)\times S^{1}
to itself.
Let
r_{1}:[0,1]\times(0,1)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{id}\times j_{1}}}{{\to}}[0,1]\times(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\overline{\phi}}}{{\to}}(0,1)\times S^{1}

be the homotopy from
j_{1}
to
j_{1}
, see discussion before Theorem
8.2
for the maps
j
,
j_{1}
, and
j_{2}
.
Let
r_{2}\colon[0,1]\times(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textup{pr}}}{{\to}}(0,1)\times S^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{2}}}{{\to}}(0,1)\times S^{1}
be the trivial
homotopy from
j_{2}
and
j_{2}
.
Define

\displaystyle l:=r_{1}\sqcup r_{2}\colon\ [0,1]\times\big((0,1)^{2}\sqcup(0,1)\times S^{1}\big)\to(0,1)\times S^{1}.

We think of
l
as a homotopy from
j
to
j
.
If we regard
\overline{\phi}
as a homotopy from the identity to itself
in
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{C_{M}\},C_{M})
,
it determines an element
e_{B}
of
H_{1}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{C_{M}\},C_{M}))
.
If we think of
l
as a homotopy from
f_{j}
to itself
in
\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})
,
it determines an element
e_{L}
of
H_{1}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))
.
The
C_{\bullet}(\textup{KS})
-algebra
(HH^{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A))

(or equivalently the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A))

in Theorem
7.14
) defines
L\in H_{1}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH^{\bullet}(A)\otimes_{k}HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A)))
for
e_{L}
:

L
is defined to be the image of
e_{L}
under
H_{*}\big(h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}\big)
.
Similarly,
e_{B}
determines an element

B
in
H_{1}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A)))
.

The morphism
f_{j}
arising from

j=j_{1}\sqcup j_{2}

is a generator of the
0
-th homology group.
The image of
f_{j}
under
h_{(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M})}^{\rm dg}

can be identified with
u\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\otimes\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\to\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
which is equivalent to the contraction morphism, cf. Theorem
8.2
.
By a comparison result of Hoyois
[
18
, Theorem 2.3]
,

B
in
H_{1}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH^{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A)))

may be viewed as the Connes operator:
if
\mathsf{A}
is an associative
(cofibrant) differential graded algebra which represents
A

through
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{k}^{\otimes})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Comp}(k)[W^{-1}])
,
the classical Connes operator

b\colon B_{\bullet}^{\rm dg}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}\to B_{\bullet}^{\rm dg}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}
(see, e.g.,
[
18
,
24
]
)
is equivalent to
B
, up to the multiplication by
\pm 1
,
through
B_{\bullet}^{\rm dg}(\mathsf{A})\otimes_{\mathsf{A}^{e}}\mathsf{A}\simeq\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq HH_{\bullet}(A)
.

The homotopy
l
and the composition

\overline{\phi}\circ(\textup{id}_{[0,1]}{\times}j)\colon[0,1]{\times}\big((0,1)^{2}\sqcup(0,1){\times}S^{1}\big)\to(0,1){\times}S^{1}
generate the
k
-vector space

k\oplus k\simeq H_{1}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))
.
Let
\mathbf{1}
denote the element of
H_{0}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)))
that corresponds to the identity morphism.
By relations of homotopies, we see that

\displaystyle L=B\circ u+u\circ(\mathbf{1}\otimes(-B))=B\circ u-u\circ(\mathbf{1}\otimes B)

in
H_{1}(\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(HH^{\bullet}(A)\otimes HH_{\bullet}(A),HH_{\bullet}(A)))
.
Here “
\circ
” indicates the composition. This relation is
known as Cartan homotopy/magic formula.
In the dg setting over
k
, the shifted complex

HH^{\bullet}(A)[1]
inherits the structure of an
L_{\infty}
-algebra,
i.e., an algebra over a (cofibrant) Lie operad in
\operatorname{Comp}(k)
,
from the
\mathbf{E}_{2}
-algebra structure on
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(A)\simeq HH^{\bullet}(A)
.
The morphism

\displaystyle L\colon\ HH^{\bullet}(A)[1]\otimes HH_{\bullet}(A)\to HH_{\bullet}(A)

induced by
L
appears as the Lie algebra
action morphism on
HH_{\bullet}(A)

(see, e.g.,
[
11
]
).
Since
u
and
B
can be explicitly described (
B
is equivalent to Connes’ operator), thus
L
also has an explicit presentation.
Finally,
H_{2}(\operatorname{Mult}_{\textup{KS}}(\{D,C_{M}\},C_{M}))
is generated by
B\circ u\circ(\mathbf{1}\otimes B)
.

9
Equivariant context

Our construction in Theorem
7.14
can easily be
generalized
to an equivariant setting:
Let
G
be a group object in
\mathcal{S}

and
BG\in\mathcal{S}
the classifying space.
Let
\mathcal{C}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
, that is, a small stable
R
-lienar idempotent-complete

\infty
-category.
Suppose that
G
acts on
\mathcal{C}
, i.e., an left action on
\mathcal{C}
.
Namely,
\mathcal{C}
is an object of
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})

whose image under the forgetful functor
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
is
\mathcal{C}
.
In this setting, we have

Theorem 9.1
.

The pair
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
of Hochschild cohomology and homology
R
-module spectra has the structure of a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra in
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
In other words,
(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}))
is promoted to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.

Remark 9.2
.

The forgetful functor
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

sends the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra in Theorem
9.1

to a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra equivalent to the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra constructed in Theorem
7.14
.

Theorem
9.1
follows from the following:

Construction 9.3
.

The construction is almost the same as that of Theorem
7.14
. Thus, we highlight necessary modifications.

(i) Let
\mathcal{D}:=\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
be the Ind-category which is an
R
-linear
compactly generated

\infty
-category. In particular,
\mathcal{D}
belongs to
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
.
Since
\mathcal{C}\mapsto\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C})
is functorial,
the left action of
G
on
\mathcal{C}
induces a left action on
\mathcal{D}
.
Namely,
\mathcal{D}
is promoted to
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
.
The functor category
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)

inherits a (pointwise) symmetric monoidal structure from that
of
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
. Let
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}^{G}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
be
an internal hom object in the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
. This is explicitly described as follows:
The internal hom object

\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
(Lemma
5.1
) in
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}

has the left action of
G^{\rm op}\times G
induced by the functoriality of the internal hom object and the action of
G
on
\mathcal{D}
(here
G^{\rm op}
denotes
the opposite group). The homomorphism
G\to G^{\rm op}\times G
informally given by
g\mapsto\big(g^{-1},g\big)
determines a left action of
G
on
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
. By the universal property of
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
,
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
endowed with the
G
-action is an internal hom object from
\mathcal{D}
to
\mathcal{D}
in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)
. As in Lemma
5.1
,
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}^{G}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
is promoted to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
. Here the equivalence follows from
the definition of the pointwise symmetric monoidal strucutre on

\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

[
25
, Remark 2.1.3.4]
.

(ii) Recall the adjunction
I\colon\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\rightleftarrows\left(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)_{\operatorname{Mod}_{R}/}\,{\colon}\!E
induces an adjunction

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)

(see Section
5
).
Applying
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,-)
to this adjunction
we get an adjunction

\displaystyle\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)\big)\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)\big).

We define the Hochschild cohomology
R
-module spectrum
