\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}):=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})\in\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\simeq\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))

to be the image of
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}^{G}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
under the right adjoint.
For ease of notation, we write
A
for
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
.
Write
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}^{G,\otimes}
for the image of
A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{D})\in\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
under the left adjoint.
Consider counit map
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}^{G,\otimes}\!\to\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}^{G}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})

in
\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\!\big(\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)\big)
which we regard as a
G
-equivariant
associative monoidal functor.
As in the non-equivariant case, the natural action of
\mathcal{M}{\rm or}_{R}^{G}(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D})
on
\mathcal{D}
gives rise to a left action of
\operatorname{RMod}_{A}^{G,\otimes}

on
\mathcal{D}
. More precisely,
\mathcal{D}
is promoted to an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RMod}_{A}^{G,\otimes}}(\operatorname{Fun}\left(BG,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right))
.
Let
\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}
be the symmetric monoidal full subcategory
spanned by compact objects.
By definition,
\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}
is the associative
monoidal small
R
-linear
\infty
-category
\operatorname{RPerf}_{A}^{\otimes}

endowed with the
G
-action given informally by the
G
-action on
A
.
As in Proposition
7.17
,
the restriction exhibits

\mathcal{C}
as a left
\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}
-module, that is,
an object of
\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}))
.

(iii) Let
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(-)\colon\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}\to\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
denote the symmetric monoidal functor
which carries
\mathcal{E}
to
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{E})
(see Definition
6.14
). Applying
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,-)
to it, we have a symmetric monoidal functor

\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})\to\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.
We define
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
to be the image of
\mathcal{C}

in
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.
By the induced functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}))\to\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\left(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\right)}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\big),

we regard
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
as a left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\big(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\big)
-module.
That is to say,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
is an object of

\displaystyle\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\left(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\right)}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BG,\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Mod}_{R}\big)\big)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\simeq\operatorname{LMod}_{\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\left(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\right)}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(BS^{1},\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\big).

According to the Morita invariance,
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\big(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\big)

can naturally be identified with
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
, where
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)
comes equipped with
a left action of
G
induced by the
G
-action on
A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
.
Let
i_{!}(A)\colon\mathbf{DCyl}\to\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})^{\otimes}
denote the operadic left Kan extension of the map
of
\infty
-operads
A\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})^{\otimes}
over
\Gamma

along
i\colon\mathbf{E}_{2}^{\otimes}\to\mathbf{DCyl}
where
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})^{\otimes}\to\Gamma

is the pointwise symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category induced by the structure on
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}^{\otimes}
. Let
i_{!}(A)_{C}
be the restriction to
\mathbf{Cyl}\subset\mathbf{DCyl}
which
we think of as an object of
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{Cyl}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.
If we replace
\operatorname{Mod}_{R}
by
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})

in the proof of Proposition
7.11
,
the argument yields a canonical equivalence
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq i_{!}(A)_{C}
in

\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
.
Then
A=\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
, the left
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}\big(\operatorname{RPerf}^{G,\otimes}_{A}\big)
-module
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C})
, and
\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(A)\simeq i_{!}(A)_{C}

determine an object of

\displaystyle\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{E}_{2}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\!\times_{\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BS^{1},\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})))}\!\operatorname{LMod}\!\big(\operatorname{Fun}\!\big(BS^{1}\!,\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})\big)\big),

which is naturally equivalent to
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))

(see Corollary
4.21
).
Consequently,
we obtain a
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra in
\operatorname{Fun}(BG,\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
having the property
described in Remark
9.2
.

Remark 9.4
.

The argument in Construction
9.3
can be applied to show
other functorialities.
For example, suppose that we are given an equivalence
f\colon\mathcal{C}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathcal{C}_{2}
in
\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
.
Then it gives rise to an equivalence

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{1}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{1}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{2}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{2}))

in
\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
.
To see this, consider the
\infty
-category
I
which consists of two objects
\{1,2\}

such that for any
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, the mapping space
\operatorname{Map}_{I}(i,j)
is a contractible space
(so that
I
is (the nerve of) an ordinary groupoid).
The equivalence
f
amounts to a functor
I\to\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
which carries

1
and
2
to
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, respectively, and carries
the unique morphism
1\to 2

to
f
. Namely, we have an object of
\operatorname{Fun}(I,\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R})
so that
the construction of Ind-categories gives rise to an object
F\in\operatorname{Fun}\left(I,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)
.
Now apply the argument in Construction
9.3
by replacing

BG
with
I
:
Namely, we first
consider the internal hom object from
F
to
F
in
\operatorname{Fun}\left(I,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)
.
Note that by
[
27
, Corollary 3.3.3.2]
and the fact that
I
is a Kan complex (i.e.,
\infty
-groupoid),
\operatorname{Fun}\left(I,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\right)
can be identified with a limit of the constant diagram

I\to\widehat{\textup{Cat}}_{\infty}
with value
\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}
.
There exists an internal hom object from
F
to
F
that is given by the tensor product
F\otimes F^{\vee}
of
F

and its dual object
F^{\vee}
, where
F^{\vee}
is given by taking termwise dual objects.
According to
[
25
, Corollary 4.7.1.40]
,
F\otimes F^{\vee}
is promoted to an endomorphism
algebra
\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}(F)\in\operatorname{Alg}_{\operatorname{As}}\big(\operatorname{Fun}\big(I,\operatorname{Pr}^{\textup{L}}_{R}\big)\big)
equipped with a left action on
F
in an essentially unique way.
The evaluation at each
1,2\in I
gives us
the endomorphism algebras
\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}_{R}(\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{1}))
and
\mathcal{E}{\rm nd}_{R}(\operatorname{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{2}))
, respectively.
Thus, replacing
BG
with
I
in the procedure in Construction
9.3
,
we obtain an object in

\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Fun}(I,\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))\simeq\operatorname{Fun}(I,\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R}))
, which brings us
an induced equivalence

(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{1}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{1}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{2}),\operatorname{\mathcal{HH}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{C}_{2}))
.
Finally, we remark that this argument can be applied to any diagram

K\to\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
such that
K
is a Kan complex (i.e., an
\infty
-groupoid).
That is, if we are given a diagram/functor
P\colon K\to\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
from an
\infty
-groupoid
K
,
it gives rise to a diagram
P_{\mathbf{KS}}\colon K\to\operatorname{Alg}_{\mathbf{KS}}(\operatorname{Mod}_{R})
of
\mathbf{KS}
-algebras
such that for any
k\in K
,
P_{\mathbf{KS}}(k)
is equivalent to the
\mathbf{KS}
-algebra
constructed from
P(k)\in\operatorname{\mathcal{S}t}_{R}
in Theorem
7.14
.
